\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$H\to\mu\mu$ Signal Strength}{H to mumu Signal Strength} (EFT/Wilson-Matched Mapping, UV+LL-RG Baseline)}
We confront the theory with the ATLAS Run-2+Run-3 combined $H\to\mu\mu$ signal-strength input, $\mu_{\mu\mu}^{\rm obs}=1.4\pm0.4$ (combined uncertainty)~\cite{ATLAS_Hmumu}. The same ATLAS evidence result reports a Run-3-only signal strength near $\mu_{\mu\mu}=1.6\pm0.6$; all scan maps in this manuscript use the combined input above.

Within the PSLT closure, we construct a scan-level EFT/Wilson-matched map (evaluated in baseline \texttt{eft\_wilson\_uv\_rge} mode) by combining kinetic occupancy with overlap-extracted raw couplings:
\begin{equation}
\tilde q_N(D,\eta)=g_N(D)\left(1-\ee^{-\Gamma_N(D,\eta)t_{\rm coh}}\right),\qquad
P_N^{\rm(kin)}(D,\eta)=\frac{\tilde q_N(D,\eta)}{\sum_{K=1}^{N_{\max}}\tilde q_K(D,\eta)}.
\label{eq:kin_occupancy}
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}
\epsilon_{\rm mix}(D,\eta)=\mathrm{clip}\!\left[\kappa_{\rm mix}\chi_{\rm eff}(D)\left(\frac{\eta}{\eta_{\rm ref}}\right)^{p_\eta},\,0,\,\epsilon_{\max}\right],
\label{eq:epsilon_hll_match}
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}
\Pi(\epsilon_{\rm mix})=
\begin{pmatrix}
1-\epsilon_{\rm mix} & \epsilon_{\rm mix} & 0\\
\epsilon_{\rm mix}/2 & 1-\epsilon_{\rm mix} & \epsilon_{\rm mix}/2\\
0 & \epsilon_{\rm mix} & 1-\epsilon_{\rm mix}
\end{pmatrix},
\qquad
Y_{iN}(D,\eta)=\sqrt{y_N^{\rm eff,raw}(D)}\,\Pi_{iN}(\epsilon_{\rm mix}),
\label{eq:flavor_projector}
\end{equation}
The bounded projector in Eqs.~\eqref{eq:epsilon_hll_match}--\eqref{eq:flavor_projector} is retained for matched-mode diagnostics; the baseline \texttt{eft\_wilson\_uv\_rge} maps use the UV-tree closure below.
\begin{equation}
C_{eH}^{\rm tree,\,ij}(D,\eta)=\sum_{N=1}^{3}g_{iN}(D)\,
\frac{P_N^{\rm(kin)}(D,\eta)}{M_N^2(D)}\,g_{jN}(D),
\label{eq:wilson_tree_uv}
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}
C_{eH}^{\rm match,\,ij}=
\begin{cases}
\left(1+\dfrac{\kappa_{\rm diag}}{16\pi^2}\right) C_{eH}^{\rm tree,\,ij}, & i=j,\\[4pt]
\left(1+\dfrac{\kappa_{\rm offdiag}}{16\pi^2}\right) C_{eH}^{\rm tree,\,ij}, & i\neq j,
\end{cases}
\label{eq:ceh_finite_match}
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}
C_{eH}^{\rm IR}=C_{eH}^{\rm match}+\frac{\ln(\mu_{\rm match}/\mu_{\rm low})}{16\pi^2}\,\gamma\!\odot C_{eH}^{\rm match},
\label{eq:ceh_llrg}
\end{equation}
where $\gamma\!\odot C$ denotes blockwise application of $(\gamma_{\rm diag},\gamma_{\rm offdiag})$ to diagonal and off-diagonal matrix entries, respectively.
In the baseline \texttt{eft\_wilson\_uv\_rge} mode used for figures, we evaluate observables with $C_{eH}\equiv C_{eH}^{\rm IR}$.
\begin{equation}
\frac{\Gamma_{\rm tot}(D,\eta)}{\Gamma_{\rm tot}(D_0,\eta_0)}
=1+s_\Gamma\sum_{\ell=e,\mu,\tau}\mathrm{BR}_\ell^{\rm SM}
\left[
\left|\frac{C_{eH}^{\ell\ell}(D,\eta)}{C_{eH}^{\ell\ell}(D_0,\eta_0)}\right|^2-1
\right],
\label{eq:width_ratio_matched}
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}
\mu_{\mu\mu}^{\rm pred}(D,\eta)=
\left|\frac{C_{eH}^{\mu\mu}(D,\eta)}{C_{eH}^{\mu\mu}(D_0,\eta_0)}\right|^2
\left[\frac{\Gamma_{\rm tot}(D,\eta)}{\Gamma_{\rm tot}(D_0,\eta_0)}\right]^{-1}.
\label{eq:mu_pred}
\end{equation}
In the baseline figures we keep a fixed reference point $(D_0,\eta_0)=(10,1)$ for comparability across scan variants.
This fixed point should be read as a \emph{reference-normalization convention} for the relative scan-level observable in Eq.~\eqref{eq:mu_pred}, not as a physical anchor of the Higgs sector itself. The stronger normalization-side information now comes from the absolute and loop-improved parent-action comparators below together with the reference-normalization-invariant Wilson-diagonal ratios reported later in the section.
Equation~\eqref{eq:mu_pred} is implemented in \path{pslt_lib.py} as the UV+LL-RG Higgs observable; it remains a scan-level EFT module rather than a full loop-level EYMH$\rightarrow$EFT derivation.
Writing
\[
x_\mu\equiv |C_{eH}^{\mu\mu}|^2,\qquad
R_{e/\mu}\equiv \frac{|C_{eH}^{ee}|^2}{|C_{eH}^{\mu\mu}|^2},\qquad
R_{\tau/\mu}\equiv \frac{|C_{eH}^{\tau\tau}|^2}{|C_{eH}^{\mu\mu}|^2},
\]
Eq.~\eqref{eq:mu_pred} may also be rewritten exactly as
\begin{equation}
\mu_{\mu\mu}^{\rm pred}
=
\frac{A_\star x_\mu}
{1+s_\Gamma\!\left[
A_\star x_\mu
\left(
\mathrm{BR}_\mu^{\rm SM}
+\mathrm{BR}_e^{\rm SM}\frac{R_{e/\mu}}{R_{e/\mu}^\star}
+\mathrm{BR}_\tau^{\rm SM}\frac{R_{\tau/\mu}}{R_{\tau/\mu}^\star}
\right)
-\sum_{\ell=e,\mu,\tau}\mathrm{BR}_\ell^{\rm SM}
\right]},
\label{eq:mu_pred_abs_bridge}
\end{equation}
with
\[
A_\star=x_{\mu,\star}^{-1},\qquad
R_{e/\mu}^\star=\frac{|C_{eH,\star}^{ee}|^2}{|C_{eH,\star}^{\mu\mu}|^2},\qquad
R_{\tau/\mu}^\star=\frac{|C_{eH,\star}^{\tau\tau}|^2}{|C_{eH,\star}^{\mu\mu}|^2}.
\]
So the current reference-normalization arbitrariness is not an unconstrained map-level rescaling; it is exactly equivalent to three scalar bridge constants. The audit \path{code/audit_hll_absolute_normalization_bridge.py} on the canonical parented D21$\times$E21 map (\path{output/hll_absolute_normalization/hll_absolute_normalization_bridge_summary.csv}) verifies Eq.~\eqref{eq:mu_pred_abs_bridge} to machine precision ($\max|\Delta\mu_{\mu\mu}|=5.56\times10^{-8}$). More importantly, the accepted-region invariant median already fixes the electron-to-muon ratio essentially uniquely: if $R_{e/\mu}^\star$ is frozen to the invariant audit median, a two-parameter fit in $(A_\star,R_{\tau/\mu}^\star)$ still closes the canonical map at the same $5.56\times10^{-8}$ level with zero acceptance mismatch. At this bridge-reduction stage the observable-side problem is therefore already narrower than a generic reference-anchor ambiguity: the live scalar constants are reduced to the absolute amplitude $A_\star$ and the tau-to-muon ratio $R_{\tau/\mu}^\star$, while $R_{e/\mu}^\star$ is effectively fixed by the invariant-ratio audit itself.
The next source audit sharpens this split further. Because the present finite-match and LL-RG layers apply the same diagonal dressing to all three charged-lepton diagonals,
\[
C_{eH}^{\ell\ell,\,\rm IR}=Z_{\rm diag}\,C_{eH}^{\ell\ell,\,\rm tree},
\qquad
Z_{\rm diag}=
\left(1+\frac{\kappa_{\rm diag}^{\rm eff}}{16\pi^2}\right)
\left(1+\frac{\gamma_{\rm diag}\log(\mu_{\rm match}/\mu_{\rm low})}{16\pi^2}\right),
\]
the flavor ratios $R_{e/\mu}$ and $R_{\tau/\mu}$ are exact UV-tree invariants of the current diagonal chain rather than additional loop-level normalization freedoms. The audits \path{code/audit_hll_absolute_amp_source.py} and \path{code/audit_hll_tau_ratio_source.py} (canonical outputs under \path{output/hll_absolute_normalization/}) verify this statement on the D21$\times$E21 parented map: on active rows the residuals between UV and IR ratios remain at machine level ($\max|\Delta R_{e/\mu}|=6.05\times10^{-13}$, $\max|\Delta R_{\tau/\mu}|=3.23\times10^{-14}$), and at the live reference point the tau-to-muon constant is identical across tree/match/IR ($R_{\tau/\mu}^\star=8.24039\times10^{-3}$). The same audit also shows that the absolute amplitude itself factorizes exactly as
\[
A_\star=A_\star^{\rm(tree)}\,Z_{{\rm diag},\star}^{-2},
\qquad
A_\star^{\rm(tree)}\equiv |C_{eH,\star}^{\mu\mu,\,\rm tree}|^{-2},
\]
with map-level factorization residual $\max|\Delta C_{\mu\mu}^{\rm fact}|=9.32\times10^{-17}$. Numerically the remaining gap is now concentrated almost entirely in the reference tree amplitude: $A_\star^{\rm(tree)}=5.26537\times10^{8}$, $A_\star=5.20717\times10^{8}$, so the full diagonal dressing shifts the absolute normalization by only about $1.11\%$. Using the live ratio constants, a tree-only replacement for $A_\star$ already preserves the accepted region exactly, while restoring only the universal LL-RG factor reduces the map-level bridge error to $\max|\Delta\mu_{\mu\mu}|=1.08\times10^{-3}$. Within the current diagonal UV+LL-RG chain, the outstanding parent-side normalization target is therefore primarily the absolute amplitude $A_\star$ itself rather than an unresolved flavor-ratio constant.
The next source audit tightens this target once more. In the canonical parented baseline one has $\texttt{uv\_blend}=0$, so the UV-tree coupling matrix reduces to a diagonal overlap form,
\[
g_{iN}^{\rm UV}(D)=\delta_{iN}\sqrt{y_{i}^{\rm raw}(D)},
\]
and therefore the muon tree coefficient collapses exactly to a single layer:
\begin{equation}
C_{eH}^{\mu\mu,\,\rm tree}(D,\eta)
=
y_2^{\rm raw}(D)\,
\frac{P_2^{\rm kin}(D,\eta)}{M_2^2(D)}.
\label{eq:cmm_tree_single_layer}
\end{equation}
The audit \path{code/audit_hll_tree_mumu_parent_source.py} verifies Eq.~\eqref{eq:cmm_tree_single_layer} on the canonical D21$\times$E21 map with $\max|\Delta C_{\mu\mu}^{\rm tree}|=9.76\times10^{-17}$ and exact layer support ($\mu$-channel share from $N=2$ equals $1$ on every active row, while the $N=1,3$ shares remain below $10^{-26}$ in magnitude). At the live reference point this gives
\[
y_2^{\rm raw}=1.65981\times10^{-4},\qquad
\frac{P_2^{\rm kin}}{M_2^2}=2.62559\times10^{-1},\qquad
C_{eH,\star}^{\mu\mu,\,\rm tree}=4.35798\times10^{-5},
\]
so the current absolute-normalization target may be written as
\[
A_\star
=
\left(
y_2^{\rm raw}(D_\star)\,
\frac{P_2^{\rm kin}(D_\star,\eta_\star)}{M_2^2(D_\star)}
\right)^{-2}
Z_{{\rm diag},\star}^{-2}.
\]
Across active rows the log-variation of $C_{eH}^{\mu\mu,\,\rm tree}$ is dominated by the overlap factor rather than by the kinetic-mass coefficient ($\sigma[\log y_2^{\rm raw}]=4.07$, $\sigma[\log(P_2^{\rm kin}/M_2^2)]=1.97\times10^{-1}$), with corresponding correlations $0.9998$ and $0.9100$ to $\log C_{eH}^{\mu\mu,\,\rm tree}$. This suggests a sharper parent-side task ordering: first derive the reference overlap block $y_2^{\rm raw}(D_\star)$ from the EYMH/projected-Yukawa side, then lift the smaller residual coefficient $P_2^{\rm kin}/M_2^2$ and the already mild universal diagonal dressing.
The next source audit identifies this overlap block more precisely. In the canonical overlap extractor the flavor kernels differ only through the chirality-width rescaling $\sigma_{\rm scale}^{(\ell)}$, and for the muon one has $\sigma_{\rm scale}^{(\mu)}=1$ exactly. Therefore the raw overlap and the mu-flavor overlap coincide pointwise:
\[
y_2^{\rm raw}(D)=y_{\mu,2}^{\rm flavor}(D)=\bigl(g_{\mu 2}^{\rm UV}(D)\bigr)^2.
\]
More explicitly, if $u_k(D)$ are the tracked low-lying modes and $K_\mu(D)$ denotes the mu-flavor left-right overlap kernel, then
\[
y_2^{\rm raw}(D)=\sum_k w_{2k}(D)\,\bigl|\langle u_k(D),K_\mu(D)\rangle\bigr|,
\]
with
\[
K_\mu(D)=f_L^{(\mu)}(D)f_R^{(\mu)}(D)W_{\rm frame}(D),
\]
and where $w_{2k}(D)$ are the normalized Gaussian weights of the local microcanonical window around the tracked second branch. The audit \path{code/audit_hll_y2raw_parent_source.py} verifies both identities on the extracted overlap profile to machine precision ($\max|y_2^{\rm raw}-y_{\mu,2}^{\rm flavor}|=0$, $\max|(g_{\mu 2}^{\rm UV})^2-y_2^{\rm raw}|=9.87\times10^{-17}$). The same audit also shows that collapsing this block to the center-mode overlap is not adequate: on the audited D4--20 detail grid, the center-only candidate $y_{{\rm center},2}$ differs from $y_2^{\rm raw}$ by a mean relative error $8.61\times10^{-1}$ and a $95\%$ relative error $1.14$. So the remaining normalization target is better understood as a \emph{windowed projected-Yukawa block} rather than as a single-mode overlap.
One more parent-side simplification is already available for the kernel itself. In the canonical baseline the overlap extractor keeps $\sigma_L=\sigma_R\equiv \sigma_\mu=2.5$ and $W_{\rm frame}=1$, so the muon kernel is not merely approximately but exactly a symmetric midplane bridge:
\[
K_\mu(\rho,z;D)
=
A_\mu^{\rm(disc)}(D)\,
\exp\!\left[-\frac{\rho^2+z^2}{\sigma_\mu^2}\right].
\]
The audit \path{code/audit_hll_kmu_parent_candidate.py} verifies this pointwise factorization on the audited D4--20 overlap grid with relative sup residual $3.05\times10^{-15}$. Replacing the discrete normalization by the corresponding finite-box continuum normalization gives a closed-form parent-side candidate
\[
A_\mu^{\rm(box)}(D)
=
\frac{\exp[-D^2/(4\sigma_\mu^2)]}
{I_\rho(\sigma_\mu,\rho_{\max})\,I_z(\sigma_\mu,D,z_{\rm margin})},
\]
where
\[
I_\rho=\pi\sigma_\mu^2\!\left(1-e^{-\rho_{\max}^2/\sigma_\mu^2}\right),
\qquad
I_z=\frac{\sqrt{\pi}\sigma_\mu}{2}
\left[
\operatorname{erf}\!\left(\frac{D+z_{\rm margin}}{\sigma_\mu}\right)
+
\operatorname{erf}\!\left(\frac{z_{\rm margin}}{\sigma_\mu}\right)
\right].
\]
This finite-box bridge already matches the canonical discrete kernel at the $10^{-4}$ level ($\max$ relative sup error $9.45\times10^{-5}$). By contrast, the naive infinite-volume amplitude
\[
A_\mu^{\rm(\infty)}(D)=\pi^{-3/2}\sigma_\mu^{-3}\exp[-D^2/(4\sigma_\mu^2)]
\]
still misses the canonical kernel by about $2.37\times10^{-1}$. At the live reference point $(D_\star,\eta_\star)=(9.6,1.0)$ the exact discrete and finite-box amplitudes are
\[
A_\mu^{\rm(disc)}(D_\star)=3.77586\times10^{-4},
\qquad
A_\mu^{\rm(box)}(D_\star)=3.77621\times10^{-4},
\]
so the remaining parent-side task is no longer the entire kernel shape. It is better read as the origin of the effective width $\sigma_\mu$ (or its first curvature-controlled correction) inside this midplane bridge block.
That effective width now admits a cleaner three-level split. First, because the canonical kernel already closes exactly as
\[
\log K_\mu(\rho,z;D)=c_0(D)-\frac{\rho^2+z^2}{\sigma_\mu^2},
\]
a direct log-Hessian fit on the audited D4--20 overlap grid recovers $\sigma_\mu=2.5$ to machine precision ($\max |\sigma_\mu^{\rm(Hess)}/\sigma_\mu-1|=2.49\times10^{-15}$, $\max$ fit residual $1.85\times10^{-13}$). Second, a box-normalized moment inversion gives an independent finite-volume check: solving
\[
\langle \rho^2+z^2\rangle_{K_\mu}=M_2^{\rm(box)}(\sigma_\mu;\rho_{\max},z_{\max})
\]
returns $\sigma_\mu^{\rm(box)}=2.4999$ with $\max |\sigma_\mu^{\rm(box)}/\sigma_\mu-1|=4.09\times10^{-5}$. Third, one step closer to the EYMH side, write the action-derived shifted potential near either core as
\[
U(\rho,\delta z;D)
=
U_0(D)+\frac12\kappa_{\rho,\mu}(D)\rho^2+\frac12\kappa_{z,\mu}(D)\delta z^2+\cdots,
\qquad
\delta z=z\mp D/2,
\]
and define the isotropized core curvature
\[
\bar\kappa_\mu(D)=\frac{2\kappa_{\rho,\mu}(D)+\kappa_{z,\mu}(D)}{3}.
\]
In a harmonic-ground-state reading one expects a Gaussian width law $\sigma_\mu\propto \bar\kappa_\mu^{-1/4}$. Calibrating the proportionality constant at the live reference point therefore gives the current best parent-side candidate
\[
\sigma_\mu^{\rm(curv)}(D)=c_\sigma\,\bar\kappa_\mu(D)^{-1/4},
\qquad
c_\sigma=22.3027.
\]
The audit \path{code/audit_hll_sigma_mu_parent_candidate.py} shows that the core Hessian is already almost isotropic on the tested D4--20 window ($\kappa_{\rho,\mu}/\kappa_{z,\mu}\in[0.999996,0.99999997]$), the resulting curvature-controlled width stays within $1.01\times10^{-3}$ of the canonical $\sigma_\mu=2.5$, and the induced finite-box kernel deformation remains small ($\max$ relative sup error $1.11\times10^{-2}$). So the next parent-side target is now even narrower: not the entire kernel width profile, but the single calibration constant $c_\sigma$ or its first curvature correction.
One more narrowing step is already available for that constant itself. The audit \path{code/audit_hll_csigma_source.py} compares three global selectors for $c_\sigma$: direct width matching,
\[
\arg\min_c \sum_D \bigl[\log \sigma_\mu^{\rm exact}(D)-\log(c\,\bar\kappa_\mu(D)^{-1/4})\bigr]^2,
\]
finite-box midplane amplitude matching,
\[
\arg\min_c \sum_D \bigl[\log A_\mu^{\rm disc}(D)-\log A_\mu^{\rm box}(D;c)\bigr]^2,
\]
and full-kernel matching,
\[
\arg\min_c \max_D \operatorname{relsup}\!\bigl(K_\mu^{\rm exact}(D),K_\mu^{\rm box}(D;c)\bigr).
\]
All three selectors collapse onto the same narrow interval:
\[
c_\sigma^{\rm ref}=22.3033,\qquad
c_\sigma^{\rm width}=22.3025,\qquad
c_\sigma^{\rm amp}=c_\sigma^{\rm kernel}=22.3100,
\]
with relative drifts only $3.8\times10^{-5}$ (width) and $3.0\times10^{-4}$ (amplitude/kernel) from the reference calibration. The finite-box core-to-box choice $c_\sigma=22.3100$ is the most useful parent-side reading at present: it improves the maximum amplitude mismatch from $1.02\times10^{-2}$ to $1.88\times10^{-3}$ and simultaneously lowers the maximum finite-box kernel defect from $1.02\times10^{-2}$ to $1.88\times10^{-3}$. In that sense the surviving ambiguity is no longer whether a stable $c_\sigma$ exists, but why this nearly unique finite-box bridge constant emerges from the EYMH / projected-Yukawa side.
That bridge constant is now supported by two independent overlap-side checks rather than by kernel matching alone. First, the pointwise core-to-box inversion in \path{code/audit_hll_csigma_core_box_local.py} solves
\[
c_\sigma^{\rm(box,local)}(D)
=
\arg\min_c
\left|
\log A_\mu^{\rm box}(D;c)-\log A_\mu^{\rm disc}(D)
\right|
\]
on the audited D4--20 overlap profile. Apart from the known near-merger tracking outlier at $D=5$, the recovered local constants stay on the same narrow band, with mean $22.3033$, minimum $22.2825$, maximum $22.3100$, and relative span $1.23\times10^{-3}$. Second, the projected-Yukawa benchmark \path{code/audit_hll_csigma_projected_overlap.py} re-solves the fine overlap block at the representative points $D=\{6,12,18\}$ using the same shift-invert target as the original overlap extractor and verifies that the canonical rebuild is exact to machine precision ($\max$ relative rebuild error $1.27\times10^{-13}$). On the same benchmark, the projected-overlap selector
\[
\arg\min_c \sum_D \bigl[\log y_2^{\rm pred}(D;c)-\log y_2^{\rm raw}(D)\bigr]^2
\]
lands at
\[
c_\sigma^{\rm(projected)}=22.3095,
\]
with only $1.60\times10^{-4}$ relative drift from $c_\sigma^{\rm ref}$ and $2.24\times10^{-5}$ from $c_\sigma^{\rm amp}$; the corresponding pointwise selector values $22.2665$, $22.3055$, and $22.3100$ reproduce $y_2^{\rm raw}$ with maximum relative error $1.27\times10^{-4}$. So the current evidence is stronger than a single-kernel fit: both the finite-box core-to-box inversion and the projected-Yukawa overlap benchmark now select the same $c_\sigma\approx 22.31$ bridge band.
One more step now identifies the body of that bridge constant itself. The audit \path{code/audit_hll_csigma_eymh_core_source.py} expands the EYMH-side shifted potential
\[
U(\rho,\delta z;D)=m_0^2\bigl(\Omega^2-1\bigr)+(1-6\xi)\frac{\nabla^2\Omega}{\Omega}
\]
directly around one core, first in the isolated one-center Plummer limit and then in the full two-center local jet. In the one-center limit,
\[
\Omega_{\rm self}(r)=1+\frac{a}{\sqrt{r^2+\varepsilon^2}}
=
\Omega_{0,{\rm self}}+\Omega_{2,{\rm self}}\,r^2+\cdots,
\qquad
\Omega_{0,{\rm self}}=1+\frac{a}{\varepsilon},
\qquad
\Omega_{2,{\rm self}}=-\frac{a}{2\varepsilon^3},
\]
while
\[
\nabla^2\Omega_{\rm self}(r)
=
L_{0,{\rm self}}+L_{2,{\rm self}}\,r^2+\cdots,
\qquad
L_{0,{\rm self}}=-\frac{3a}{\varepsilon^3},
\qquad
L_{2,{\rm self}}=\frac{15a}{2\varepsilon^5}.
\]
So the isotropic one-center curvature source is already explicit:
\[
\kappa_{\rm self}
=
2\left[
m_0^2\bigl(2\Omega_{0,{\rm self}}\Omega_{2,{\rm self}}\bigr)
+(1-6\xi)\left(\frac{L_{2,{\rm self}}}{\Omega_{0,{\rm self}}}
-\frac{L_{0,{\rm self}}\Omega_{2,{\rm self}}}{\Omega_{0,{\rm self}}^2}\right)
\right].
\]
With the canonical EYMH parameters $(a,\varepsilon,m_0,\xi)=(0.04,0.1,1,0.14)$ this gives
\[
\kappa_{\rm self}=6353.3061,
\qquad
c_\sigma^{\rm(self)}=\sigma_\mu\,\kappa_{\rm self}^{1/4}=22.3198,
\]
already within $1.78\times10^{-3}$ of the full extracted bridge band. The same audit then includes the second center analytically in the local jet by expanding the distant term around $\delta z=z-D/2=0$ and reconstructing the full quadratic coefficients of both $\Omega$ and $\nabla^2\Omega/\Omega$. This produces an analytic two-center curvature $\bar\kappa_\mu^{\rm(analytic)}(D)$ that tracks the finite-difference EYMH Hessian to $1.68\times10^{-4}$ relative error and therefore gives
\[
c_\sigma^{\rm(analytic)}(D)=\sigma_\mu\,\bar\kappa_\mu^{\rm(analytic)}(D)^{1/4}
\]
with maximum relative error only $4.21\times10^{-5}$ on the audited D4--20 window. Equally importantly, the mirror correction remains parametrically small:
\[
\frac{\delta\kappa_{\rm mirror}(D)}{\kappa_{\rm self}}
=
\frac{\bar\kappa_\mu^{\rm(analytic)}(D)-\kappa_{\rm self}}{\kappa_{\rm self}}
\in[-6.91\times10^{-3},-1.39\times10^{-3}],
\]
so the surviving D-dependence is genuinely a subleading two-center correction to an essentially one-center EYMH core source. In that sense the mainline bridge problem is now narrower again: `why does $c_\sigma$ exist?' is no longer the right question; the more precise question is why the one-center Plummer/EYMH core sets $c_\sigma\approx22.32$ and how the small mirror correction refines it to the observed $22.30$ band.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition U5 (Core-to-projected normalization bridge).}
The remaining $c_\sigma$ ambiguity admits a direct normalization-side closure on the projected-Yukawa benchmark. Define the box-normalized projected overlap functional
\[
\mathcal N_{\mu,2}^{\rm(proj-box)}(D;c)
:=
\sum_k w_{2k}(D)\left|\left\langle u_k(D),K_\mu^{\rm box}(D;c)\right\rangle\right|,
\]
where the representative projected selector already gave
\[
c_\sigma^{\rm(projected)}=22.3095
\]
on $D=\{6,12,18\}$. Then the one-center EYMH / Plummer source
\[
c_\sigma^{\rm(self)}=22.3198,
\]
already fixes the bulk of the projected-Yukawa box normalization, while the pointwise analytic mirror refinement
\[
c_\sigma^{\rm(analytic)}(D)=\sigma_\mu\,\bar\kappa_\mu^{\rm(analytic)}(D)^{1/4}
\]
saturates the same benchmark.
\emph{Verified numerics.} The audit \path{code/audit_hll_csigma_projected_box_source.py} inserts these EYMH candidates directly into $\mathcal N_{\mu,2}^{\rm(proj-box)}$. The one-center source lies only $4.61\times10^{-4}$ above the projected selector and already reproduces the overlap block with $y_2$ log-RMSE $6.15\times10^{-3}$, maximum relative $y_2$ error $9.26\times10^{-3}$, and mean relative $y_2$ error $4.93\times10^{-3}$. Once the analytic mirror correction is included pointwise through $c_\sigma^{\rm(analytic)}(D)$, the same functional improves to log-RMSE $6.29\times10^{-4}$ with maximum relative $y_2$ error $9.89\times10^{-4}$, slightly outperforming the fixed projected selector itself (log-RMSE $9.08\times10^{-4}$, maximum relative error $1.45\times10^{-3}$). So the normalization statement is now sharper than ``the one-center core sets $22.3198$'': the one-center Plummer/EYMH core already fixes the bulk of the projected-Yukawa box normalization, and the small two-center mirror correction is enough to saturate the projected benchmark.
That remaining gap also now has a clean first-correction audit. Define the bridge constant
\[
c_\sigma(D)=\sigma_\mu\,\bar\kappa_\mu(D)^{1/4},
\]
which on the audited D4--20 grid is already almost $D$-invariant: the audit \path{code/audit_hll_sigma_mu_curvature_correction.py} finds
\[
c_\sigma^{\rm ref}=22.3027,
\qquad
\frac{\max c_\sigma-\min c_\sigma}{\langle c_\sigma\rangle}=1.38\times10^{-3}.
\]
To probe the first non-harmonic correction, expand the same core potential one order further,
\[
U(\rho,\delta z;D)
=
U_0(D)+\frac12\kappa_{\rho,\mu}(D)\rho^2+\frac12\kappa_{z,\mu}(D)\delta z^2
+\lambda_{\rho,\mu}(D)\rho^4+\lambda_{z,\mu}(D)\delta z^4+\cdots,
\]
and introduce the isotropized quartic invariant
\[
\lambda_\mu(D)=\frac{2\lambda_{\rho,\mu}(D)+\lambda_{z,\mu}(D)}{3},
\qquad
q_{4,\mu}(D)=\frac{\lambda_\mu(D)}{\bar\kappa_\mu(D)^{3/2}}.
\]
The current best corrected EYMH-side candidate is then
\[
\sigma_\mu^{\rm(curv,4)}(D)
=
c_{\sigma,*}\,\bar\kappa_\mu(D)^{-1/4}
\Bigl[1+b_4\bigl(q_{4,\mu}(D)-q_{4,\mu}(D_\star)\bigr)\Bigr],
\qquad
b_4=0.4244.
\]
Numerically this single quartic correction collapses the remaining width drift from $1.01\times10^{-3}$ to $8.47\times10^{-7}$, and pushes the finite-box kernel defect down from $1.11\times10^{-2}$ to $9.47\times10^{-5}$, essentially saturating the residual box-normalization error. In that sense the current bridge problem is now very narrow: the surviving EYMH-side ambiguity is no longer the width profile, but the source of the nearly constant bridge coefficient $c_\sigma$ and, at the next order, the quartic-response coefficient $b_4$.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition U6 (Absolute-normalization synthesis chain).}
On the current diagonal UV+LL-RG chain, the observable-side absolute normalization reduces exactly to the reference overlap block and a structurally fixed finite-box bridge:
\[
A_\star
=
Z_{{\rm diag},\star}^{-2}
\left[
y_2^{\rm raw}(D_\star)\,
\frac{P_2^{\rm kin}(D_\star,\eta_\star)}{M_2^2(D_\star)}
\right]^{-2},
\]
with \(R_{e/\mu}^\star\) already fixed by the accepted-region invariant audit for practical purposes and \(R_{\tau/\mu}^\star\) an exact UV-tree invariant of the current diagonal chain.  Moreover,
\[
y_2^{\rm raw}(D)=y_{\mu,2}^{\rm flavor}(D)=\bigl(g_{\mu 2}^{\rm UV}(D)\bigr)^2,
\]
and the corresponding overlap kernel closes exactly as
\[
K_\mu(\rho,z;D)=A_\mu^{\rm(disc)}(D)\exp\!\left[-\frac{\rho^2+z^2}{\sigma_\mu^2}\right].
\]
Thus the only remaining parent-side source ambiguity is the EYMH origin of the nearly constant bridge coefficient \(c_\sigma\) in the structurally fixed width law
\[
\sigma_\mu(D)=c_\sigma(D)\,\bar\kappa_\mu(D)^{-1/4},
\]
or, after the first quartic tightening,
\[
\sigma_\mu^{\rm(curv,4)}(D)
=
c_{\sigma,\star}\,\bar\kappa_\mu(D)^{-1/4}
\Bigl[1+b_4\bigl(q_{4,\mu}(D)-q_{4,\mu}(D_\star)\bigr)\Bigr].
\]
On the representative projected-Yukawa benchmark \(D=\{6,12,18\}\), this residual bridge constant is already fixed in bulk by the one-center source \(c_\sigma^{\rm(self)}\) and saturated by the analytic two-center refinement \(c_\sigma^{\rm(analytic)}(D)\).
\emph{Audit synthesis.}
The new consolidation audit \path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_hll_absolute_normalization_synthesis_source.py} reads the existing normalization-side summaries directly and confirms that the chain now separates into exact-closure stages and secondary-tightening stages.  On the exact side, the diagonal flavor ratios remain UV-tree invariants to machine precision (\(\max|\Delta R_{e/\mu}^{\rm UV\to IR}|=6.05\times10^{-13}\), \(\max|\Delta R_{\tau/\mu}^{\rm UV\to IR}|=3.23\times10^{-14}\)); the amplitude factorization \(A_\star=A_\star^{\rm(tree)}Z_{{\rm diag},\star}^{-2}\) closes with residual \(9.32\times10^{-17}\); the single-layer reduction \(C_{\mu\mu}^{\rm tree}=y_2^{\rm raw}(P_2^{\rm kin}/M_2^2)\) closes with residual \(9.76\times10^{-17}\) and exact \(N=2\) support; the overlap identity \(y_2^{\rm raw}=(g_{\mu2}^{\rm UV})^2\) closes with residual \(9.87\times10^{-17}\); and the Gaussian midplane kernel factorization closes with relative sup residual \(3.05\times10^{-15}\).  On the structural-but-not-final side, the finite-box Gaussian bridge already stays at \(9.45\times10^{-5}\) relative sup error, the curvature width law is within \(1.01\times10^{-3}\) of the canonical width, and the quartic correction collapses that drift to \(8.47\times10^{-7}\) while lowering the finite-box kernel defect to \(9.47\times10^{-5}\).  Excluding the known near-merger outlier at \(D=5\), the pointwise core-to-box inversion keeps \(c_\sigma\) on the narrow band \([22.2825,22.3100]\) with relative span \(1.23\times10^{-3}\).  The one-center EYMH source itself gives \(c_\sigma^{\rm(self)}=22.3198\), only \(7.37\times10^{-4}\) above the reference selector and \(4.61\times10^{-4}\) above the projected selector, while the analytic two-center jet tracks the extracted \(c_\sigma(D)\) with maximum relative error \(4.21\times10^{-5}\).  Finally, on the projected-box benchmark, the one-center source alone reproduces the overlap block with log-RMSE \(6.15\times10^{-3}\) and maximum relative \(y_2\) error \(9.26\times10^{-3}\), whereas the analytic mirror refinement improves this to log-RMSE \(6.29\times10^{-4}\) and maximum relative error \(9.89\times10^{-4}\), slightly better than the fixed projected selector itself.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary U6 (What is closed, and what remains only as secondary tightening, for \texorpdfstring{$A_\star$}{A*}).}
On the current audited domain, the following normalization-side blocks are already structurally closed:
\begin{enumerate}
\item the flavor-ratio side \((R_{e/\mu}^\star,R_{\tau/\mu}^\star)\), with \(R_{e/\mu}^\star\) practically fixed by the invariant audit and \(R_{\tau/\mu}^\star\) exact along the diagonal UV chain;
\item the exact amplitude factorization \(A_\star=A_\star^{\rm(tree)}Z_{{\rm diag},\star}^{-2}\);
\item the single-layer tree reduction \(C_{\mu\mu}^{\rm tree}=y_2^{\rm raw}(P_2^{\rm kin}/M_2^2)\);
\item the overlap identity \(y_2^{\rm raw}=y_{\mu,2}^{\rm flavor}=(g_{\mu2}^{\rm UV})^2\);
\item the Gaussian midplane kernel shape \(K_\mu=A_\mu^{\rm(disc)}\exp[-(\rho^2+z^2)/\sigma_\mu^2]\);
\item the structural width profile \(\sigma_\mu\propto \bar\kappa_\mu^{-1/4}\) together with its first quartic tightening.
\end{enumerate}
Only secondary tightening remains in:
\[
c_\sigma,\qquad
\delta c_\sigma^{\rm(mirror)},\qquad
b_4,
\]
namely in the one-center EYMH normalization that fixes the bridge coefficient, the already small analytic two-center mirror correction, and the first quartic-response coefficient.  Therefore the mainline task is no longer to discover a new overlap object, a new kernel shape, or a new support reduction for \(A_\star\).  The chain is already structurally closed; what remains, if any further improvement is desired, is only to formalize the one-center EYMH source of \(c_\sigma\) and, secondarily, to tighten its mirror/quartic corrections.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition U7 (\texorpdfstring{$c_\sigma$}{c_sigma} closes as a one-center EYMH constant plus a tiny analytic mirror multiplier).}
Let
\[
c_\sigma^{\rm(exact)}(D)=\sigma_\mu\,\bar\kappa_\mu^{\rm(FD)}(D)^{1/4},
\qquad
c_\sigma^{\rm(self)}=\sigma_\mu\,\kappa_{\rm self}^{1/4},
\]
and define the analytic two-center curvature refinement by
\[
\delta\kappa_{\rm mirror}(D)=\bar\kappa_\mu^{\rm(analytic)}(D)-\kappa_{\rm self}.
\]
Then the analytic EYMH bridge coefficient satisfies the exact factorization
\[
c_\sigma^{\rm(analytic)}(D)
=
\sigma_\mu\,\bar\kappa_\mu^{\rm(analytic)}(D)^{1/4}
=
c_\sigma^{\rm(self)}
\left(1+\frac{\delta\kappa_{\rm mirror}(D)}{\kappa_{\rm self}}\right)^{1/4}.
\]
Equivalently,
\[
c_\sigma^{\rm(analytic)}(D)
=
c_\sigma^{\rm(self)}
\left[
1+\frac14\frac{\delta\kappa_{\rm mirror}(D)}{\kappa_{\rm self}}
+O\!\left(\left(\frac{\delta\kappa_{\rm mirror}(D)}{\kappa_{\rm self}}\right)^2\right)
\right],
\]
so the entire \(D\)-dependence of the bridge coefficient is carried by a parametrically small mirror multiplier on top of the one-center EYMH core constant.
\emph{Verified numerics.}
The audit \path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_hll_csigma_tightening_synthesis.py} consolidates the current \(c_\sigma\)-side summaries and confirms that this is no longer merely a heuristic reading.  The extracted bridge coefficient itself already stays on the narrow D4--20 band
\[
22.2802\le c_\sigma^{\rm(exact)}(D)\le 22.3111,
\qquad
\frac{\max c_\sigma^{\rm(exact)}-\min c_\sigma^{\rm(exact)}}{\langle c_\sigma^{\rm(exact)}\rangle}=1.38\times10^{-3}.
\]
The one-center source is constant,
\[
c_\sigma^{\rm(self)}=22.3198,
\]
and the analytic mirror factorization closes to machine precision,
\[
\max_D\left|
c_\sigma^{\rm(analytic)}(D)
-c_\sigma^{\rm(self)}
\left(1+\frac{\delta\kappa_{\rm mirror}(D)}{\kappa_{\rm self}}\right)^{1/4}
\right|
=
3.55\times10^{-15}.
\]
Moreover,
\[
\frac{\delta\kappa_{\rm mirror}(D)}{\kappa_{\rm self}}
\in[-6.91\times10^{-3},-1.39\times10^{-3}],
\]
so the mirror multiplier itself lies in the very narrow interval
\[
0.998269\le
\left(1+\frac{\delta\kappa_{\rm mirror}(D)}{\kappa_{\rm self}}\right)^{1/4}
\le 0.999653,
\]
with relative span \(1.38\times10^{-3}\).  Even the first-order linearization already captures that multiplier to
\[
\max_D {\rm relerr}\!\left(
1+\frac14\frac{\delta\kappa_{\rm mirror}}{\kappa_{\rm self}},
\left(1+\frac{\delta\kappa_{\rm mirror}}{\kappa_{\rm self}}\right)^{1/4}
\right)
=4.50\times10^{-6}.
\]
Against the extracted finite-difference bridge coefficient, the one-center constant alone stays within
\[
\max_D {\rm relerr}\!\left(c_\sigma^{\rm(self)},c_\sigma^{\rm(exact)}\right)=1.78\times10^{-3},
\]
while the analytic mirror-refined coefficient improves this immediately to
\[
\max_D {\rm relerr}\!\left(c_\sigma^{\rm(analytic)},c_\sigma^{\rm(exact)}\right)=4.21\times10^{-5}.
\]
The same tightening is visible on the projected-Yukawa box benchmark: the one-center source gives maximum relative \(y_2\) error \(9.26\times10^{-3}\) and log-RMSE \(6.15\times10^{-3}\), whereas the analytic mirror refinement lowers these to \(9.89\times10^{-4}\) and \(6.29\times10^{-4}\), i.e. improvement factors \(9.37\) and \(9.79\), respectively.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary U7 (What remains secondary after the \texorpdfstring{$c_\sigma$}{c_sigma} tightening).}
On the audited normalization chain, the mainline \(c_\sigma\) problem is no longer an open support-selection or kernel-shape problem.  It is already closed, to current numerical precision, by:
\begin{enumerate}
\item the one-center EYMH core constant \(c_\sigma^{\rm(self)}\), which sets the bulk normalization scale;
\item the exact analytic mirror multiplier \(\left(1+\delta\kappa_{\rm mirror}/\kappa_{\rm self}\right)^{1/4}\), which carries the residual \(D\)-dependence;
\item the projected-box benchmark, which is already saturated by the analytic refinement at sub-\(10^{-3}\) maximum relative \(y_2\) error.
\end{enumerate}
Accordingly, the remaining \(c_\sigma\)-side work is now only secondary tightening:
\[
b_4,
\qquad
\text{and at most sub-}10^{-3}\text{ projected-box polish.}
\]
No new overlap object, no new support reduction, and no new kernel ansatz is needed for the normalization mainline.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition U1 (UV-to-EFT witness).}
On the audited map, the scan-level chain
\[
C_{eH}^{\rm tree}\;\longrightarrow\; C_{eH}^{\rm match}\;\longrightarrow\; C_{eH}^{\rm IR}
\]
admits an explicit operator-basis reconstruction with numerically controlled deformation relative to the UV-tree baseline.
\emph{Verified numerics.} The reproducible audit \path{scan_hll_uv_to_eft_matching.py} (canonical outputs \path{paper/hll_uv_to_eft_map.csv} and \path{paper/hll_uv_to_eft_summary.csv}) shows that in the baseline UV settings ($\texttt{uv\_blend}=0$, $\texttt{uv\_m2\_power}=1$, $\kappa_{\rm diag}=\kappa_{\rm offdiag}=0$) the finite-match step is numerically null at map level ($\max |\Delta C_{\mu\mu}^{\rm match}|=10^{-30}$), while LL-RG drift remains bounded: $\langle |\Delta \mu_{\mu\mu}|\rangle \simeq 6.19\times10^{-4}$ and $\max |\Delta \mu_{\mu\mu}|\simeq 8.93\times10^{-3}$; $f(\chi^2_{\mu\mu}<4)$ is unchanged between UV-tree and UV+LL-RG.
To make that witness explicit at the operator level, we also export a layer-resolved basis audit with \path{scan_hll_uv_operator_basis_audit.py}; the corresponding map and summary are stored under \path{paper/hll_uv_operator_basis_*}. There the UV-tree closure is reconstructed as
\begin{equation}
C_{eH}^{\rm tree}=\sum_{N=1}^{3}\left(\frac{P_N^{\rm kin}}{M_N^2}\right)\,\mathcal B_N^{(eH)},
\qquad
\mathcal B_N^{(eH)}\equiv \mathbf g_N^{(eH)}\mathbf g_N^{(eH)\,T},
\label{eq:ceh_operator_basis}
\end{equation}
where $\mathbf g_N^{(eH)}$ is the flavor-layer coupling vector entering Eq.~\eqref{eq:wilson_tree_uv}; this notation avoids collision with the micro-degeneracy scalar $g_N$ and the visibility factor $B_N$ used elsewhere. With explicit blockwise witnesses for $\Delta C^{\rm match}$ and $\Delta C^{\rm RGE}$, the tree/match/IR reconstruction residuals are numerically zero at map level, while the reconstructed $\mu_{\mu\mu}$ agrees with the native UV+LL-RG observable to $\max |\Delta \mu_{\mu\mu}^{\rm recon}|=7.31\times 10^{-8}$. This does not yet constitute a full EYMH$\rightarrow$SMEFT derivation, but it upgrades the current closure from a black-box Wilson-map audit to an explicit basis-level EFT witness.
As a next structured step beyond fixed $(\kappa_{\rm diag},\kappa_{\rm offdiag})$, we test a finite-match comparator whose effective shifts are built from local UV-basis invariants (shell spread, coefficient variation of $P_N^{\rm kin}/M_N^2$, and a normalized off-diagonal mixing norm). We treat this as a structured comparator, not as a replacement for the current constant finite-match baseline.
On the canonical D21$\times$E21 grid, the basis witness remains exact ($\max|\Delta \mu_{\mu\mu}^{\rm recon}|=5.55\times10^{-8}$, reconstruction residuals zero), and the refreshed comparator stays in the small-deformation regime relative to the constant baseline ($\langle|\Delta\mu_{\mu\mu}|\rangle=5.06\times10^{-4}$, $p95=1.03\times10^{-3}$, $\max|\Delta\mu_{\mu\mu}|=7.87\times10^{-3}$) with zero acceptance mismatch. The off-diagonal component is numerically inactive in the current UV basis, while the diagonal-threshold scale simply controls the strength of a small diagonal threshold deformation.
To quantify this interpretation, we run a Phase-2 diagonal-threshold window audit. On the refreshed D21$\times$E21 scan, both gates require zero acceptance mismatch and select
\[
\begin{aligned}
\kappa_{\rm diag}^{\rm(th)}&\in[0.25,\,1.0]
&&\text{(conservative comparator window)},\\
\kappa_{\rm diag}^{\rm(th)}&\in[0.25,\,1.5]
&&\text{(extended stable window)}.
\end{aligned}
\]
The canonical witness choice $\kappa_{\rm diag}^{\rm(th)}=1.0$ therefore sits at the upper edge of the conservative window: it keeps the diagonal threshold feed-through explicit while remaining in the small-deformation regime. This sharpens the current interpretation of $\kappa_{\rm diag}^{\rm(th)}$ as a diagonal threshold susceptibility. The remaining missing ingredient is not off-diagonal closure, which is numerically absent in the present basis, but a parent-action normalization for this susceptibility.
We now push this normalization one step closer to the parent action by introducing an action-normalized finite-match comparator. The basis witness remains exact ($\max|\Delta \mu_{\mu\mu}^{\rm recon}|=5.54\times10^{-8}$, zero reconstruction residuals), and the comparator keeps zero acceptance mismatch with only small deformation relative to the refreshed constant baseline ($\langle|\Delta\mu_{\mu\mu}|\rangle=1.00\times10^{-3}$, $p95=1.86\times10^{-3}$, $\max|\Delta\mu_{\mu\mu}|=1.59\times10^{-2}$).
We now probe the remaining gap directly with an absolute parent-action comparator. In this mode the external diagonal-scale choice is removed and replaced by an absolute witness built from parent-action-side invariants and coefficient alignment:
\[
\kappa_{\rm abs}^{(d)}\equiv S_{\rm kin}^{(P)}\,A_{\rm coeff},
\qquad
A_{\rm coeff}\equiv \frac{\|c_N\|_2}{\|c_N\|_1},
\]
so that the effective diagonal threshold is generated locally rather than chosen externally. On the canonical D21$\times$E21 grid, this absolute parent-action comparator remains exact at the basis level ($\max|\Delta \mu_{\mu\mu}^{\rm recon}|=5.55\times10^{-8}$, zero reconstruction residuals), and the map again keeps zero acceptance mismatch relative to the refreshed constant baseline with only small deformation ($\langle|\Delta\mu_{\mu\mu}|\rangle=7.17\times10^{-4}$, $p95=1.17\times10^{-3}$, $\max|\Delta\mu_{\mu\mu}|=1.14\times10^{-2}$).
To test whether this remaining prefactor can be pushed closer to a genuine curved-background loop origin, we next introduced a heat-kernel comparator family. The direct lesson from the raw and flat-space-subtracted local witnesses is negative: a purely local $a_2$-type diagonal density remains dominated by the well region and does not align with the absolute parent-action normalization. The useful signal appears only after promoting the loop witness to a \emph{contrast-based} non-local quantity that compares well and barrier curvature structure. On the canonical D21$\times$E21 grid this contrast-based loop comparator remains exact at the basis level, keeps zero acceptance mismatch, and has only small deformation relative to the refreshed constant baseline ($\langle|\Delta\mu_{\mu\mu}|\rangle=4.88\times10^{-4}$, $p95=8.76\times10^{-4}$, $\max|\Delta\mu_{\mu\mu}|=7.67\times10^{-3}$). We then add one more loop-improved comparator layer by modulating the parent-action absolute witness with a contrast-based loop prefactor,
\begin{align}
\kappa_{\rm diag}^{\rm eff}
&=
 \kappa_{\rm abs}^{(d)}\,
 \Pi_{\rm hk}^{(d)}\,
 s_{\rm sh}\,
 (1+c_{\rm cv})\,
 a_{\rm norm}^{(d)},
\\
\kappa_{\rm offdiag}^{\rm eff}
&=
 \kappa_{\rm abs}^{(o)}\,
 \Pi_{\rm hk}^{(o)}\,
 s_{\rm sh}\,
 m_{\rm off}\,
 a_{\rm norm}^{(o)}.
\end{align}
Here $(\kappa_{\rm abs}^{(d/o)},\Pi_{\rm hk}^{(d/o)},s_{\rm sh},c_{\rm cv},m_{\rm off},a_{\rm norm}^{(d/o)})$ denote the absolute parent-action witness, heat-kernel loop prefactor, shell-spread witness, coefficient variation, off-diagonal mixing norm, and normalized action weight. This loop-improved absolute comparator remains exact at the basis level ($\max|\Delta \mu_{\mu\mu}^{\rm recon}|=5.56\times10^{-8}$, zero reconstruction residuals) and keeps zero acceptance mismatch, with only small deformation relative to the refreshed constant baseline ($\langle|\Delta\mu_{\mu\mu}|\rangle=3.37\times10^{-4}$, $p95=6.92\times10^{-4}$, $\max|\Delta\mu_{\mu\mu}|=5.33\times10^{-3}$).
We now add one further EYMH-side screening layer, which keeps the same contrast-based loop family but modulates it by local mass-access and curvature-screen factors together with shell-dispersion screening,
\begin{align}
A_X &= \sqrt{\frac{X_{\rm well}}{1+X_{\rm well}}},
&
A_R &= (1+R_{\rm bar})^{-1/2},\\
\Pi_{\rm EYMH}
&=
\Pi_{\rm hk}
 A_X A_R
 \sqrt{\frac{s_{\rm sh}}{1+s_{\rm sh}}}
 \sqrt{c_{\rm align}}
 (1+g_{\rm cv}+c_{\rm tree})^{-1/2}.
\end{align}
Here $(X_{\rm well},R_{\rm bar},s_{\rm sh},c_{\rm align},g_{\rm cv},c_{\rm tree})$ denote the well-mass, barrier-curvature, shell-spread, coefficient-alignment, gap-variation, and tree-diagonal variation witnesses. This EYMH-screened loop comparator remains exact at the basis level ($\max|\Delta \mu_{\mu\mu}^{\rm recon}|=5.56\times10^{-8}$, zero reconstruction residuals) and keeps zero acceptance mismatch, with only small deformation relative to the refreshed constant baseline ($\langle|\Delta\mu_{\mu\mu}|\rangle=4.17\times10^{-5}$, $p95=7.63\times10^{-5}$, $\max|\Delta\mu_{\mu\mu}|=6.46\times10^{-4}$). We therefore read it as the current best \emph{EYMH-side absolute loop-prefactor comparator}.
We then promote the two dominant source factors already isolated by the EYMH-side audits into a refreshed source-informed EYMH comparator. This mode again remains exact at the basis level, keeps zero acceptance mismatch, and stays in the same small-deformation class relative to the refreshed constant baseline ($\langle|\Delta\mu_{\mu\mu}|\rangle=5.2\times10^{-5}$, $p95=1.09\times10^{-4}$, $\max|\Delta\mu_{\mu\mu}|=7.81\times10^{-4}$). The promoted source-informed prefactor remains nonzero across the whole D21$\times$E21 map, so this is the current best \emph{source-informed EYMH comparator}.

We now rewrite that same source-informed block in explicit parent-action participation/compressibility language. This parented rewrite remains exact at the basis level ($\max|\Delta \mu_{\mu\mu}^{\rm recon}|=5.56\times10^{-8}$, zero reconstruction residuals), keeps zero acceptance mismatch, and on the canonical D21$\times$E21 fix grid is map-identical to the source-informed comparator. Relative to the refreshed constant baseline it therefore inherits the same small-deformation scale ($\langle|\Delta\mu_{\mu\mu}|\rangle=5.2\times10^{-5}$, $p95=1.09\times10^{-4}$, $\max|\Delta\mu_{\mu\mu}|=7.81\times10^{-4}$). The numerical role of this mode is not to introduce a new baseline, but to show that the same comparator can be written directly in parent-action participation/coherence and shell-background compressibility language without changing the canonical map.
Figure~\ref{fig:hll_uv_parented_comparator} summarizes this normalization-side endpoint visually: the parented rewrite is map-identical to the source-informed comparator while the remaining deformation relative to the constant finite-match baseline is small and structured.
