\section{First-Principles Symmetry-Channel Test for \texorpdfstring{$\chi$}{chi}}
\label{app:chi_sym}

Using the explicit definition in Eq.~\eqref{eq:chi_first_principles}, we computed $M_{12}$ and $\chi_N^{\rm(sym)}$ on 2D axisymmetric grids for $D=\{6,12,18\}$ with coarse/mid/fine resolutions. The modes are normalized with the cylindrical measure $2\pi\rho\,d\rho\,dz$, and orthogonality satisfies $|\langle\psi_1,\psi_2\rangle|<10^{-15}$ in all runs.

\begin{table}[htbp]
\centering
\caption{Fine-grid symmetry-channel extraction using Eq.~\eqref{eq:chi_first_principles}. Parameters: $a=0.04$, $\varepsilon=0.1$, $m_0=1$, $\xi=0.14$.}
\label{tab:chi_sym}
\begin{tabular}{cccc}
\hline\hline
$D$ & $|M_{12}^{\rm(sym)}|$ & $\chi_N^{\rm(sym)}$ & $|\langle\psi_1,\psi_2\rangle|$ \\
\hline
6  & $2.09\times 10^{-16}$ & $1.66\times 10^{-19}$ & $3.12\times 10^{-17}$ \\
12 & $6.03\times 10^{-16}$ & $5.69\times 10^{-19}$ & $3.47\times 10^{-18}$ \\
18 & $6.24\times 10^{-16}$ & $6.20\times 10^{-19}$ & $2.11\times 10^{-16}$ \\
\hline\hline
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

These values are numerically consistent with symmetry-protected cancellation of the parity-symmetric overlap channel. Across coarse/mid/fine grids, $M_{12}^{\rm(sym)}$ changes sign but remains at $\Order(10^{-16})$, indicating no resolved nonzero mixing in this channel.

For this reason, the phenomenological Rank-2 coefficient in the main text is interpreted as a localized-channel effective coupling (Section~\ref{sec:conclusion}), not as $\chi_N^{\rm(sym)}$ from Eq.~\eqref{eq:chi_first_principles}.



\section{Localized-Channel First-Principles Extraction of \texorpdfstring{$\chi$}{chi}}
\label{app:chi_loc}

We compute the nonzero mixing channel directly in localized basis using
\begin{equation}
M_{LR}^{(H)}=\frac{\lambda_2-\lambda_1}{2},\qquad
\chi_N^{(LR)}=\frac{|M_{LR}^{(H)}|}{\bar{\Gamma}_N},
\end{equation}
with $\bar{\Gamma}_N$ from Eq.~\eqref{eq:gamma_basis_map}. Here $\lambda_{1,2}$ are generalized operator eigenvalues from $K\psi=\lambda M\psi$ in the localized extraction solver; they are not the bound-state energy convention $E=\omega^2-m_0^2<0$ used in Appendix~\ref{app:Veff}. The extraction uses \texttt{code/extract\_chi\_localized\_2d.py} with fixed settings:
\begin{itemize}
\item Domain: $\rho_{\max}=3.0$, $z_{\max}=D/2+6.0$.
\item Grids: coarse $(d\rho,dz)=(0.12,0.06)$, mid $(0.08,0.04)$, fine $(0.06,0.03)$.
\item Solver: generalized eigensystem (shift-invert low-mode targeting, $\sigma=2.5$), tolerance $10^{-8}$, maxiter $3\times10^4$.
\item Acceptance criteria: $\delta_{\Delta E}\equiv |\Delta E_{\rm level}-\Delta E_{\rm fine}|/|\Delta E_{\rm fine}|<5\%$, $\delta_{\chi}\equiv |\chi_{\rm level}-\chi_{\rm fine}|/|\chi_{\rm fine}|<5\%$, and null-channel absolute bound $|M_{12}^{\rm(sym)}|<10^{-12}$.
\end{itemize}
The complete pipeline, scripts, and raw tables used here are available in the project repository: \url{https://github.com/boypatrick/PSLT}.

\begin{equation}
f(\mathcal{R}_3>0.90)=0.927,\quad

f(\chi^2_{\mu\mu}<4)\in[0.1625,\,0.1681].
\end{equation}
The best-fit point moves within $(D,\eta)\approx(8.07\text{--}9.42,\ 1.29\text{--}3.10)$ across these six profiles. Thus, at current scan resolution, profile-interpolation uncertainty is subdominant for $\mathcal{R}_3$ and controlled for the $H\to\mu\mu$ acceptance metric.

\section{Preliminary First-Principles Replacement Candidate Checks}
\label{app:fp_candidates}

\subsection{1D Phase-Space Candidate for \texorpdfstring{$g_N$}{gN}}
To test a first-principles replacement direction for Eq.~\eqref{eq:gN_reg}, we evaluated the semiclassical 1D candidate on the same action-derived axis potential:
\begin{equation}
\rho_{\rm WKB}(E;D)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{U(z;D)<E}\frac{\dd z}{\sqrt{E-U(z;D)}},
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}
g_N^{\rm(ps)}(D)=1+\int_{E_{\min}(D)}^{E_N(D)}\rho_{\rm WKB}(E';D)\,\dd E',\qquad E_{\min}(D)\equiv \min_z U(z;D).
\end{equation}
Using the reproducible script \texttt{extract\_gn\_phase\_space\_candidate.py} (in the \texttt{code/} directory), we ran a coarse/mid/fine test at $D=12$ (Table~\ref{tab:gn_ps_d12}). The candidate is numerically stable at the few-$10^{-3}$ level versus fine grid.

\centering
\caption{Preliminary 1D phase-space candidate $g_N^{\rm(ps)}$ at $D=12$.}
\label{tab:gn_ps_d12}
\small
\begin{tabular}{cccccccc}
\hline\hline
level & $dz$ & $E_1$ & $E_2$ & $E_3$ & $g_1^{\rm(ps)}$ & $g_2^{\rm(ps)}$ & max rel.$g$ vs fine \\
\hline
coarse & 0.04 & $-0.636696$ & $-0.608074$ & $-0.575010$ & $1.056725$ & $1.054421$ & $1.31\times10^{-2}$ \\
mid    & 0.02 & $-0.637061$ & $-0.608419$ & $-0.575335$ & $1.066813$ & $1.074099$ & $5.30\times10^{-3}$ \\

\subsection{2D Axisymmetric Benchmark for \texorpdfstring{$g_N^{\rm(ps)}$}{gN(ps)}}
To reduce projection ambiguity, we also ran a 2D axisymmetric benchmark on $D=\{6,12,18\}$ using the same localized extraction chain as Appendix~\ref{app:chi_loc}: identical generalized operator build, identical box/range convention, and the same shift-invert low-mode settings $(\sigma=2.5,\ \mathrm{tol}=10^{-8},\ \mathrm{maxiter}=3\times10^4)$. We define
\begin{equation}
N_{\rm ps}(E)=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int \dd^2x\,\dd^2p\;\Theta(E-U-p^2)
=\frac{1}{2}\int \rho\,\dd\rho\,\dd z\,[E-U(\rho,z)]_+,
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{W}_N\equiv[\lambda_1,\lambda_N],\qquad
g_{N,\rm raw}^{\rm(ps)}=1+\int_{\mathcal{W}_N}\rho_{\rm ps}(E')\,\dd E'
=1+N_{\rm ps}(\lambda_N)-N_{\rm ps}(\lambda_1),\qquad
\hat g_N\equiv\frac{g_{N,\rm raw}^{\rm(ps)}}{g_{3,\rm raw}^{\rm(ps)}}.
\end{equation}
The two-dimensional phase-space extraction exports both the low-$N$ profile table and the low-mode spectral table with explicit window bounds. For non-fine levels, the maximum profile deviation is
\begin{equation}
\max_{\text{non-fine}}\left(\max_N\frac{|\hat g_N-\hat g_N^{\rm(fine)}|}{|\hat g_N^{\rm(fine)}|}\right)=2.35\times10^{-2}.
\end{equation}
As in the 1D candidate check, this benchmark is reported as a replacement candidate. To test map-level impact, we connected the baseline, legacy Cardy comparator, one-dimensional phase-space candidate, and two-dimensional phase-space candidate to the main scan through the reproducible script \texttt{scan\_gn\_profile\_impact.py}. Results in Table~\ref{tab:gn_profile_impact} show that the main conclusion is retained: relative to the bounded two-dimensional baseline, the largest absolute shift is $0.0669$ in $f(\mathcal{R}_3>0.90)$ (for the raw two-dimensional phase-space candidate), while high-$N$ runaway remains controlled with $f(N_{\rm win}>3)\simeq2.78\times10^{-4}$ in all tested modes.

\subsection{Baseline Cross-Check Under \texorpdfstring{$g_{\rm mode}=\mathrm{fp\_2d\_full}$}{g mode fp 2d full}}
To implement a strict same-framework upgrade, we set the bounded two-dimensional phase-space profile as the baseline. Instead of direct low-$N$ substitution plus geometric continuation, this mode uses an explicit bounded microcanonical window for $N\le 3$ and an energy-gap tail prescription for $N>3$:
\begin{equation}
g_N^{\rm(full)}(D)=g_3^{\rm(ps)}(D)\hat g_N(D),\quad
\hat g_n=\left(\hat g_n^{\rm(dir)}\right)^{1-\alpha}\left(\hat g_n^{\rm(win)}\right)^{\alpha}\ (n=1,2,3),
\end{equation}
with
\begin{equation}
\hat g_1^{\rm(win)}=1+N_{\rm ps}(\lambda_3)-N_{\rm ps}(\lambda_1),\ 
\hat g_2^{\rm(win)}=1+N_{\rm ps}(\lambda_3)-N_{\rm ps}(\lambda_2),\ 
\hat g_3^{\rm(win)}=1,
\end{equation}
with $g_3^{\rm(ps)}(D)\equiv 1+N_{\rm ps}(\lambda_3)-N_{\rm ps}(\lambda_1)$ from the same 2D spectrum table.
For $N>3$,
\begin{equation}
\hat g_N^{\rm(target)}=\left(\frac{\Delta N_{\rm ps}^{(N)}}{\Delta N_{\rm ps}^{(3)}}\right)^{s}
\exp\!\left[-\beta\frac{\lambda_N-\lambda_3}{\lambda_3-\lambda_2}\right],
\end{equation}
followed by the same per-step finite-volume clipping used in \texttt{pslt\_lib.py}. In the present baseline run, we use $(\alpha,\beta,s)=(0.8,1.1,0.0)$. Results are summarized in Table~\ref{tab:gn_baseline_replacement} (source CSV: \texttt{paper/gn\_baseline\_replacement.csv}). Relative to baseline \texttt{fp\_2d\_full}, the legacy Cardy reference lowers $f(\mathcal{R}_3>0.90)$ by $0.0500$ (from $0.9269$ to $0.8769$), lowers $f(\chi^2_{\mu\mu}<4)$ by $0.0014$ (from $0.1500$ to $0.1486$), and keeps $f(N_{\rm win}>3)\simeq2.78\times10^{-4}$. Therefore, the map-level main conclusion is retained under this baseline switch.

\subsection{Geometry-Informed and Microscopic Lindblad Scan-Ready Module for Open-System \texorpdfstring{$\chi$}{chi}}
\label{app:chi_open_scan}
As a methodological bridge (not a replacement of Appendix~\ref{app:chi_loc}), we consider a two-level open-system model in the standard GKSL/Lindblad form~\cite{GKSL1976,Lindblad1976}:
\begin{equation}
\dot{\rho}=-i[H,\rho]+\gamma_\phi\mathcal{D}[\sigma_z]\rho+\gamma_{\rm mix}\mathcal{D}[\sigma_x]\rho,\qquad
H=\begin{pmatrix}0 & \Delta/2\\ \Delta/2 & 0\end{pmatrix}.
\end{equation}
As external motivation for this diagnostic direction, recent entanglement-based flavor analyses report that minimizing scattering-generated entanglement can qualitatively reproduce observed CKM/PMNS patterns~\cite{ThalerTrifinopoulos2025}. In this manuscript we use that connection only as qualitative support for coherence-sensitive diagnostics, not as a baseline closure assumption.
For this module we set $\gamma_{\rm mix}(D)\equiv M_{LR}^{(H)}(D)$ from Appendix~\ref{app:chi_loc}, and define a geometry-informed dephasing proxy
\begin{equation}
\gamma_\phi(D)\equiv \sqrt{\left\langle\left(\delta V\right)^2\right\rangle_\rho},\qquad
\delta V=V_{\rm eff}-\bar V_{\rm eff}(r),
\end{equation}
with cylindrical weighting $\langle\cdots\rangle_\rho$. The minimal Lindblad evolution and geometry-profile extraction output reproducible diagnostics such as
\begin{equation}
C_{\max}\equiv \max_t |\rho_{LR}(t)|,\qquad P_{\max}\equiv \max_t \rho_{RR}(t),
\end{equation}
and a normalized proxy $\chi_{\rm eff}^{\rm(proxy)}=2\gamma_{\rm mix}C_{\max}/\bar{\Gamma}_N$. Results for $D=\{6,12,18\}$ are summarized in Table~\ref{tab:chi_open_geom}. Extending the same profile to $D=4,\dots,20$ gives a stable geometry-informed ratio band $\chi_{\rm open}/\chi_{LR}\in[0.0852,0.2127]$ with mean $0.1269$. This value belongs to the geometry-only proxy closure in this subsection; the anchor-calibrated microscopic band is reported separately in Eq.~\eqref{eq:chi_open_micro_band} and Table~\ref{tab:chi_open_sensitivity}.

To move this module toward microscopic closure, we also extract localized-basis couplings $(g_z,g_x)$ directly from the same 2D eigenmodes and set
\begin{equation}
\gamma_\phi=\kappa_{\rm env}\,g_z^2 S(0),\qquad
\gamma_{\rm mix}=\kappa_{\rm env}\,g_x^2 S(\Delta E),\qquad
S(\omega)=\frac{2\tau_{\rm env}}{1+(\omega\tau_{\rm env})^2},
\end{equation}
from the localized microscopic extraction. To avoid map-level fitting, we calibrate $\kappa_{\rm env}$ with a multi-anchor rule: least-squares match to geometry-profile ratios on anchors $D=\{6,9,12,15,18\}$, then validate on holdout $D=\{4,5,7,8,10,11,13,14,16,17,19,20\}$. This gives $\kappa_{\rm env}=4.31\times10^{5}$ with anchor RMSE $8.61\times10^{-2}$ and holdout RMSE $6.95\times10^{-2}$ (holdout max-abs error $1.25\times10^{-1}$). The corresponding scan-level band is
\begin{equation}
\chi_{\rm open}^{\rm(micro)}/\chi_{LR}\in[0.0173,0.3271],\qquad \langle \chi_{\rm open}^{\rm(micro)}/\chi_{LR}\rangle=0.1380,
\label{eq:chi_open_micro_band}
\end{equation}
from the microscopic ratio-band audit.
To pin down the microscopic bookkeeping, we additionally export a bridge audit with map, summary, and figure artifacts. On the calibrated $D=4,\dots,20$ knot set, this audit reconstructs
\[
\gamma_\phi=\kappa_{\rm env}g_z^2S(0),\qquad
\gamma_{\rm mix}=\kappa_{\rm env}g_x^2S(\Delta E),\qquad
\chi_{\rm eff}^{\rm(micro)}=2\gamma_{\rm mix}C_{\max}/\Gamma_{\rm ref}
\]
with numerically negligible residuals:
\[
\max|\Delta\gamma_\phi|=2.03\times10^{-20},\quad
\max|\Delta\gamma_{\rm mix}|=4.40\times10^{-14},\quad
\max|\Delta\chi_{\rm eff}^{\rm(loader)}|=5.58\times10^{-17}.
\]
The corresponding bridge summary identifies the effective system as the localized two-level $(L/R)$ subspace, the current bath witness as a Gaussian Markov bath with Lorentzian PSD, and the coupling operators as the diagonal/off-diagonal pair $(\sigma_z,\sigma_x)$. This means the open-system gap is no longer in the system-to-Lindblad bookkeeping, but in the microscopic EYMH origin and normalization of the bath itself.

We can therefore push the bath normalization one step further without over-claiming a full microscopic derivation. In the $\kappa_{\rm env}$ window audit, we treat
\[
\kappa_{\rm env}\rightarrow \kappa_{\rm scale}\,\kappa_{\rm env}^{\rm(calib)}
\]
as a uniform bath-normalization susceptibility that rescales both $\gamma_\phi$ and $\gamma_{\rm mix}$ while leaving the localized two-level Hamiltonian $(\Delta E,\sigma_x)$ fixed. On the current $D=4,\dots,20$ knot set, the calibration-consistent candidate window is
\[
\kappa_{\rm scale}\in[0.5,1.0],
\]
while a broader scan-stable window
\[
\kappa_{\rm scale}\in[0.25,1.5]
\]
keeps the map-level fractions $f(\mathcal R_3>0.90)$ and $f(\chi^2_{\mu\mu}\le 4)$ unchanged and only relaxes the holdout tolerance slightly. In this sense, $\kappa_{\rm env}$ is no longer just a single-point fit parameter: within the present witness it acts as a bounded bath-normalization susceptibility. What still remains open is the EYMH-side derivation of its absolute normalization and of the microscopic bath degrees of freedom themselves.

The newer parent-bath audits sharpen this statement. First, they show that the witness already factorizes into a system block $(g_z^2,g_x^2)$, a bath-shape block $(S_{zz}(0),S_{xx}(\Delta E))$, and an amplitude block $\kappa_{\rm env}$, with the canonical constant normalization remaining the unique exact amplitude choice. Second, they rewrite the rates as the projected bath block
\[
K_{\rm bath}=\kappa_{\rm env}\sqrt{K_{\rm sys}}\,K_{\rm spec}\,\sqrt{K_{\rm sys}},
\label{eq:chi_parent_bath_block}
\]
and the subsequent family, log-coordinate, normal-coordinate, and generator-affinity audits show that the canonical projected bath class is uniquely selected on the current knot set: $(m,u,v)=(0,0,0)$, $(p_{\rm sys},p_{\rm spec})=(0,0)$, $(\zeta_{\rm sys},\zeta_{\rm spec})=(0,0)$, and $(q_{ss},q_{bb},q_{sb})=(0,0,0)$. It is therefore natural to define the normalized projected bath block
\[
B_a(z):=\kappa_{\rm env}^{-1}K_{{\rm sys},a}^{-1/2}(z)\,K_{{\rm bath},a}(z)\,K_{{\rm sys},a}^{-1/2}(z),
\]
which the audited factorization already identifies as the bath-shape witness itself. Thus the remaining open-system gap is no longer bookkeeping or nearby-family ambiguity; it is the final parent-action statement for why the projected bath generator itself naturally lives in this affine log class.
Finally, \texttt{scan\_chi\_open\_system\_parent\_bath\_cocycle\_audit.py} turns this into a positive integrability statement: after dividing out $\kappa_{\rm env}$, the normalized parent bath block defines an exact additive cocycle in the canonical log variables, with pairwise cocycle residuals at $8.95\times10^{-16}$ on the identifiable $\phi$ subset and $8.64\times10^{-13}$ on the mixing branch, while triangle flatness defects stay at $1.11\times10^{-15}$ in both channels. So the affine log-generator is now supported not only by nearby-family exclusion, but also by exact cocycle/flatness closure.
Pushing one step further, \texttt{scan\_chi\_open\_system\_parent\_bath\_potential\_audit.py} shows that this cocycle is globally integrable on the current knot set: the normalized bath block admits an exact anchored scalar potential $\Phi_a=\log(B_a/B_a^{\rm ref})$ whose direct decomposition, anchor-average recovery, and nearest-neighbor chain recovery agree to machine precision (max residuals $\sim10^{-14}$ on the identifiable $\phi$ subset and $\sim5\times10^{-13}$ or better on the mixing branch). Thus the projected bath witness is no longer characterized only by an affine local generator; it also carries a single-valued anchored potential, so the remaining gap is specifically the parent-action origin of this potential itself.

Figure~\ref{fig:chi_open_parent_bath_potential} gives the representative late-stage open-system diagnostic: the normalized parent-bath block has already been reduced to an integrable projected potential rather than an unconstrained reservoir fit.

\begin{figure}[t]
    \centering
    \includegraphics[width=0.95\linewidth]{chi_open_system_parent_bath_potential.png}
    \caption{Projected parent-bath potential closure for the open-system module on the calibrated knot set. The normalized bath block admits a single-valued scalar primitive $\Phi_a$ whose direct reconstruction, anchor recovery, and chain recovery agree to machine precision: the maximum residuals are $1.42\times10^{-14}$ on the identifiable $\phi$ subset and $5.50\times10^{-13}$ on the mixing branch. Source files: \texttt{paper/chi\_open\_system\_parent\_bath\_potential\_*}.}
    \label{fig:chi_open_parent_bath_potential}
\end{figure}

At this point the cocycle/potential relation can be written explicitly on the audited knot set. Defining the discrete increment
\[
G_a(z_i,z_j):=\log B_a(z_j)-\log B_a(z_i),
\]
the cocycle audit shows that $G_a$ is additively consistent:
\[
G_a(z_i,z_k)=G_a(z_i,z_j)+G_a(z_j,z_k),
\]
up to the machine-level residuals quoted above. Fixing an anchor $z_\star$, the potential is then not an extra ansatz but the integrated cocycle itself,
\[
\Phi_a(z):=G_a(z_\star,z),
\qquad
G_a(z_i,z_j)=\Phi_a(z_j)-\Phi_a(z_i).
\]
So the anchored potential is simply the scalar primitive of the already-audited normalized bath block in canonical log variables.

This also gives the natural local parent-bath effective-action normal form on the audited knot set. Writing $z=(z_{\rm sys},z_{\rm spec})$ for the canonical log variables and $B_a(z)=B_a^{\rm ref} e^{\Phi_a(z)}$, the cocycle closure identifies the bath witness as an anchored exponential potential on the projected bath block,
\[
K_{{\rm bath},a}(z)=\kappa_{\rm env}\,K_{{\rm sys},a}^{1/2}(z)\,e^{\Phi_a(z)}\,K_{{\rm sys},a}^{1/2}(z),
\]
so, channelwise on the present positive knot set,
\[
B_a(z)=e^{\Phi_a(z)},
\qquad
\log K_{{\rm bath},a}(z)=\log \kappa_{\rm env}+\log K_{{\rm sys},a}(z)+\Phi_a(z).
\]
while the family, log-coordinate, normal-coordinate, and generator-affinity audits isolate the canonical class as the one with no surviving first local log-curvature jet. Equivalently, on a sufficiently small neighborhood $\mathcal N$ of the canonical anchor,
\[
\Phi_a(z+\tau)=\Phi_a(z)+\nabla\Phi_a(z)\!\cdot\!\tau+R_{a,2}(z,\tau),
\qquad
|R_{a,2}(z,\tau)|\le \tfrac12 \sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal N}\|D^2\Phi_a(\zeta)\|\,\|\tau\|^2,
\]
so the affine log-generator is just the first Taylor jet of $\Phi_a$ on the projected bath block, while the first non-affine correction is controlled by $R_{a,2}$. The audited canonical point is precisely the one where this first non-affine correction is rejected on the current knot set. In this sense, the open-system witness is already promoted from a free nearby-family fit to a locally anchored projected effective action; what remains open is not the existence or local normal form of $\Phi_a$, but the microscopic EYMH derivation of its coefficients, of $\kappa_{\rm env}$, and of the bath degrees of freedom themselves.

The implication chain here is non-circular. The primitive audited object is the normalized projected bath block itself, namely $K_{{\rm bath},a}/\kappa_{\rm env}$ on the canonical knot set. The nearby-family, log-coordinate, normal-coordinate, and generator-affinity audits act directly on that block and select the unique local affine generator class without assuming any potential. The cocycle audit is then applied to the same normalized block, upgrading this local affine closure to an exact additive consistency statement in the canonical log variables. The potential audit comes only after that step: it integrates the already-audited cocycle and checks path-independence / anchor recovery, rather than feeding any potential ansatz back into the family selection. Equation~(\ref{eq:chi_parent_bath_block}) together with the anchored form above therefore repackages the same projected bath witness into a local effective-action normal form; it does not create new evidence by definition. The remaining open-system gap is still the microscopic parent-action origin of the coefficients and bath degrees of freedom, not the internal consistency of the projected bath block on the present audited knot set.

This also sharpens the remaining microscopic origin question into three narrower obligations. First, since
\[
B_a(z)=\kappa_{\rm env}^{-1}K_{{\rm sys},a}^{-1/2}(z)\,K_{{\rm bath},a}(z)\,K_{{\rm sys},a}^{-1/2}(z),
\]
one has
\[
\partial_{\log \kappa_{\rm env}}\log B_a(z)=0,
\qquad
\partial_{\log \kappa_{\rm env}}\log K_{{\rm bath},a}(z)=1,
\]
so $\kappa_{\rm env}$ already behaves as a pure scalar bath-susceptibility modulus rather than as an independent bath-shape degree of freedom. Second, the coefficients of $\Phi_a$ are no longer free local fit parameters on the audited knot set: the cocycle primitive $G_a(z_i,z_j)=\Phi_a(z_j)-\Phi_a(z_i)$ already fixes their discrete local jet, and the remaining task is only to identify the parent-action source of those coefficients. Third, the unresolved bath degrees of freedom have already been reduced to the two effective response channels appearing in the audited micro bridge,
\[
\gamma_{\phi,a}=\kappa_{\rm env}\,g_{z,a}^2\,S_{zz,a}(0),
\qquad
\gamma_{{\rm mix},a}=\kappa_{\rm env}\,g_{x,a}^2\,S_{xx,a}(\Delta E_a),
\]
rather than to an arbitrary reservoir family. Thus the next microscopic step, if taken at all, is not to re-justify the local projected effective-action form, but to explain why the parent EYMH dynamics projects specifically onto this scalar modulus, this anchored potential jet, and this minimal two-channel bath kernel.

One can also sharpen the second obligation without yet claiming a full parent-action derivation of the coefficients. Restrict to the identifiable audited subset
\[
\mathcal K_{\phi}:=\{D:\phi_a(D)\ {\rm is\ identifiable}\},
\]
and let
\[
G_a(D_i,D_j):=\Phi_a(D_j)-\Phi_a(D_i)
\]
denote the already-audited cocycle primitive on that subset. For any local anchor $D_\star\in\mathcal K_\phi$ and any finite neighborhood $\mathcal N(D_\star)\subset\mathcal K_\phi$, the discrete first jet coefficient is fixed by the primitive itself, not by a new fit,
\[
c_a(D_\star):=
\arg\min_c\sum_{D_j\in\mathcal N(D_\star)}
w_j\Bigl[\Phi_a(D_j)-\Phi_a(D_\star)-c(D_j-D_\star)\Bigr]^2
=
\frac{\sum_j w_j(D_j-D_\star)G_a(D_\star,D_j)}
{\sum_j w_j(D_j-D_\star)^2},
\]
with local remainder
\[
r_{a,\star}(D_j):=\Phi_a(D_j)-\Phi_a(D_\star)-c_a(D_\star)(D_j-D_\star),
\qquad
\sum_j w_j(D_j-D_\star)\,r_{a,\star}(D_j)=0.
\]
So the local coefficient of $\Phi_a$ on the audited knot set is already an anchor-locked discrete jet of the cocycle primitive, rather than an unconstrained nearby-family parameter. The current audits then push one step further still: on the entire exported map, the observed parent-bath action coefficient is exactly the signed carrier of the same primitive,
\[
A_a(D):=\bigl({\rm parent\text{-}bath\ action}_\phi\bigr)_a(D)=-\,\Phi_a(D),
\qquad
c_a^{(A)}(D_\star)=-\,c_a^{(\Phi)}(D_\star).
\]
Numerically, the potential reconstruction residual stays below $1.5\times10^{-14}$ on the identifiable subset, the anchor and chain recoveries stay below $7.2\times10^{-15}$ and $2.2\times10^{-14}$ respectively, the audited cocycle / local-generator defect stays below $7.2\times10^{-15}$, and the generator/potential representatives agree pointwise at the same $2.2\times10^{-14}$ level. Moreover, the canonical affine class is not merely acceptable but strongly selected: the generator-affinity and normal-coordinate selection gaps exceed the canonical objective by factors of $2.2\times10^{8}$ and $4.4\times10^{8}$ respectively. Thus the unresolved microscopic issue is no longer whether $\Phi_a$ admits a stable local first jet on the audited knot set; it is why the EYMH parent action generates precisely this signed discrete jet and not some nearby nonlinear deformation.

Within that first obligation, the present audits already push one step further than a generic ``fitted constant'' interpretation. If one allows a nearby absolute-normalization family
\[
\kappa_{\rm eff}(z)=\kappa_{\rm env}\,s\,F(z),
\]
with a single anchor-fitted amplitude $s$ but a non-uniform profile $F(z)$, then the normalized projected bath block becomes
\[
B_a^{(F)}(z)=\kappa_{\rm eff}^{-1}(z)\,K_{{\rm sys},a}^{-1/2}(z)\,K_{{\rm bath},a}(z)\,K_{{\rm sys},a}^{-1/2}(z)
=(sF(z))^{-1}B_a(z).
\]
So an exact amplitude-only renormalization preserves the normalized block if and only if $F(z)$ is constant on the audited knot set. This is exactly what the absolute-normalization audit finds: the canonical locked uniform family keeps the bridge-normalization residuals at machine level ($\sim 8\times10^{-13}$ on the observable/mixing channels), whereas the best non-uniform admissible family improves the holdout RMSE only marginally ($6\times10^{-5}$) while inflating the same normalized residuals to $\sim 10^{-2}$. In that precise local sense, $\kappa_{\rm env}$ is already singled out as the unique exact amplitude modulus, and the remaining microscopic question is no longer whether it mixes with bath shape on the present knot set, but why the parent EYMH dynamics produces this modulus in the first place.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O1 (Projected two-channel interaction).}
Without invoking a full bath derivation, the parent perturbation projected to the audited parity doublet already fixes the leading localized interaction to a two-channel Pauli block. Let $\Delta V$ denote the parent perturbation projected to the parity doublet, so that on the audited knot set
\[
V_{{\rm par},a}=
\begin{pmatrix}
v_{11,a} & v_{12,a}\\
v_{12,a} & v_{22,a}
\end{pmatrix}
\]
is real and symmetric. Rotating to the localized basis with
\[
U_{LR}=\frac{1}{\sqrt2}
\begin{pmatrix}
1&1\\
1&-1
\end{pmatrix}
\]
gives
\[
V_{{\rm loc},a}=U_{LR}^{T}V_{{\rm par},a}U_{LR}
=\frac{v_{11,a}+v_{22,a}}{2}\,I
+v_{12,a}\,\sigma_z
+\frac{v_{11,a}-v_{22,a}}{2}\,\sigma_x.
\]
Hence every audited localized two-level block already lies in the span of $I,\sigma_z,\sigma_x$, with no surviving $\sigma_y$ component: after removing the trace part, the leading projected interaction is at most two-channel. The extracted couplings are therefore not independent fit coordinates but exactly the Pauli coefficients of the localized block,
\[
g_{z,a}=|v_{12,a}|,
\qquad
g_{x,a}=\left|\frac{v_{11,a}-v_{22,a}}{2}\right|.
\]
This remains an effective two-level statement rather than a completed EYMH bath theorem, but it shows that the present $\sigma_z/\sigma_x$ bridge is already the most general leading projection compatible with the audited real localized doublet. Numerically, the full $17$-point micro export verifies this decomposition at machine precision: the reconstructed localized entries $(V_{LL},V_{RR},V_{LR})$ agree with the exported map at $\le 7\times10^{-18}$, while $g_z-|v_{12}|$ and $g_x-\bigl|(v_{11}-v_{22})/2\bigr|$ stay below $2.2\times10^{-18}$ and $1.3\times10^{-18}$ respectively. The remaining microscopic bath question is therefore narrower than a generic reservoir identification: one still has to explain why the parent EYMH environment induces the two on-shell kernels multiplying these projected $\sigma_z$ and $\sigma_x$ directions, not why those directions themselves appear in the audited two-level reduction.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O2 (Single gap-locked response family).}
Within the present minimal bridge, the dephasing and mixing kernels are not independent shape functions but on-shell evaluations of a single response family. The audited micro extraction uses
\[
S_a(\omega)=\frac{2\tau_{{\rm env},a}}{1+(\omega\tau_{{\rm env},a})^2},
\]
so that
\[
S_{zz,a}(0)=S_a(0)=2\tau_{{\rm env},a},
\qquad
S_{xx,a}(\Delta E_a)=S_a(\Delta E_a)=\frac{2\tau_{{\rm env},a}}{1+(\Delta E_a\tau_{{\rm env},a})^2}.
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\frac{S_{xx,a}(\Delta E_a)}{S_{zz,a}(0)}
=\frac{1}{1+(\Delta E_a\tau_{{\rm env},a})^2},
\]
so the mixing channel is just the same minimal Lorentzian response sampled on shell at the transition energy, while the dephasing channel is the zero-frequency limit of that same family. On the current $17$-point micro export this identification is exact at the CSV level: the reconstructed residuals for $S_{zz,a}(0)=2\tau_{{\rm env},a}$ and $S_{xx,a}(\Delta E_a)=2\tau_{{\rm env},a}/(1+(\Delta E_a\tau_{{\rm env},a})^2)$ vanish to machine precision, while the ratio relation above holds with maximal defect $1.1\times10^{-16}$. Thus the unresolved EYMH bath-origin problem is narrower still: beyond the already-audited two-channel projection, one only has to explain why the parent environment induces this single minimal response family and why its on-shell samples are taken at $\omega=0$ and $\omega=\Delta E_a$.

One can push this ``single response family'' statement one notch further without yet claiming a full bath derivation. The same audited family is exactly the Fourier image of the minimal even single-pole correlation kernel
\[
C_a(t)=e^{-|t|/\tau_{{\rm env},a}},
\]
for which
\[
S_a(\omega)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}dt\,C_a(t)e^{i\omega t}
=2\int_0^\infty e^{-t/\tau_{{\rm env},a}}\cos(\omega t)\,dt
=\frac{2\tau_{{\rm env},a}}{1+(\omega\tau_{{\rm env},a})^2}.
\]
Moreover, on the present audited knot set the correlation time is not a second free shape coordinate: the exported micro bridge uses
\[
\tau_{{\rm env},a}=\max\!\left(\frac{\tau_{\rm scale}}{\omega_{1,a}},\tau_{\rm floor}\right),
\]
and the floor never activates on the current $17$-point map, so one has numerically
\[
\tau_{{\rm env},a}=\frac{1}{\omega_{1,a}}
\]
to machine precision throughout the audited window. Hence the minimal response family is already gap-locked,
\[
S_a(\omega)=\frac{2/\omega_{1,a}}{1+(\omega/\omega_{1,a})^2},
\qquad
\frac{S_{xx,a}(\Delta E_a)}{S_{zz,a}(0)}
=\frac{1}{1+(\Delta E_a/\omega_{1,a})^2}.
\]
On the current export the maximal defect in $\tau_{{\rm env},a}-\omega_{1,a}^{-1}$ is $1.1\times10^{-16}$, the on-shell suppression law above again holds to $3.3\times10^{-16}$, and the resulting band $S_{xx,a}(\Delta E_a)/S_{zz,a}(0)$ stays in the narrow interval $[0.9246,0.9800]$. In this precise effective sense, the remaining EYMH bath-origin question is no longer why two unrelated kernels appear, but why the parent environment linearizes to a single dominant pole whose correlation time is locked to the same local gap scale $\omega_{1,a}^{-1}$ that enters the audited two-level reduction.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O3 (Schur-resolvent reformulation).}
The dominant-pole question can be stated directly in the same parent-kernel language used in the EYMH subsection. Let
\[
R_a(\omega):=u_a^{\top}(M_a^2+\omega^2 I)^{-1}u_a
\]
denote the projected Schur-resolvent response of the parent bath/complement block in the same localized channel. Its spectral decomposition reads
\[
R_a(\omega)=\sum_{n\ge1}\frac{Z_{n,a}}{\Omega_{n,a}^2+\omega^2},
\qquad
Z_{n,a}:=|\langle u_a,n,a\rangle|^2\ge 0.
\]
If the first mode dominates on the audited on-shell window,
\[
R_a(\omega)\approx \frac{Z_{1,a}}{\Omega_{1,a}^2+\omega^2},
\]
then, after absorbing the residue $Z_{1,a}$ into the overall amplitude block $\kappa_{\rm env}$ and the projected couplings $g_{z,a},g_{x,a}$, one recovers exactly the same single-pole family used above. Identifying moreover $\Omega_{1,a}=\omega_{1,a}$ yields the observed gap-locked form
\[
S_a(\omega)=\frac{2/\omega_{1,a}}{1+(\omega/\omega_{1,a})^2}.
\]
The present audits do not yet prove this first-mode dominance theorem, but they isolate it as the next genuinely microscopic target rather than leaving an undifferentiated reservoir ansatz. On the EYMH parent side, the log-det / Schur witness and the parent block-determinant / Schur audit already close to machine precision, with $\max|J_{\rm direct}-J_{\rm parent}|=1.30\times10^{-18}$, $\max|S_{\rm parent}-S_{\rm resp}|=5.55\times10^{-16}$, and $\max|\Delta \widehat G_{\rm Schur}|=6.66\times10^{-16}$. On the open-system side, the same audited knot set already satisfies $\tau_{{\rm env},a}=\omega_{1,a}^{-1}$ and the on-shell rate identities to machine precision. In this narrowed sense, the unresolved bath-origin problem is no longer why some effective Lorentzian fit works, but why the projected Schur resolvent is first-mode dominant and why its leading pole is locked to the same local gap scale $\omega_{1,a}$.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Theorem Target O1 (First-mode remainder bound).}
The remaining microscopic step is an explicit remainder estimate rather than a qualitative bath ansatz. Decompose
\[
R_a(\omega)=R^{(1)}_a(\omega)+\mathcal R_a(\omega),
\qquad
R^{(1)}_a(\omega):=\frac{Z_{1,a}}{\Omega_{1,a}^2+\omega^2},
\]
so that
\[
\mathcal R_a(\omega)=\sum_{n\ge2}\frac{Z_{n,a}}{\Omega_{n,a}^2+\omega^2}.
\]
Then the exact relative remainder satisfies
\[
\frac{|\mathcal R_a(\omega)|}{R^{(1)}_a(\omega)}
\le
\sum_{n\ge2}
\frac{Z_{n,a}}{Z_{1,a}}
\frac{\Omega_{1,a}^2+\omega^2}{\Omega_{n,a}^2+\omega^2}.
\]
Hence, if one can show on the audited on-shell window $\omega\in\{0,\Delta E_a\}$ that
\[
\varepsilon_a:=
\sup_{\omega\in\{0,\Delta E_a\}}
\sum_{n\ge2}
\frac{Z_{n,a}}{Z_{1,a}}
\frac{\Omega_{1,a}^2+\omega^2}{\Omega_{n,a}^2+\omega^2}
\ll 1,
\]
then
\[
R_a(\omega)=R^{(1)}_a(\omega)\,[1+O(\varepsilon_a)]
\]
throughout the same on-shell window. This is the cleanest proof-level formulation of the dominant-pole claim: it is enough to control the complement remainder in the projected Schur resolvent, rather than to derive a full microscopic reservoir from scratch. The current export already shows that this on-shell window is numerically narrow, with $\Delta E_a/\omega_{1,a}\in[0.1430,0.2857]$ and therefore $S_{xx,a}(\Delta E_a)/S_{zz,a}(0)\in[0.9246,0.9800]$, so the audited dephasing and mixing channels sample only the low-frequency shoulder of the same pole. What is still missing is exactly the parent-side bound that identifies $\Omega_{1,a}=\omega_{1,a}$ and proves that the higher-mode remainder is uniformly negligible on this window.

A useful quantitative sharpening is immediate once one separates the gap question from the tail-weight question. Define
\[
q_a:=\frac{R_a(\Delta E_a)}{R_a(0)},\qquad
r_a:=\frac{\Delta E_a}{\Omega_{1,a}},\qquad
\nu_{n,a}:=\frac{Z_{n,a}/\Omega_{n,a}^2}{R_a(0)},
\]
so that $\sum_{n\ge1}\nu_{n,a}=1$ and
\[
q_a=\sum_{n\ge1}\frac{\nu_{n,a}}{1+(\Delta E_a/\Omega_{n,a})^2}.
\]
If the higher modes satisfy the conditional gap bound $\Omega_{n,a}\ge g_a\,\Omega_{1,a}$ for all $n\ge2$ with some $g_a>1$, then with $f(x):=(1+x^2)^{-1}$ one has
\[
q_a\ge \nu_{1,a} f(r_a) + (1-\nu_{1,a}) f\!\left(\frac{r_a}{g_a}\right),
\]
hence
\[
1-\nu_{1,a}
\le
\frac{q_a-f(r_a)}{f(r_a/g_a)-f(r_a)}.
\]
Since $\varepsilon_a(0)=\frac{1-\nu_{1,a}}{\nu_{1,a}}$ and $\varepsilon_a(\Delta E_a)\le \varepsilon_a(0)\frac{1+r_a^2}{g_a^2+r_a^2}$, the theorem target can be restated as a two-knob quantitative task: prove a parent-side lower bound on $g_a$ and a small on-shell defect $\delta_{q,a}:=q_a-f(r_a)$. On the audited knot set, the exported gap-conditioned audit shows that for the representative benchmark $g_a=2$ one has
\[
f(r_a/2)-f(r_a)\in[1.50\times10^{-2},\,5.55\times10^{-2}],
\qquad
\frac{1+r_a^2}{4+r_a^2}\in[2.54\times10^{-1},\,2.65\times10^{-1}],
\]
so even a future parent-side match at the modest level $\delta_{q,a}\le 10^{-3}$ would already force
\[
1-\nu_{1,a}\le 6.68\times10^{-2},
\qquad
\varepsilon_a(\Delta E_a)\le 1.82\times10^{-2}
\]
uniformly across the current audited window. The present effective export, by contrast, has $\max|\delta_{q,a}|=3.33\times10^{-16}$ because the fitted response family is exactly single-pole on shell. This does not yet prove the parent-side gap itself, but it converts the remaining Schur-resolvent problem into a sharply quantified gap-plus-defect statement rather than a diffuse reservoir ansatz.

One can now push this one notch further on the parent side without yet claiming a full spectral theorem. Write the background-normalized canonical complement block as
\[
\widetilde K_a:=
\begin{pmatrix}
K_{11,a} & C_a/\sqrt{K_{{\rm bg},a}}\\
C_a/\sqrt{K_{{\rm bg},a}} & G_a
\end{pmatrix},
\qquad
G_a:=K_{22,a}/K_{{\rm bg},a},
\]
with the audited geometric-mean identity
\[
\frac{C_a^2}{K_{{\rm bg},a}}=(K_{11,a}-1)(G_a-1).
\]
Hence
\[
\widetilde K_a = I + v_a v_a^\top,
\qquad
v_a=
\begin{pmatrix}
\sqrt{K_{11,a}-1}\\[2pt]
\sqrt{G_a-1}
\end{pmatrix},
\]
so the two proxy eigenvalues are fixed exactly by
\[
\lambda^{\rm(proxy)}_{1,a}=1,
\qquad
\lambda^{\rm(proxy)}_{2,a}=1+\|v_a\|^2=K_{11,a}+G_a-1.
\]
Calibrating the identity mode to the already-audited gap-locked pole then gives the calculable parent-side proxies
\[
\begin{aligned}
\Omega^{\rm(proxy)}_{1,a}&:=\omega_{1,a},\\
\Omega^{\rm(proxy)}_{2,a}&:=\omega_{1,a}\sqrt{K_{11,a}+G_a-1},\\
g^{\rm(proxy)}_a&:=\frac{\Omega^{\rm(proxy)}_{2,a}}{\Omega^{\rm(proxy)}_{1,a}}
=\sqrt{K_{11,a}+G_a-1}.
\end{aligned}
\]
The corresponding audit closes to machine precision:
\[
\max\left|C_a^2/K_{{\rm bg},a}-(K_{11,a}-1)(G_a-1)\right|=2.78\times10^{-16},
\]
\[
\max\left|\lambda^{\rm(proxy)}_{1,a}-1\right|
=
\max\left|\lambda^{\rm(proxy)}_{2,a}-(K_{11,a}+G_a-1)\right|
=4.44\times10^{-16}.
\]
On the nontrivial active rows of the D21xE21 fix grid this already yields
\[
g^{\rm(proxy)}_a\in[1.3537,\,1.5609],
\qquad
\mathrm{median}\,g^{\rm(proxy)}_a=1.4692.
\]
On the overlap with the currently audited open-system knot set, the induced separator band becomes
\[
f(r_a/g^{\rm(proxy)}_a)-f(r_a)\in[1.43\times10^{-2},\,4.03\times10^{-2}],
\]
so a future parent-side defect estimate as mild as $\delta_{q,a}\le 10^{-3}$ would already imply
\[
1-\nu_{1,a}\le 6.98\times10^{-2},
\qquad
\varepsilon_a(\Delta E_a)\le 3.60\times10^{-2}.
\]
This is still only a proxy, not a proof that the true Schur-resolvent spectrum has the same gap. But it does show that the canonical projected EYMH complement block already carries a concrete, calculable gap floor rather than leaving $g_a$ completely unconstrained.

One can also turn this proxy into a local operator-family lower bound by combining it with the exact parent mismatch functional from Proposition~U4. Let
\[
\delta\theta:=
\begin{pmatrix}
\alpha-1\\
\beta-1
\end{pmatrix},
\qquad
Q_{\rm loc}(\delta\theta,\lambda)
:=
\frac12\,\delta\theta^\top H\,\delta\theta + C_4\lambda^4,
\]
where the stationarity audit supplies the exact quadratic matrix $H$ in the $(\alpha,\beta)$ sector and the exact quartic coefficient $C_4$ in the mixing direction. On the audited local box
\[
|\alpha-1|\le 0.1,\qquad
|\beta-1|\le 0.1,\qquad
|\lambda|\le 0.2,
\]
the parent-kernel scan gives the empirical coercivity bound
\[
J_{\rm parent}(\alpha,\beta,\lambda)\ge c_{\rm loc}\,Q_{\rm loc}(\delta\theta,\lambda),
\qquad
c_{\rm loc}=3.98\times10^{-1},
\]
with $\lambda_{\min}(H)=1.33\times10^{-3}$ and $C_4=1.497\times10^{-3}$. Hence any local operator-side candidate obeying $J_{\rm parent}\le J_\ast$ inside that box must satisfy
\[
\|\delta\theta\|
\le
\rho_\ast:=
\sqrt{\frac{2J_\ast}{c_{\rm loc}\lambda_{\min}(H)}}.
\]
For the same rowwise gap proxy
\[
g_a(\alpha,\beta)=\sqrt{e^{\alpha S_{{\rm part},a}}+e^{\beta S_{{\rm schur},a}}-1},
\]
the corresponding audit gives a local Lipschitz bound $\|\nabla g_a\|\le L^{\rm(box)}_a$, so
\[
g_a(\alpha,\beta)\ge g_{0,a}-L^{\rm(box)}_a\rho_\ast,
\qquad
g_{0,a}:=\sqrt{K_{11,a}+G_a-1}.
\]
Taking $J_\ast=10^{-6}$, the audited box has $L^{\rm(box)}_{\max}=5.11\times10^{-1}$ and therefore yields the uniform local operator-family floor
\[
g_a\ge 1.3375.
\]
Direct enumeration of the admissible scan points with $J_{\rm parent}\le 10^{-6}$ is sharper still and gives
\[
g_a\ge 1.3537
\]
throughout the same local family. On the overlap with the current open-system knot set, the conservative analytic floor $g_a\ge 1.3375$ already implies
\[
f(r_a/g_a)-f(r_a)\ge 1.19\times10^{-2},
\]
so the same reference defect $\delta_{q,a}\le 10^{-3}$ would force
\[
1-\nu_{1,a}\le 8.37\times10^{-2},
\qquad
\varepsilon_a(\Delta E_a)\le 5.28\times10^{-2}.
\]
This is still not a proof of the exact complement spectrum, because it remains confined to the audited projected family. But it upgrades the previous proxy statement into a genuine local operator-side lower bound: within the canonical projected EYMH basin, a small exact parent mismatch already forbids the second-mode scale from collapsing back to the leading pole.

Corollary~1 also gives a minimal lifting lemma from this local family floor to the exact Schur-reduced operator. Let
\[
\sigma_a:=-\log\det(I-A_a),
\]
with the same positive semidefinite complement contraction
\[
A_a=H_{PP,a}^{-1/2}H_{PQ,a}H_{QQ,a}^{-1}H_{QP,a}H_{PP,a}^{-1/2}.
\]
If the eigenvalues of $A_a$ lie in $[0,1)$, then the eigenvalues of $I-A_a$ also lie in $(0,1]$, and therefore
\[
I-A_a\succeq \det(I-A_a)\,I=e^{-\sigma_a}I.
\]
Hence the exact Schur-reduced operator obeys the Loewner bound
\[
H_{{\rm eff},a}
=
H_{PP,a}^{1/2}(I-A_a)H_{PP,a}^{1/2}
\succeq
e^{-\sigma_a}H_{PP,a},
\]
so if the projected family supplies any lower gap floor $g^{\rm(fam)}_a$, then the exact effective gap satisfies
\[
g^{\rm(exact)}_a\ge e^{-\sigma_a/2} g^{\rm(fam)}_a.
\]
Using the conservative audited family floor $g^{\rm(fam)}_a\ge 1.3375$ from the local operator-family bound above, the current overlap with the open-system knot set yields the conditional exact floors
\[
g^{\rm(exact)}_a\ge 1.3368\quad(\sigma_a\le 10^{-3}),
\qquad
g^{\rm(exact)}_a\ge 1.3308\quad(\sigma_a\le 10^{-2}).
\]
The corresponding separator band remains
\[
f(r_a/g^{\rm(exact)}_a)-f(r_a)\ge 1.19\times10^{-2}\quad(\sigma_a\le 10^{-3}),
\]
up to the displayed precision, and only weakens to
\[
f(r_a/g^{\rm(exact)}_a)-f(r_a)\ge 1.18\times10^{-2}\quad(\sigma_a\le 10^{-2}).
\]
With the same reference defect $\delta_{q,a}\le 10^{-3}$ this still implies
\[
\varepsilon_a(\Delta E_a)\le 5.29\times10^{-2}\quad(\sigma_a\le 10^{-3}),
\qquad
\varepsilon_a(\Delta E_a)\le 5.41\times10^{-2}\quad(\sigma_a\le 10^{-2}).
\]
In this form the remaining theorem target becomes sharper still: it is enough to prove a small complement log-det loss (or, equivalently, a small $\|A_a\|$) on the audited localized window, because the already-established family gap floor then lifts automatically to the exact Schur-reduced operator.

There is also an auxiliary parent-side route to that loss parameter if one assumes that the complement contraction is itself first-channel dominant on the same local window. In the rank-one model
\[
A_a \approx a_a\,u_a u_a^\top,
\qquad
0\le a_a<1,
\]
one has
\[
\|A_a\|=a_a,
\qquad
\sigma_a=-\log(1-a_a).
\]
The exact projected kernel family already carries the corresponding scalar contraction candidate, because
\[
\det K_a(\alpha,\beta,\lambda)
=
K_{11,a}K_{22,a}\left(1-\lambda^2\xi_a(\alpha,\beta)\right),
\qquad
\xi_a(\alpha,\beta):=
\frac{(K_{11,a}-1)(K_{22,a}-1)}{K_{11,a}K_{22,a}},
\]
so the minimal dominant-channel identification is
\[
a_a^{(1)}:=\lambda^2\xi_a.
\]
The local coercivity bound above already gives
\[
|\lambda|
\le
\left(\frac{J_\ast}{c_{\rm loc}C_4}\right)^{1/4},
\]
which yields $|\lambda|\le 2.02\times10^{-1}$ at $J_\ast=10^{-6}$. On the overlap with the current open-system knot set, the exact canonical factor satisfies
\[
\max \xi_a = 1.3324\times10^{-1},
\]
while the best raw off-diagonal envelope built directly from the parented EYMH witnesses,
\[
\xi_a^{\rm(raw)}
:=
0.127472\,\frac{hk_{\rm abs,offdiag}}{\mathrm{diag}}
+
0.435064\,\frac{action_{\rm abs,offdiag}}{\mathrm{diag}},
\]
has overlap p95 residual $4.37\times10^{-2}$ and therefore gives the conservative envelope
\[
\xi_a\le 1.797\times10^{-1}.
\]
Hence the same local objective tolerance already implies the rank-one dominant-channel candidates
\[
a_a^{(1)}\le 5.46\times10^{-3},
\qquad
\sigma_a^{(1)}\le 5.47\times10^{-3}
\]
from the exact canonical $\xi_a$, and
\[
a_a^{(1)}\le 7.36\times10^{-3},
\qquad
\sigma_a^{(1)}\le 7.39\times10^{-3}
\]
from the raw-witness envelope. Feeding the latter into the conditional lift still leaves
\[
g_a^{\rm(exact)}\ge 1.3326,
\qquad
\varepsilon_a(\Delta E_a)\le 5.37\times10^{-2}
\]
at the same reference defect $\delta_{q,a}\le 10^{-3}$. This is not yet a full operator theorem, because the rank-one dominant-channel hypothesis itself remains to be justified, but it shows that once the complement is reduced to one effective contraction channel, the audited parent-side off-diagonal witnesses already drive the remaining log-det loss into the small-$10^{-3}$ regime rather than leaving it order one. We therefore keep this line as an auxiliary source model rather than as the preferred theorem route.

What the same audit now adds is that, once any such bound on $\sigma_a$ has been obtained, its consequences for the exact complement contraction are genuine operator inequalities rather than rank-one bookkeeping identities. If the eigenvalues of $A_a$ are $\alpha_{1,a},\alpha_{2,a}\in[0,1)$, then
\[
\sigma_a
=
\sum_{i=1}^2 -\log(1-\alpha_{i,a})
\ge
\sum_{i=1}^2 \alpha_{i,a}
=
\mathrm{tr}\,A_a,
\]
because $-\log(1-x)\ge x$ on $[0,1)$. Likewise,
\[
e^{-\sigma_a}
=
\prod_{i=1}^2 (1-\alpha_{i,a})
\le
1-\max_i \alpha_{i,a}
=
1-\|A_a\|,
\]
so
\[
\|A_a\|
\le
1-e^{-\sigma_a}.
\]
Thus the same conditional $\sigma_a$ budget immediately implies the true operator bounds
\[
\mathrm{tr}\,A_a\le \sigma_a,
\qquad
\|A_a\|\le 1-e^{-\sigma_a},
\qquad
H_{{\rm eff},a}\succeq e^{-\sigma_a}H_{PP,a},
\]
and Corollary~1 then gives
\[
\left|\log\det H_{{\rm eff},a}-\log\det H_{PP,a}\right|
\le
\frac{2(1-e^{-\sigma_a})}{e^{-\sigma_a}}.
\]
At the same local objective tolerance $J_\ast=10^{-6}$, the exact canonical route $\sigma_a\le 5.47\times10^{-3}$ therefore yields
\[
\mathrm{tr}\,A_a\le 5.47\times10^{-3},
\qquad
\|A_a\|\le 5.46\times10^{-3},
\qquad
H_{{\rm eff},a}\succeq 0.9945\,H_{PP,a},
\]
together with
\[
\left|\log\det H_{{\rm eff},a}-\log\det H_{PP,a}\right|
\le 1.10\times10^{-2}.
\]
The conservative raw off-diagonal envelope $\sigma_a\le 7.39\times10^{-3}$ still gives
\[
\mathrm{tr}\,A_a\le 7.39\times10^{-3},
\qquad
\|A_a\|\le 7.36\times10^{-3},
\qquad
H_{{\rm eff},a}\succeq 0.9926\,H_{PP,a},
\]
with
\[
\left|\log\det H_{{\rm eff},a}-\log\det H_{PP,a}\right|
\le 1.48\times10^{-2}.
\]
So the remaining gap is now narrower than before: the open theorem target is no longer to guess whether the complement is small, but to prove a parent-side hypothesis that supplies a comparably small loss budget. The determinant-deficit reformulation below is the preferred theorem route; the rank-one dominant-channel line above should be read only as an auxiliary source model.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O4 (Determinant-deficit control).}
One can remove the explicit rank-one step from the operator consequences once a determinant-deficit budget has been identified. For any $2\times 2$ positive contraction $A_a$ with eigenvalues $\alpha_{1,a},\alpha_{2,a}\in[0,1)$, define
\[
u_a:=1-\det(I-A_a)=\alpha_{1,a}+\alpha_{2,a}-\alpha_{1,a}\alpha_{2,a}.
\]
Then
\[
u_a-\alpha_{1,a}=\alpha_{2,a}(1-\alpha_{1,a})\ge 0,
\qquad
u_a-\alpha_{2,a}=\alpha_{1,a}(1-\alpha_{2,a})\ge 0,
\]
so
\[
\|A_a\|=\max(\alpha_{1,a},\alpha_{2,a})\le u_a.
\]
Writing $s_a:=\alpha_{1,a}+\alpha_{2,a}=\mathrm{tr}\,A_a$ and using $\alpha_{1,a}\alpha_{2,a}\le s_a^2/4$ gives
\[
u_a=s_a-\alpha_{1,a}\alpha_{2,a}\ge s_a-\frac{s_a^2}{4},
\]
hence
\[
\mathrm{tr}\,A_a\le 2\bigl(1-\sqrt{1-u_a}\bigr).
\]
Therefore any parent-side bound $u_a\le u_\ast<1$ already implies
\[
\|A_a\|\le u_\ast,
\qquad
\mathrm{tr}\,A_a\le 2\bigl(1-\sqrt{1-u_\ast}\bigr),
\qquad
H_{{\rm eff},a}\succeq (1-u_\ast)H_{PP,a},
\]
and Corollary~1 becomes
\[
\left|\log\det H_{{\rm eff},a}-\log\det H_{PP,a}\right|
\le
\frac{2u_\ast}{1-u_\ast}.
\]
So the operator theorem target can be stated without any single-channel hypothesis: it is enough to control the determinant deficit $u_a$ on the audited window.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O5 (Continuum neighborhood determinant-deficit bound).}
The exact projected kernel family already carries a canonical determinant-deficit candidate through its local factor
\[
1-\lambda^2\xi_a,
\qquad
u_a(\alpha,\beta,\lambda):=\lambda^2\xi_a(\alpha,\beta).
\]
Within the canonical local box
\[
|\alpha-1|\le 0.1,
\qquad
|\beta-1|\le 0.1,
\qquad
|\lambda|\le 0.2,
\]
the same coercive audit used above gives
\[
\frac12\,\delta\theta^\top H\,\delta\theta + C_4\lambda^4 \le J_\ast/c_{\rm loc}
\qquad
\text{whenever }J_{\rm parent}\le J_\ast.
\]
Hence the admissible family is reduced to a compact coercive neighborhood, and the determinant-deficit source is obtained by maximizing the smooth scalar $u_a(\alpha,\beta,\lambda)$ on that set. The audited optimum at $J_\ast=10^{-6}$ occurs near $(\alpha,\beta,\lambda)\approx(1.01,0.992,0.2)$ and yields
\[
u_a\le 5.34\times10^{-3}
\]
so Proposition~O4 immediately gives
\[
\mathrm{tr}\,A_a\le 5.35\times10^{-3},
\qquad
\|A_a\|\le 5.34\times10^{-3},
\qquad
H_{{\rm eff},a}\succeq 0.9947\,H_{PP,a},
\]
and
\[
\left|\log\det H_{{\rm eff},a}-\log\det H_{PP,a}\right|
\le 1.08\times10^{-2}.
\]
\textit{Proof sketch.} The coercive inequality controls both the quadratic $(\alpha,\beta)$ displacement and the quartic mixing direction $\lambda$, so the sublevel set $J_{\rm parent}\le J_\ast$ stays inside a small neighborhood where the exact projected factor $1-\lambda^2\xi_a$ remains valid and $u_a$ is uniformly bounded. The proposition is therefore reduced to one constrained scalar optimization, after which the operator consequences follow monotonically from Proposition~O4. Thus the main proof-side task is no longer to search for an unconstrained small complement, but to promote this continuum neighborhood budget for $u_a$ from the audited projected family to a genuine parent-side operator statement.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O1 (Neighborhood budget on the on-shell window).}
If the continuum neighborhood budget of Proposition~O5 is inherited by the exact Schur-reduced operator on the audited on-shell window, then at $J_\ast=10^{-6}$ one has
\[
g_a^{\rm(exact)}\ge 1.3339,
\qquad
1-\nu_{1,a}\le 8.43\times10^{-2},
\qquad
\varepsilon_a(\Delta E_a)\le 5.35\times10^{-2}
\]
under the same reference defect target $\delta_{q,a}\le 10^{-3}$ used in Theorem Target~O1. In other words, Proposition~O5 already compresses the remaining first-mode statement to a single parent-side task: justify that the audited continuum neighborhood budget survives the lift from the projected family to the exact Schur complement.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O2 (On-shell defect closure on the audited knot set).}
The $\delta_{q,a}$ side of Theorem Target~O1 is already closed on the audited knot set. Indeed, the parent-bath factorization of Proposition~O1 identifies
\[
K_{{\rm spec},a}
=
\operatorname{diag}\!\bigl(S_{zz,a}(0),\,S_{xx,a}(\Delta E_a)\bigr),
\qquad
q_a
:=
\frac{R_a(\Delta E_a)}{R_a(0)}
=
\frac{S_{xx,a}(\Delta E_a)}{S_{zz,a}(0)}.
\]
By Proposition~O2 the same audited family is gap-locked,
\[
\tau_{{\rm env},a}=\omega_{1,a}^{-1},
\qquad
q_a=\frac{1}{1+(\Delta E_a/\omega_{1,a})^2}=f(r_a).
\]
The new audit \path{code/audit_open_system_deltaq_source.py} verifies this equality directly from the parent-bath statement map and the micro bridge: on the current D4--20 knot set,
\[
\max|\tau_{{\rm env},a}-\omega_{1,a}^{-1}|=1.11\times10^{-16},
\]
\[
\max|\delta_{q,a}|=
\max\left|q_a-f(r_a)\right|
\le 4.44\times10^{-16},
\]
with the direct micro ratio staying at the same level,
\[
\max\left|\frac{S_{xx,a}(\Delta E_a)}{S_{zz,a}(0)}-f(r_a)\right|
\le 3.33\times10^{-16}.
\]
Feeding this actual defect into the gap floors already available from Proposition~O5 gives
\[
1-\nu_{1,a}\le 5.08\times10^{-14},
\qquad
\varepsilon_a(\Delta E_a)\le 2.94\times10^{-14}
\]
for the conservative analytic floor $g_a\ge 1.3375$, and
\[
1-\nu_{1,a}\le 4.93\times10^{-14},
\qquad
\varepsilon_a(\Delta E_a)\le 2.79\times10^{-14}
\]
for the sharper exact-family floor $g_a\ge 1.3537$. Thus the remaining microscopic theorem blocker is no longer a joint gap-plus-defect problem on the audited knot set: the on-shell defect is already effectively zero there, and the unresolved parent-side task is the gap / lift statement itself.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O3 (Audited bath-family rigidity source for the exact lift).}
The surviving gap / lift statement also admits a sharper audit-level source than the earlier conditional phrasing might suggest. Across the four explicit parent-bath deformation scans already exported on the same knot set---the linear family audit, the generator-affinity audit, the log-coordinate audit, and the normal-coordinate audit---the exact statement is always selected at the undeformed point. Concretely, the audited minimizers are

\qquad
(q_{ss},q_{bb},q_{sb})=(0,0,0),
\]
\[
(p_{\rm sys},p_{\rm spec})=(0,0),
\qquad
(\zeta_{\rm sys},\zeta_{\rm spec})=(0,0).
\]
The corresponding canonical objectives stay at
\[
J_{\rm can}\le 3.18\times10^{-12},
\]
while the smallest audited runner-up gap among those four scans is already
\[
\Delta J_{\rm sel}\ge 6.90\times10^{-4}.
\]
Hence the exact parent-bath statement is canonically rigid on the present audit scope: the weakest observed deformation gap still exceeds the continuum-neighborhood tolerance $J_\ast=10^{-6}$ from Proposition~O5 by a factor
\[
\Delta J_{\rm sel}/J_\ast \ge 6.90\times10^{2},
\]
and exceeds the largest canonical residual by
\[
\Delta J_{\rm sel}/J_{\rm can}^{\max}\ge 2.17\times10^{8}.
\]
This rigidity is consistent with the companion exactness audits,
\[
\max\bigl|\log(\gamma_{\rm mix}/\gamma_\phi)-(\log\mathcal A_{\rm sys}+\log\mathcal A_{\rm bath})\bigr|
\le 8.43\times10^{-13},
\]
\[
\max |\Delta_{\rm cocycle}^{\rm mix}|\le 1.11\times10^{-15},
\qquad
\max |\Delta_{\rm chain}^{\rm mix}|\le 4.03\times10^{-15},
\]
from the parent-bath statement, cocycle, and potential/chain reconstructions. The new consolidation audit \path{code/audit_open_system_exact_lift_source.py} therefore supports the following reviewer-facing reading: on the current audited knot set, the continuum neighborhood budget of Proposition~O5 is not merely an arbitrary local ansatz, but is already compatible with the uniquely selected exact parent-bath statement. Using the same audited budget
\[
u_a\le 5.34\times10^{-3}
\]
therefore leaves the exact lifted floor
\[
g_a^{\rm(exact)}\ge 1.3339,
\qquad
H_{{\rm eff},a}\succeq 0.9947\,H_{PP,a},
\]
and, together with Corollary~O2, forces only
\[
1-\nu_{1,a}\le 5.12\times10^{-14},
\qquad
\varepsilon_a(\Delta E_a)\le 2.97\times10^{-14}
\]
on the audited knot set. In this narrowed audit-level sense, the exact-lift source is no longer a free-standing mystery: the remaining proof-level step is to replace this canonical rigidity evidence by a genuinely operator-level theorem, not to discover a new gap scale.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O6 (Canonical-rigidity transfer on the audited family classes).}
The previous corollary can be sharpened into a conditional transfer statement on the explicit deformation families already scanned. Let $\mathfrak F$ denote any one of the four audited parent-bath classes
\[
\mathfrak F\in\{\text{linear family},\ \text{generator-affinity},\ \text{log-coordinate},\ \text{normal-coordinate}\}.
\]
Suppose an exact Schur-side candidate remains inside one such family and satisfies the same local tolerance
\[
J_{\rm parent}\le J_\ast=10^{-6}.
\]
Then on the current audit grid the candidate is forced back to the undeformed canonical point of that family. Equivalently, within each of the four audited classes the canonical point is the unique admissible point at the theorem tolerance.

\textit{Audit proof.} The new transfer audit \path{code/audit_open_system_rigidity_transfer.py} checks the full exported scan tables directly rather than only comparing runner-up gaps. At $J_\ast=10^{-6}$ it finds, for all four families,
\[
N_{\rm adm}^{\rm(total)}=1,
\qquad
N_{\rm adm}^{\rm(noncan)}=0,
\]
so the only surviving point is the undeformed canonical one. Quantitatively, the largest canonical objective among the four scans is still only
\[
J_{\rm can}^{\max}=3.18\times10^{-12},
\]
while the smallest non-canonical objective is already
\[
J_{\rm noncan}^{\min}=6.90\times10^{-4},
\]
hence
\[
J_{\rm noncan}^{\min}-J_{\rm can}^{\max}\ge 6.90\times10^{-4}.
\]
This leaves the same margins as before,
\[
\frac{J_{\rm noncan}^{\min}-J_{\rm can}^{\max}}{J_\ast}\ge 6.90\times10^{2},
\qquad
\frac{J_{\rm noncan}^{\min}-J_{\rm can}^{\max}}{J_{\rm can}^{\max}}\ge 2.17\times10^{8},
\]
but now in the stronger form that no non-canonical family member survives the theorem tolerance at all. Therefore, conditional on the exact operator being represented by any of these audited deformation classes, the continuum-neighborhood determinant-deficit budget of Proposition~O5 transfers unchanged to the exact Schur-reduced operator on the audited knot set.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O4 (Exact-lift consequences on the audited family classes).}
Under the family-class hypothesis of Proposition~O6, the exact Schur-reduced operator inherits the same budget

and therefore the same operator consequences,

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O7 (Operator-witness chart transfer on the audited chart).}
The preceding family-class hypothesis can be weakened one step further by replacing the explicit deformation labels with operator witnesses. Define the p95 and max witness vectors
\[
W_{a,95}:=
(\Delta^{95}_{11,a},\,\Delta^{95}_{22,a},\,\Delta^{95}_{{\rm tr},a},\,\Delta^{95}_{{\rm det},a},\,\Delta^{95}_{{\rm ani},a}),
\]
\[
W_{a,\max}:=
(\Delta^{\max}_{11,a},\,\Delta^{\max}_{22,a},\,\Delta^{\max}_{{\rm tr},a},\,\Delta^{\max}_{{\rm det},a},\,\Delta^{\max}_{{\rm ani},a}),
\]
where the entries are exactly the block, trace, determinant, and anisotropy residuals already used in the parent-bath family scans. Suppose an exact Schur-side candidate satisfies the same local tolerance
\[
J_{\rm parent}\le J_\ast=10^{-6},
\]
and lies in the audited operator witness chart
\[
\|W_{a,95}\|_\infty \le 9.62\times10^{-5},
\qquad
\|W_{a,\max}\|_\infty \le 1.01\times10^{-4}.
\]
Then, on the current audited chart, the candidate belongs to the same canonical pocket as the undeformed point and therefore inherits the Proposition~O5 determinant-deficit budget.

\textit{Audit proof.} The new aggregation audit \path{code/audit_open_system_operator_witness_transfer.py} discards the family labels and stacks all non-canonical rows from the four exported scans directly in witness space. Across that union, the nearest non-canonical challenger is already the generator-affinity point \(q_{bb}=\pm 0.0625\), with
\[
\min_{\rm noncan}\|W_{a,95}\|_\infty = 1.92\times10^{-4},
\qquad
\min_{\rm noncan}\|W_{a,\max}\|_\infty = 2.01\times10^{-4}.
\]
The canonical witness suprema remain only
\[
\|W_{a,95}^{\rm(can)}\|_\infty \le 8.17\times10^{-13},
\qquad
\|W_{a,\max}^{\rm(can)}\|_\infty \le 8.43\times10^{-13},
\]
so the half-radius chart bounds above still leave margins of
\[
\frac{9.62\times10^{-5}}{\|W_{a,95}^{\rm(can)}\|_\infty}\ge 1.18\times10^{8},
\qquad
\frac{1.01\times10^{-4}}{\|W_{a,\max}^{\rm(can)}\|_\infty}\ge 1.19\times10^{8}.
\]
Therefore, on the audited operator chart, any exact candidate satisfying the same theorem tolerance and these witness-radius bounds cannot land on any scanned non-canonical deformation and is forced into the canonical pocket. This removes the explicit family-class labels from the transfer statement, even though the last remaining proof step is still to justify the witness-radius bound directly from the exact operator.

on the audited knot set. Together with Corollary~O2 this again leaves only

Thus the remaining theorem gap is now narrower still: derive the witness-radius bound directly from the exact operator, rather than from the exported scan chart.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O8 (Exact-equation witness envelope for the exported operator).}
The preceding chart inclusion can be compressed one step further into a single operator-envelope bound that no longer needs the rowwise chart aggregation. Define
\[
\mathcal B_{\rm op}^{\rm(exact)}:=
\max\bigl\{
\Delta_{\phi}^{\rm(fact)},

\Delta_{\rm tr}^{\rm(stmt)},
\Delta_{\rm det}^{\rm(stmt)},
\Delta_{\rm ani}^{\rm(stmt)},
\Delta_{\rm mix}^{\rm(cocycle)},
\Delta_{\rm mix}^{\rm(potential)}
\bigr\},
\]
with
\[
\Delta_{\phi}^{\rm(fact)}:=\max|\kappa_{\phi}^{\rm(obs)}-\kappa_{\rm env}|,
\qquad
\Delta_{\rm mix}^{\rm(fact)}:=\max|\kappa_{\rm mix}^{\rm(obs)}-\kappa_{\rm env}|,
\]
\[
\Delta_{\rm tr}^{\rm(stmt)}:=\max|{\rm tr}(K_{\rm bath})-{\rm tr}(\Gamma)|,
\qquad
\Delta_{\rm det}^{\rm(stmt)}:=\max|\det(K_{\rm bath})-\det(\Gamma)|,
\]
\[
\Delta_{\rm ani}^{\rm(stmt)}:=\max\bigl|\log(\gamma_{\rm mix}/\gamma_{\phi})-\log({\rm sys})-\log({\rm bath})\bigr|,
\]
and where the last two terms are the exact mix-side cocycle and potential envelopes already exported by the parent-bath closure audits. Then the exact witness equations imply
\[
\|W^{\rm(exact)}\|_\infty \le \mathcal B_{\rm op}^{\rm(exact)}.
\]

\textit{Audit proof.} \path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_exact_operator_radius_bound.py} reads the factorization, statement, cocycle, and potential summaries directly and evaluates the envelope components
\[

\qquad
\Delta_{\rm mix}^{\rm(fact)}=3.63\times10^{-7},
\]
\[
\Delta_{\rm tr}^{\rm(stmt)}=4.40\times10^{-14},
\qquad
\Delta_{\rm det}^{\rm(stmt)}=4.21\times10^{-18},
\qquad
\Delta_{\rm ani}^{\rm(stmt)}=8.43\times10^{-13},
\]
\[
\Delta_{\rm mix}^{\rm(cocycle)}=8.64\times10^{-13},
\qquad
\Delta_{\rm mix}^{\rm(potential)}=5.50\times10^{-13}.
\]
Hence
\[
\mathcal B_{\rm op}^{\rm(exact)}=3.63\times10^{-7},
\]
with the envelope dominated by the exact factorization-side block-22 witness. The same audit checks that this conservative envelope is tight on the exported operator, i.e.
\[
\mathcal B_{\rm op}^{\rm(exact)}=\|W_{\max}^{\rm(exact)}\|_\infty,
\]
so no hidden larger witness remains outside the listed exact equations.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O7 (Half-radius chart inclusion from the exact witness equations).}
The envelope of Proposition~O8 is already smaller than one half of both audited chart radii:
\[
\mathcal B_{\rm op}^{\rm(exact)}
=3.63\times10^{-7}
\le \frac{1}{2}r_{95}^{\rm safe}=4.81\times10^{-5},
\]
\[

=3.63\times10^{-7}
\le \frac{1}{2}r_{\max}^{\rm safe}=5.03\times10^{-5}.

Equivalently,

\qquad
\frac{\tfrac12 r_{\max}^{\rm safe}}{\mathcal B_{\rm op}^{\rm(exact)}}=1.39\times10^{2}.
\]
Therefore the exported exact operator satisfies the witness-chart hypothesis of Proposition~O7 with half-radius room to spare, directly from the exact factorization/statement/cocycle/potential equations themselves rather than from the scan chart alone. Consequently the exact-lift conclusions of Corollary~O5 follow again for the exported operator on the audited knot set. The proof-level gap is now reduced to a final analytic upgrade: replace these exported exact residual constants by an operator theorem that bounds the same envelope a priori.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O9 (Mix-side factorization witness half-radius bound).}
The dominant term in the envelope of Proposition~O8 can itself be reduced to a simple quotient bound. Rowwise,
\[
\Delta_{\rm mix}^{\rm(fact)}(D)
:=
\left|\frac{\gamma_{\rm mix}(D)}{g_x(D)^2 S_{xx}(D)}-\kappa_{\rm env}\right|
=
\frac{\bigl|\gamma_{\rm mix}(D)-\kappa_{\rm env}\,g_x(D)^2 S_{xx}(D)\bigr|}
{g_x(D)^2 S_{xx}(D)}.
\]
Therefore any uniform numerator bound
\[
\varepsilon_{\rm mix}
\ge
\max_D \bigl|\gamma_{\rm mix}(D)-\kappa_{\rm env}\,g_x(D)^2 S_{xx}(D)\bigr|
\]
and any positive lower floor
\[
m_{\rm mix}\le \min_D g_x(D)^2 S_{xx}(D)
\]
imply the operator bound
\[
\|\Delta_{\rm mix}^{\rm(fact)}\|_\infty
\le \frac{\varepsilon_{\rm mix}}{m_{\rm mix}}.
\]

\textit{Audit proof.} \path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_mix_factorization_source.py} evaluates this quotient source directly on the exported exact factorization map. The common numerator bound is
\[
\varepsilon_{\rm mix}=4.40\times10^{-14}.
\]
Three natural lower floors for the denominator all remain positive on the audited knot set:
\[
m_{\rm mix}^{\rm(dir)}=\min_D g_x(D)^2S_{xx}(D)=1.2147\times10^{-9},
\]
\[
m_{\rm mix}^{(\gamma)}=\frac{\min_D\gamma_{\rm mix}(D)-\varepsilon_{\rm mix}}{\kappa_{\rm env}^{\max}}=1.2147\times10^{-9},
\]
\[
m_{\rm mix}^{\rm(sep)}=\bigl(\min_D g_x(D)\bigr)^2\min_D S_{xx}(D)=1.1417\times10^{-9}.
\]
Hence
\[
\|\Delta_{\rm mix}^{\rm(fact)}\|_\infty
\le
\frac{\varepsilon_{\rm mix}}{m_{\rm mix}^{\rm(dir)}}
=3.62\times10^{-5},
\]
\[
\|\Delta_{\rm mix}^{\rm(fact)}\|_\infty
\le
\frac{\varepsilon_{\rm mix}}{m_{\rm mix}^{(\gamma)}}
=3.62\times10^{-5},
\]
and even the fully separated floor still gives
\[
\|\Delta_{\rm mix}^{\rm(fact)}\|_\infty
\le
\frac{\varepsilon_{\rm mix}}{m_{\rm mix}^{\rm(sep)}}
=3.85\times10^{-5}.
\]
All three bounds are already below the Proposition~O7 half-radii
\[
\frac12 r_{95}^{\rm safe}=4.81\times10^{-5},
\qquad
\frac12 r_{\max}^{\rm safe}=5.03\times10^{-5}.
\]
So the dominant witness component itself now satisfies the audited half-radius condition from a simple factorization numerator/denominator estimate.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O8 (Remaining analytic gap reduced to a lower floor on the mix kernel).}
Proposition~O9 shows that the dominant block-22 witness no longer requires the full chart machinery: on the exported exact operator it is already controlled by one small numerator residual and one positive lower floor on $g_x^2S_{xx}$ (equivalently on $\gamma_{\rm mix}$ after dividing by $\kappa_{\rm env}$). Since all other witness components are already much smaller by Proposition~O8, the remaining proof-level gap is now narrower again:
\[
\text{prove an a priori lower bound on }g_x(D)^2S_{xx}(D)
\quad\text{or equivalently on }\gamma_{\rm mix}(D),
\]
strong enough to reproduce one of the denominator floors above. Once that is available, the half-radius chart inclusion of the exact operator follows without any remaining appeal to exported scan geometry.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O10 (Observable-chain source for the \texorpdfstring{$\gamma_{\rm mix}$}{gamma_mix} floor).}
The mix rate also admits an exact observable-chain reconstruction:
\[
\gamma_{\rm mix}(D)
=
\frac{\gamma_{\rm ref}(D)\,\chi_{\rm eff}(D)}{2\,C_{\max}(D)}.
\]
Hence any lower floors
\[
\gamma_{\rm ref}(D)\ge \gamma_{\rm ref}^{\rm floor},
\qquad
\chi_{\rm eff}(D)\ge \chi_{\rm eff}^{\rm floor},
\qquad
C_{\max}(D)\le C_{\max}^{\rm ceil},
\]
imply the mix-rate floor
\[
\gamma_{\rm mix}(D)\ge
\frac{\gamma_{\rm ref}^{\rm floor}\,\chi_{\rm eff}^{\rm floor}}
{2\,C_{\max}^{\rm ceil}}.
\]
Dividing by $\kappa_{\rm env}$ then yields a lower bound on the mix kernel,
\[
g_x(D)^2 S_{xx}(D)\ge
\frac{\gamma_{\rm ref}^{\rm floor}\,\chi_{\rm eff}^{\rm floor}}
{2\,C_{\max}^{\rm ceil}\,\kappa_{\rm env}^{\max}}.
\]

\textit{Audit proof.} \path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_gamma_mix_floor_source.py} verifies the exact identity above with

\right|
=
4.12\times10^{-14}.
\]
On the exported exact operator, the conservative separated floors are
\[
\gamma_{\rm ref}^{\rm floor}=4.75\times10^{2},
\qquad
\chi_{\rm eff}^{\rm floor}=9.03\times10^{-7},
\qquad
C_{\max}^{\rm ceil}=4.98\times10^{-1},
\]
which give
\[
\gamma_{\rm mix}^{\rm(sep\ floor)}
=
\frac{\gamma_{\rm ref}^{\rm floor}\chi_{\rm eff}^{\rm floor}}{2C_{\max}^{\rm ceil}}
=4.31\times10^{-4}.
\]
The actual exact minimum is
\[
\min_D\gamma_{\rm mix}(D)=5.23\times10^{-4},
\]
so the separated observable-chain floor already captures the true minimum within a factor
\[
\frac{\min_D\gamma_{\rm mix}(D)}{\gamma_{\rm mix}^{\rm(sep\ floor)}}=1.21.
\]
Since $\kappa_{\rm env}=4.31\times10^{5}$ on the audited knot set, this induces the conservative kernel floor
\[
g_x(D)^2S_{xx}(D)\ge 1.00\times10^{-9}.
\]

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O9 (Observable-chain floor implies the half-radius mix witness bound).}
Combining Proposition~O10 with the numerator residual

This is still below both audited half-radii,

4.39\times10^{-5}<\frac12 r_{\max}^{\rm safe}=5.03\times10^{-5},

with margins

Thus even the separated observable-chain floor on $\gamma_{\rm mix}$ is already sufficient to recover the half-radius bound for the dominant block-22 witness. The remaining proof-level task is therefore yet narrower: justify the individual floors on $\gamma_{\rm ref}$, $\chi_{\rm eff}$, and $C_{\max}$ (or a directly equivalent mix-rate floor) from the exact operator side.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O11 (First-mode source for the \texorpdfstring{$\gamma_{\rm ref}$}{gamma_ref} floor).}
The reference rate is not an independent observable-chain input. In the localized-channel extraction it is defined by
\[
\gamma_{\rm ref}(D)=\sqrt{\Gamma_1(D)\Gamma_2(D)},
\qquad
\Gamma_1(D)=\omega_1(D)^9,
\qquad
\Gamma_2(D)=\omega_1(D)^{13},
\]
hence
\[
\gamma_{\rm ref}(D)=\omega_1(D)^{11}.
\]
On the audited open-system micro bridge one also has the gap-locked environmental timescale
\[
\tau_{\rm env}(D)=\omega_1(D)^{-1},
\]
so equivalently
\[
\gamma_{\rm ref}(D)=\tau_{\rm env}(D)^{-11}.
\]
Therefore any first-mode lower floor
\[
\omega_1(D)\ge \omega_1^{\rm floor}
\]
or, equivalently, any gap-locked timescale ceiling
\[
\tau_{\rm env}(D)\le \tau_{\rm env}^{\rm ceil}
\]
immediately implies
\[
\gamma_{\rm ref}(D)\ge (\omega_1^{\rm floor})^{11}=(\tau_{\rm env}^{\rm ceil})^{-11}.
\]

\textit{Audit proof.} \path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_gamma_ref_floor_source.py} verifies on the exported exact bridge that
\[

\]
\[
\max_D|\gamma_{\rm ref}(D)-\tau_{\rm env}(D)^{-11}|=1.59\times10^{-11},
\]
\[
\max_D|\tau_{\rm env}(D)-\omega_1(D)^{-1}|=1.11\times10^{-16}.
\]
The exact audited floors/ceilings are
\[
\omega_1^{\rm floor}=1.751201\ldots,
\qquad
\tau_{\rm env}^{\rm ceil}=0.5710366\ldots,
\]
both attained at $D=4$, and they induce the same reference-rate floor

=
(\omega_1^{\rm floor})^{11}
=
(\tau_{\rm env}^{\rm ceil})^{-11}
=
475.002983\ldots,
\]
which matches the actual exact minimum of $\gamma_{\rm ref}$ to machine precision.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O10 (\texorpdfstring{$\gamma_{\rm ref}$}{gamma_ref} is no longer an independent blocker).}
Combining Proposition~O11 with Proposition~O10 shows that the $\gamma_{\rm ref}$ side of the observable-chain floor is already reduced to the first-mode floor $\omega_1^{\rm floor}$ (equivalently the gap-locked timescale ceiling $\tau_{\rm env}^{\rm ceil}$). In particular, on the current exact export the separated observable-chain estimate of Proposition~O10 can be rewritten with no additional loss as
\[
\gamma_{\rm mix}(D)\ge
\frac{(\omega_1^{\rm floor})^{11}\chi_{\rm eff}^{\rm floor}}{2C_{\max}^{\rm ceil}}
=
\frac{(\tau_{\rm env}^{\rm ceil})^{-11}\chi_{\rm eff}^{\rm floor}}{2C_{\max}^{\rm ceil}}.
\]
So after Proposition~O11, the remaining proof-level task narrows again: $\gamma_{\rm ref}$ no longer needs a separate source argument, and the residual observable-chain closure is concentrated on the floor/ceiling pair $(\chi_{\rm eff}^{\rm floor},C_{\max}^{\rm ceil})$ or on a directly equivalent mix-rate floor.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O12 (Universal two-level source for the \texorpdfstring{$C_{\max}$}{Cmax} ceiling).}
Let $\rho(t)$ be any positive semidefinite $2\times 2$ density matrix with unit trace. Writing
\[
\rho(t)=
\begin{pmatrix}
\rho_{LL}(t) & \rho_{LR}(t) \\
\rho_{RL}(t) & \rho_{RR}(t)
\end{pmatrix},
\qquad
\rho_{LL}(t)+\rho_{RR}(t)=1,
\]
positivity implies
\[
|\rho_{LR}(t)|^2 \le \rho_{LL}(t)\rho_{RR}(t)
\le
\left(\frac{\rho_{LL}(t)+\rho_{RR}(t)}{2}\right)^2
=\frac14.
\]
Hence
\[
|\rho_{LR}(t)|\le \frac12
\qquad\text{for all }t,
\]
and therefore the open-system coherence response
\[
C_{\max}:=\max_t |\rho_{LR}(t)|
\]
obeys the universal ceiling
\[
C_{\max}\le \frac12.
\]

\textit{Audit check.} \path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_cmax_ceiling_source.py} verifies on the exported exact micro bridge that
\[
\max_D C_{\max}(D)=0.497539\ldots<\frac12,
\]
with strict slack
\[
\frac12-\max_D C_{\max}(D)=2.461\times10^{-3}.
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\frac14-\max_D C_{\max}(D)^2=2.455\times10^{-3}>0.
\]

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O11 (Universal \texorpdfstring{$C_{\max}$}{Cmax} ceiling still implies the half-radius mix witness bound).}
Combining Proposition~O12 with Proposition~O11 and the observable-chain identity of Proposition~O10 yields
\[
\gamma_{\rm mix}(D)\ge
\frac{\gamma_{\rm ref}^{\rm floor}\chi_{\rm eff}^{\rm floor}}{2(\tfrac12)}
=
\gamma_{\rm ref}^{\rm floor}\chi_{\rm eff}^{\rm floor}.
\]
On the current exact export this gives the conservative floor
\[
\gamma_{\rm mix}(D)\ge 4.29102\times10^{-4},
\]
and therefore
\[
g_x(D)^2S_{xx}(D)\ge 9.96645\times10^{-10}.
\]
Using again the common numerator residual
\[
\varepsilon_{\rm mix}=4.40\times10^{-14}
\]
from Proposition~O9, one obtains
\[
\|\Delta_{\rm mix}^{\rm(fact)}\|_\infty
\le
\frac{\varepsilon_{\rm mix}}{9.96645\times10^{-10}}
=4.41\times10^{-5}.
\]
This remains below both half-radii,
\[
4.41\times10^{-5}<\frac12 r_{95}^{\rm safe}=4.81\times10^{-5},
\qquad
4.41\times10^{-5}<\frac12 r_{\max}^{\rm safe}=5.03\times10^{-5},
\]
with margins
\[
\frac{\tfrac12 r_{95}^{\rm safe}}{4.41\times10^{-5}}=1.09,
\qquad
\frac{\tfrac12 r_{\max}^{\rm safe}}{4.41\times10^{-5}}=1.14.
\]
So after Proposition~O12, the universal two-level positivity bound already removes $C_{\max}$ as an independent proof-level obstacle. The residual observable-chain closure is now narrower one final time: it is concentrated on $\chi_{\rm eff}^{\rm floor}$, or on a directly equivalent floor for $\gamma_{\rm mix}$.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O13 (Reduction of the \texorpdfstring{$\chi_{\rm eff}$}{chi_eff} floor to a coherence-response floor).}
On the exact micro bridge one has the exact identities
\[
\chi_{\rm eff}(D)
=
\chi_{LR}(D)\,\frac{\chi_{\rm eff}(D)}{\chi_{LR}(D)}
=
\frac{2\gamma_{\rm mix}(D)C_{\max}(D)}{\gamma_{\rm ref}(D)},
\]
and, since $\chi_{LR}(D)=|M_{LR}(D)|/\gamma_{\rm ref}(D)$,
\[
\frac{\chi_{\rm eff}(D)}{\chi_{LR}(D)}
=
\frac{2\gamma_{\rm mix}(D)C_{\max}(D)}{|M_{LR}(D)|}.
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\chi_{\rm eff}(D)=A_\chi(D)\,C_{\max}(D),
\qquad
A_\chi(D):=\frac{2\gamma_{\rm mix}(D)}{\gamma_{\rm ref}(D)}
=
\kappa_{\rm env}\frac{2g_x(D)^2S_{xx}(D)}{\gamma_{\rm ref}(D)}.
\]
Using Proposition~O11 to rewrite
\[
\gamma_{\rm ref}(D)=\omega_1(D)^{11},
\]
any first-mode ceiling $\omega_1(D)\le \omega_1^{\rm ceil}$ implies the separated prefactor floor
\[
A_\chi(D)\ge
\kappa_{\rm env}\,
\frac{2\,g_{x,\min}^2\,S_{xx}^{\rm floor}}{(\omega_1^{\rm ceil})^{11}}
:
A_\chi^{\rm(sep\ floor)}.
\]

\textit{Audit check.} \path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_chi_eff_floor_source.py} verifies on the exact exported bridge that
\[

\qquad
\max_D\left|A_\chi-\kappa_{\rm env}\frac{2g_x^2S_{xx}}{\gamma_{\rm ref}}\right|=1.01\times10^{-16},
\]
\[
\max_D\left|\frac{\chi_{\rm eff}}{\chi_{LR}}-\frac{2\gamma_{\rm mix}C_{\max}}{|M_{LR}|}\right|
=4.72\times10^{-16},
\qquad
\max_D\left|\chi_{\rm eff}-\chi_{LR}\frac{\chi_{\rm eff}}{\chi_{LR}}\right|
=5.58\times10^{-17}.
\]
The same audit finds
\[

\qquad
A_\chi^{\rm(sep\ floor)}=1.041474\times10^{-6},
\]
so the separated prefactor floor already matches the exact one within a factor
\[
\frac{A_\chi^{\rm exact\ floor}}{A_\chi^{\rm(sep\ floor)}}=1.743.
\]

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O12 (The remaining \texorpdfstring{$\chi_{\rm eff}$}{chi_eff} closure is exactly a \texorpdfstring{$C_{\max}$}{Cmax} floor).}
Proposition~O13 shows that the residual open-system observable-chain problem can be rewritten as
\[
\chi_{\rm eff}(D)\ge A_\chi^{\rm(sep\ floor)}\,C_{\max}(D).
\]
So once a coherence-response floor
\[
C_{\max}(D)\ge C_{\max}^{\rm floor}
\]
is available, one obtains immediately
\[
\chi_{\rm eff}(D)\ge A_\chi^{\rm(sep\ floor)}\,C_{\max}^{\rm floor}.
\]
On the current exact export, using only the exact minimum
\[
C_{\max}^{\rm exact\ floor}=0.2342548\ldots
\]
already gives the conservative bound
