\]
while the actual exported floor is
\[
\chi_{\rm eff}^{\rm exact\ floor}=9.03367\times10^{-7},
\]
so even this coarse separated reduction undershoots the exact floor by only
\[
\frac{\chi_{\rm eff}^{\rm exact\ floor}}
{A_\chi^{\rm(sep\ floor)}C_{\max}^{\rm exact\ floor}}
=3.70.
\]
Hence after Propositions~O11--O13 the residual observable-chain closure is now fully localized: the only scalar still missing from the exact export to a complete proof-side floor statement is a lower bound on $C_{\max}$ itself.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O14 (Underdamped closed form for \texorpdfstring{$C_{\max}$}{Cmax} on the exact two-level bridge).}
In the localized two-level Lindblad block used by the exact micro bridge,
\[
H=\frac{\Delta E}{2}\sigma_x,
\qquad
L_\phi=\sqrt{\gamma_\phi}\,\sigma_z,
\qquad
L_{\rm mix}=\sqrt{\gamma_{\rm mix}}\,\sigma_x,
\]
with initial state $\rho(0)=|L\rangle\langle L|$, the Bloch evolution closes on the $(y,z)$ plane:
\[
\dot y = -\Delta E\, z - 2(\gamma_\phi+\gamma_{\rm mix})y,
\qquad
\dot z = \Delta E\, y - 2\gamma_{\rm mix} z.
\]
Writing
\[
\Gamma := \gamma_\phi + 2\gamma_{\rm mix},
\qquad
\omega := \sqrt{\Delta E^2-\gamma_\phi^2},
\]
the underdamped regime $\Delta E>\gamma_\phi$ gives the exact solution
\[
y(t)=-\frac{\Delta E}{\omega}e^{-\Gamma t}\sin(\omega t),
\qquad
C(t)=|\rho_{LR}(t)|=\frac{|y(t)|}{2}
=
\frac{\Delta E}{2\omega}e^{-\Gamma t}|\sin(\omega t)|.
\]
The first-lobe maximum occurs at
\[
t_\ast = \omega^{-1}\arctan\!\left(\frac{\omega}{\Gamma}\right),
\]
so
\[
C_{\max}
=
\frac{\Delta E}{2\sqrt{\Gamma^2+\omega^2}}
\exp\!\left[
-\frac{\Gamma}{\omega}\arctan\!\left(\frac{\omega}{\Gamma}\right)
\right].
\]

\textit{Audit check.} \path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_cmax_floor_source.py} verifies on the exported exact bridge that all rows are underdamped,
\[
\min_D(\Delta E-\gamma_\phi)=2.59273\times10^{-1}>0,
\qquad
\max_D \frac{\gamma_\phi}{\Delta E}=2.07\times10^{-4},
\]
and that the closed form above matches the exported Lindblad maxima to within
\[
\max_D |C_{\max}^{\rm(export)}-C_{\max}^{\rm(closed)}|
=2.82\times10^{-4}.
\]
The closed-form minimum is attained at
\[
D=5,
\qquad
C_{\max}^{\rm(closed)\ floor}=2.34338\times10^{-1},
\]
while the exported exact minimum is
\[
C_{\max}^{\rm exact\ floor}=2.34255\times10^{-1},
\]
so
\[
\frac{C_{\max}^{\rm exact\ floor}}{C_{\max}^{\rm(closed)\ floor}}=0.999644.
\]

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O13 (Separated \texorpdfstring{$C_{\max}$}{Cmax} floor and resulting \texorpdfstring{$\chi_{\rm eff}$}{chi_eff} floor).}
On the audited exact parameter box, the same closed form is numerically monotone:
\[
\partial_{\Delta E} C_{\max}>0,
\qquad
\partial_{\gamma_\phi} C_{\max}<0,
\qquad
\partial_{\gamma_{\rm mix}} C_{\max}<0,
\]
with audited derivative margins
\[
\min \partial_{\Delta E} C_{\max}=3.16\times10^{-3},
\qquad
\max \partial_{\gamma_\phi} C_{\max}=-3.98\times10^{-1},
\qquad
\max \partial_{\gamma_{\rm mix}} C_{\max}=-7.96\times10^{-1}.
\]
Therefore the exact bridge already admits the separated floor
\[
C_{\max}(D)\ge
C_{\max}^{\rm(sep\ floor)}
=
\frac{\Delta E_{\min}}
{2\sqrt{\Gamma_{\max}^2+\omega_{\min}^2}}
\exp\!\left[
-\frac{\Gamma_{\max}}{\omega_{\min}}
\arctan\!\left(\frac{\omega_{\min}}{\Gamma_{\max}}\right)
\right],
\]
where
\[
\Delta E_{\min}=2.59278\times10^{-1},
\qquad
\gamma_{\phi,\max}=9.56\times10^{-5},
\qquad
\gamma_{{\rm mix},\max}=1.48999\times10^{-1},
\]
\[
\omega_{\min}=\sqrt{\Delta E_{\min}^2-\gamma_{\phi,\max}^2}=2.59278\times10^{-1},
\qquad
\Gamma_{\max}=\gamma_{\phi,\max}+2\gamma_{{\rm mix},\max}=2.98093\times10^{-1},
\]
giving
\[
C_{\max}^{\rm(sep\ floor)}=1.44083\times10^{-1}.
\]
The exact exported floor exceeds this by
\[
\frac{C_{\max}^{\rm exact\ floor}}{C_{\max}^{\rm(sep\ floor)}}=1.626.
\]
Combining this with Proposition~O13 gives the conservative observable floor
\[
\chi_{\rm eff}(D)\ge
A_\chi^{\rm(sep\ floor)} C_{\max}^{\rm(sep\ floor)}
=1.50059\times10^{-7},
\]
while the actual exported exact floor is
\[
\chi_{\rm eff}^{\rm exact\ floor}=9.03367\times10^{-7},
\]
so
\[
\frac{\chi_{\rm eff}^{\rm exact\ floor}}
{A_\chi^{\rm(sep\ floor)} C_{\max}^{\rm(sep\ floor)}}
=6.02.
\]
Thus after Propositions~O13--O14 the observable-chain side is effectively closed on the current exact bridge: the remaining gap is no longer the existence of a floor but only how much of the present slack one wants to recover analytically.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O15 (Ratio-box \texorpdfstring{$C_{\max}$}{Cmax} floor).}
The closed form of Proposition~O14 depends only on the two dimensionless ratios
\[
a:=\frac{\gamma_\phi}{\Delta E},
\qquad
b:=\frac{\gamma_{\rm mix}}{\Delta E}.
\]
Indeed
\[
C_{\max}=F(a,b),
\]
where
\[
F(a,b)
=
\frac{1}{2\sqrt{1+4b^2+4ab}}
\exp\!\left[
-\frac{a+2b}{\sqrt{1-a^2}}
\arctan\!\left(\frac{\sqrt{1-a^2}}{a+2b}\right)
\right].
\]
This representation is scale-free: the dependence on $\Delta E$ enters only through the ratios $(a,b)$.
On the exported exact bridge, \path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_cmax_ratio_floor_source.py} verifies that $F$ is monotone decreasing on the audited ratio box
\[
0\le a\le a_{\max},
\qquad
0\le b\le b_{\max},
\]
with derivative margins
\[
\max \partial_a F=-2.44\times10^{-1}<0,
\qquad
\max \partial_b F=-4.88\times10^{-1}<0.
\]
Here
\[
a_{\max}=\max_D \frac{\gamma_\phi(D)}{\Delta E(D)}
=2.06749\times10^{-4},
\qquad
b_{\max}=\max_D \frac{\gamma_{\rm mix}(D)}{\Delta E(D)}
=2.93097\times10^{-1}.
\]
Therefore the closed-form ratio floor is
\[
C_{\max}^{\rm(ratio,closed)\ floor}=F(a_{\max},b_{\max})=2.34328\times10^{-1}.
\]
Subtracting the exported exact-vs-closed residual bound from Proposition~O14 gives the rigorous exact-bridge floor
\[
C_{\max}(D)\ge
C_{\max}^{\rm(ratio,rig)\ floor}
:=
C_{\max}^{\rm(ratio,closed)\ floor}
-\max_D |C_{\max}^{\rm(export)}-C_{\max}^{\rm(closed)}|
=2.34045\times10^{-1}.
\]

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O14 (Tightened \texorpdfstring{$\chi_{\rm eff}$}{chi_eff} floor from the ratio-box \texorpdfstring{$C_{\max}$}{Cmax} floor).}
Combining Proposition~O15 with Proposition~O13 yields

A_\chi^{\rm(sep\ floor)} C_{\max}^{\rm(ratio,rig)\ floor}
=2.43752\times10^{-7}.
\]
The exact exported floors are
\[

so
\[
\frac{C_{\max}^{\rm exact\ floor}}{C_{\max}^{\rm(ratio,rig)\ floor}}=1.000895,
\qquad
\frac{\chi_{\rm eff}^{\rm exact\ floor}}
{A_\chi^{\rm(sep\ floor)} C_{\max}^{\rm(ratio,rig)\ floor}}
=3.706.
\]
Thus the observable-chain side is not merely known to admit a floor: after Proposition~O15 the remaining gap is essentially only the slack in the amplitude factor $A_\chi^{\rm(sep\ floor)}$, not in the coherence-response floor itself.

On the exported exact bridge,

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O15 (Tightened observable floor from the aligned amplitude source).}
Combining Proposition~O16 with Corollary~O14 yields, on the exported exact bridge,

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O17 (Alignment of the \texorpdfstring{$\gamma_{\rm mix}$}{gamma_mix} floor with the \texorpdfstring{$x$}{x}-channel overlap floor on the exact bridge).}

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O16 (The remaining \texorpdfstring{$\gamma_{\rm mix}$}{gamma_mix} slack is not response-dominated).}
Proposition~O17 shows that the exact exported \texorpdfstring{$\gamma_{\rm mix}$}{gamma_mix} floor is already aligned with the $x$-channel overlap floor, and that replacing the local response factor by the global response minimum changes the floor by only
\[

Therefore the next proof-side target should not be read as an independent floor theorem for $S_{xx}$. \medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O18 (Parity-diagonal source for the \texorpdfstring{$g_x$}{gx} floor on the exact bridge).}
Let
\[
V_{\rm par}^{(\delta V)}(D)
=
\begin{pmatrix}
V_{11}(D) & V_{12}(D)\\
V_{12}(D) & V_{22}(D)
\end{pmatrix}
\]
be the parity-basis projection of the fluctuation operator $\delta V=V_{\rm eff}-\bar V(r)$ onto the lowest two parity modes, and let
\[
U_{LR}=\frac{1}{\sqrt 2}
\begin{pmatrix}
1&1\\
1&-1
\end{pmatrix}
\]
be the localized-basis rotation.  Then
\[
V_{\rm loc}^{(\delta V)}(D)
=
U_{LR}^{\top}V_{\rm par}^{(\delta V)}(D)U_{LR}
=
\begin{pmatrix}
\tfrac12(V_{11}+2V_{12}+V_{22}) & \tfrac12(V_{11}-V_{22})\\
\tfrac12(V_{11}-V_{22}) & \tfrac12(V_{11}-2V_{12}+V_{22})
\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Hence the localized-channel couplings are exact reparameterizations of the same projected fluctuation matrix:
\[
g_x(D)=\left|\bigl(V_{\rm loc}^{(\delta V)}(D)\bigr)_{LR}\right|
=
\frac12\left|V_{11}(D)-V_{22}(D)\right|,
\]
\[
g_z(D)=\frac12\left|\bigl(V_{\rm loc}^{(\delta V)}(D)\bigr)_{LL}-\bigl(V_{\rm loc}^{(\delta V)}(D)\bigr)_{RR}\right|
=
\left|V_{12}(D)\right|.
\]
\path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_gx_floor_source.py} verifies on the exported exact bridge that
\[
\max_D\left|g_x-\left|V_{LR}^{(\delta V)}\right|\right|=0,
\]
\[
\max_D\left|g_x-\frac12\left|V_{11}-V_{22}\right|\right|
=1.00\times10^{-16},
\qquad
\max_D\left|g_z-\left|V_{12}\right|\right|
=2.18\times10^{-18}.
\]
Moreover the minima of
\[
g_x(D),\qquad
\frac12\left|V_{11}(D)-V_{22}(D)\right|,\qquad
\gamma_{\rm mix}(D)
\]
all occur at the same audited knot
\[
D=11,
\]
where
\[
g_x^{\rm floor}=3.35398\times10^{-5},
\qquad
\left|V_{11}-V_{22}\right|_{\rm floor}=6.70795\times10^{-5}.
\]
Thus the remaining exact-bridge floor question for $g_x$ is already equivalent to a static parity-diagonal contrast floor for the projected fluctuation operator $\delta V$.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O17 (The remaining \texorpdfstring{$g_x$}{gx} floor gap is static rather than bath-side).}
Propositions~O17--O18 reduce the last open-system overlap-side floor statement to the chain
\[
\gamma_{\rm mix}^{\rm exact\ floor}
=
\kappa_{\rm env}\,(g_x^{\rm floor})^2\,S_{xx}(D_{g_x,\min}),
\qquad
g_x^{\rm floor}
=
\frac12\left|V_{11}-V_{22}\right|_{\rm floor}.
\]
Therefore the unresolved proof-side target is no longer naturally read as a bath-response theorem for $S_{xx}$ or as an additional localized-basis theorem for $V_{LR}^{(\delta V)}$.  On the current exact bridge it is enough to produce an a priori lower bound on the parity-diagonal contrast
\[
\left|V_{11}(D)-V_{22}(D)\right|
\]
for the projected fluctuation operator $\delta V$ over the audited knot window.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O19 (One-center pair plus mirror remainder decomposition for the parity contrast).}
Write the two-center shifted potential as
\[
U_2(\rho,z;D)=U_+(\rho,z;D)+U_-(\rho,z;D)+U_\times(\rho,z;D),
\]
where
\[
U_\pm
=
m_0^2\bigl((1+s_\pm)^2-1\bigr)
c_\xi\frac{\ell_\pm}{1+s_\pm},
\qquad
c_\xi:=1-6\xi,
\]
with
\[
s_\pm(\rho,z;D)=\frac{a}{\sqrt{\rho^2+(z\mp D/2)^2+\varepsilon^2}},
\qquad
\ell_\pm(\rho,z;D)=
-3a\varepsilon^2\bigl(\rho^2+(z\mp D/2)^2+\varepsilon^2\bigr)^{-5/2},
\]
and
\[
U_\times
=
2m_0^2 s_+ s_-
+
c_\xi\left[
\frac{\ell_++\ell_-}{1+s_++s_-}
-\frac{\ell_+}{1+s_+}
-\frac{\ell_-}{1+s_-}
\right].
\]
Let
\[
\delta V_j(\rho,z;D):=U_j(\rho,z;D)-\bar U_j(r;D),
\qquad
r=\sqrt{\rho^2+z^2},
\]
with the same origin-centered spherical-average convention as the exact bridge, so that
\[
\delta V=\delta V_+ + \delta V_- + \delta V_\times.
\]
For the exact contrast density
\[
\mathcal C(\rho,z;D):=
2\pi\rho\bigl(\psi_1(\rho,z;D)^2-\psi_2(\rho,z;D)^2\bigr),
\]
the parity-diagonal contrast decomposes exactly as
\[
V_{11}(D)-V_{22}(D)
=
\int \mathcal C\,\delta V_+
+
\int \mathcal C\,\delta V_-
+
\int \mathcal C\,\delta V_\times.
\]
On the symmetry-adapted continuum pair one has
\[
\int \mathcal C\,\delta V_+
=
\int \mathcal C\,\delta V_-,
\]
so the exact contrast may be read as a one-center pair plus a mirror/cross remainder:
\[
V_{11}(D)-V_{22}(D)=2I_{\rm self}(D)+I_\times(D).
\]
The audit \path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_parity_contrast_self_mirror_source.py} closes the additive split to machine precision,
\[
\max_D\|U_2-U_+-U_--U_\times\|_\infty
=8.88\times10^{-15},
\]
\[
\max_D\|\delta V-\delta V_+-\delta V_--\delta V_\times\|_\infty
=7.11\times10^{-15},
\]
and reconstructs the projected contrast with
\[
\max_D\left|(V_{11}-V_{22})-(I_++I_-+I_\times)\right|
=3.79\times10^{-18}.
\]
Across the full audited window \(D=4,\dots,20\), the one-center pair carries between
\[
97.74\%
\quad\text{and}\quad
99.833\%
\]
of the exact parity contrast in absolute value, while the mirror/cross remainder contributes only
\[
0.167\%
\quad\text{to}\quad
2.257\%.
\]
At the exact floor knot \(D=11\),
\[
V_{11}-V_{22}=6.70795\times10^{-5},
\]
\[
I_+ + I_- = 6.69675\times10^{-5},
\qquad
I_\times = 1.12022\times10^{-7},
\]
so the exact floor is already \(99.833\%\) saturated by the one-center pair, with the remainder reduced to a \(0.167\%\) correction.  The cross piece itself splits as
\[
I_\times=I_\times^{\rm mass}+I_\times^{\rm deriv},
\]
where at \(D=11\)
\[
I_\times^{\rm mass}=3.85528\times10^{-7},
\qquad
I_\times^{\rm deriv}=-2.73506\times10^{-7},
\]
showing that the small remainder comes from a partial cancellation inside the mirror term rather than from the failure of the one-center anchor.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O18 (The remaining \texorpdfstring{$|V_{11}-V_{22}|$}{|V11-V22|} gap is now a one-center floor problem with a small static remainder).}
Propositions~O18--O19 sharpen the last overlap-side target from a generic parity-contrast floor to a much narrower statement.  On the exact bridge,
\[
|V_{11}(D)-V_{22}(D)|
\]
is already overwhelmingly carried by the one-center pair \(2I_{\rm self}(D)\), while the mirror/cross remainder \(I_\times(D)\) keeps the same sign as the exact contrast and remains below \(2.26\%\) of it on every audited knot.  Therefore the next proof-side task should not be framed as a new bath-side or barrier-center theorem.  A theorem-level lower bound on the one-center pair \(2I_{\rm self}(D)\), together with a coarse control of the small static remainder \(I_\times(D)\), is enough to close the present exact-bridge gap for
\[
|V_{11}(D)-V_{22}(D)|.
\]

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O20 (Core-window lower bound for the one-center pair on the exact bridge).}
For \(R>0\), let
\[
B_\pm(R;D):=\left\{(\rho,z):\rho^2+(z\mp D/2)^2\le R^2\right\},
\qquad
B_{\rm core}(R;D):=B_+(R;D)\cup B_-(R;D),
\]
and define
\[
I_{\rm self,pair}(D):=I_+(D)+I_-(D),
\]
\[
I_{\rm self,core}^{(R)}(D)
:=
\int_{B_{\rm core}(R;D)}
\mathcal C(\rho,z;D)\,
\bigl(\delta V_+(\rho,z;D)+\delta V_-(\rho,z;D)\bigr)\,d\rho\,dz,
\]
\[
I_{\rm self,tail}^{(R)}(D)
:=
I_{\rm self,pair}(D)-I_{\rm self,core}^{(R)}(D).
\]
The audit \path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_parity_contrast_self_floor_source.py} scans
\[
R\in[1.20,1.40]
\]
in steps of \(0.01\) and identifies the optimal safe radius
\[
R_\ast=1.35,
\]
for which every audited knot \(D=4,\dots,20\) satisfies
\[
\operatorname{sgn} I_{\rm self,core}^{(R_\ast)}(D)
=
\operatorname{sgn} I_{\rm self,pair}(D),
\qquad
\operatorname{sgn} I_{\rm self,tail}^{(R_\ast)}(D)
=
-\operatorname{sgn} I_{\rm self,pair}(D).
\]
Hence
\[
|I_{\rm self,pair}(D)|
=
|I_{\rm self,core}^{(R_\ast)}(D)|
-|I_{\rm self,tail}^{(R_\ast)}(D)|.
\]
Moreover the same audit gives the uniform tail-to-core control
\[
|I_{\rm self,tail}^{(R_\ast)}(D)|
\le
\eta_\ast\,|I_{\rm self,core}^{(R_\ast)}(D)|,
\qquad
\eta_\ast=0.573731\ldots
\]
so that
\[
|I_{\rm self,pair}(D)|
\ge
\lambda_\ast\,|I_{\rm self,core}^{(R_\ast)}(D)|,
\qquad
\lambda_\ast:=1-\eta_\ast=0.426269\ldots
\]
on the full audited window. Since the core-window floor is
\[
\min_D |I_{\rm self,core}^{(R_\ast)}(D)|
=
1.33812\times10^{-4},
\]
this already yields the nontrivial lower bound
\[
|I_{\rm self,pair}(D)|
\ge
5.70400\times10^{-5}.
\]
The exact one-center-pair floor is
\[
\min_D |I_{\rm self,pair}(D)|
=
6.69675\times10^{-5}
\qquad\text{at }D=11,
\]
so the optimized core-window lower bound is only a factor
\[
1.174
\]
below the exact minimum.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O19 (The parity-contrast floor now follows from a one-center core floor plus a tiny same-sign remainder).}
Combine Proposition~O20 with Proposition~O19 and the sign information from the same decomposition audit. On the exact bridge one has
\[
\operatorname{sgn} I_\times(D)=\operatorname{sgn}(V_{11}(D)-V_{22}(D))
\]
for every audited knot, together with
\[
\max_D \frac{|I_\times(D)|}{|V_{11}(D)-V_{22}(D)|}=2.2568\%.

Therefore

|V_{11}(D)-V_{22}(D)|
\ge
|I_{\rm self,pair}(D)|
\ge
\lambda_\ast\,|I_{\rm self,core}^{(R_\ast)}(D)|
\ge
5.70400\times10^{-5}
\]
already on the exact bridge, without invoking any barrier-center WKB ansatz. Since
\[
\min_D |V_{11}(D)-V_{22}(D)|=6.70795\times10^{-5},
\]
the remaining proof-side gap has become very narrow: produce an a priori lower bound on the one-center core contribution \(I_{\rm self,core}^{(R_\ast)}\), and retain only a coarse signed control of the small mirror remainder \(I_\times\).

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O21 (Compact box-anchor lower bound for the one-center core on the exact bridge).}
Keep the same optimal core radius \(R_\ast=1.35\), and define the compact local box anchor
\[
B_{\rm box}(\rho_\#,z_\#;D)
:=
\left\{(\rho,z): \rho\le \rho_\#,\ |z\mp D/2|\le z_\#\right\}\cap B_{\rm core}(R_\ast;D),

with the canonical audited choice

Let

and

I_{\rm self,shell}^{(\rho_\#,z_\#)}(D)
:=
I_{\rm self,core}^{(R_\ast)}(D)-I_{\rm self,anchor}^{(\rho_\#,z_\#)}(D).

The audit \path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_parity_contrast_self_core_anchor_source.py} scans
\[
\rho_\#\in[0.55,0.85],\qquad z_\#\in[1.05,1.30],

=
\operatorname{sgn} I_{\rm self,core}^{(R_\ast)}(D),
\qquad
\operatorname{sgn} I_{\rm self,shell}^{(\rho_\#,z_\#)}(D)
=
\operatorname{sgn} I_{\rm self,core}^{(R_\ast)}(D)

for every audited knot \(D=4,\dots,20\). Therefore

|I_{\rm self,core}^{(R_\ast)}(D)|
\ge
|I_{\rm self,anchor}^{(\rho_\#,z_\#)}(D)|.

The corresponding compact-anchor floor is

=

whereas the exact core-window floor is

=

So the compact box anchor already saturates the exact core-window minimum to within a factor

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O20 (The one-center core floor is already carried by a compact local anchor).}

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O22 (The compact anchor is already controlled by a same-center static source plus a local mirror loss).}
Keep the canonical compact anchor
\[
B_{\rm anchor}(D):=B_{\rm box}(0.70,1.21;D).
\]
Split the anchor integral into its same-center and mirror pieces:
\[
I_{\rm self,anchor}(D)=I_{\rm diag,anchor}(D)+I_{\rm mir,anchor}(D),
\]
where
\[
I_{\rm diag,anchor}(D)
:=
\int_{B_+(D)\cap B_{\rm anchor}(D)}
\mathcal C(\rho,z;D)\,\delta V_+(\rho,z;D)\,d\rho\,dz
+
\int_{B_-(D)\cap B_{\rm anchor}(D)}
\mathcal C(\rho,z;D)\,\delta V_-(\rho,z;D)\,d\rho\,dz,
\]
\[
I_{\rm mir,anchor}(D)
:=
\int_{B_+(D)\cap B_{\rm anchor}(D)}
\mathcal C(\rho,z;D)\,\delta V_-(\rho,z;D)\,d\rho\,dz
+
\int_{B_-(D)\cap B_{\rm anchor}(D)}
\mathcal C(\rho,z;D)\,\delta V_+(\rho,z;D)\,d\rho\,dz.
\]
The audit \path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_parity_contrast_anchor_static_source.py} scans all same-center sub-boxes
\[
\rho_{\rm box}\in[0.40,0.70],\qquad
z_{\rm box}\in[0.80,1.21],
\]
in steps of \(0.01\), requiring the same-center box and the remaining same-center shell to keep the sign of \(I_{\rm self,anchor}\) on every audited knot \(D=4,\dots,20\).  Under this criterion the optimal box is the full canonical anchor itself:
\[
(\rho_{\rm box}^{\rm opt},z_{\rm box}^{\rm opt})=(0.69,1.21)\sim(0.70,1.21).
\]
Moreover, on the full audited window one has
\[
\operatorname{sgn} I_{\rm diag,anchor}(D)
=
\operatorname{sgn} I_{\rm self,anchor}(D),
\]
and a uniform local mirror ratio
\[
\mu_{\rm anc}
:=
\max_D \frac{|I_{\rm mir,anchor}(D)|}{|I_{\rm diag,anchor}(D)|}
=
0.415143\ldots<1.
\]
Therefore
\[
|I_{\rm self,anchor}(D)|
\ge
(1-\mu_{\rm anc})\,|I_{\rm diag,anchor}(D)|.
\]
The same-center static floor is
\[
\min_D |I_{\rm diag,anchor}(D)|=1.63757\times10^{-4}
\qquad\text{at }D=11,
\]
so the induced anchor lower bound is
\[
|I_{\rm self,anchor}(D)|
\ge
(1-\mu_{\rm anc})\,|I_{\rm diag,anchor}(D)|
\ge
9.57744\times10^{-5}.
\]
Compared against the exact compact-anchor floor
\[
\min_D |I_{\rm self,anchor}(D)|=1.32321\times10^{-4},
\]
this static-source bound is only a factor
\[
1.38159
\]
below the exact minimum.

The compact anchor itself is already obtained from that source by a local mirror correction with uniform ratio \(\mu_{\rm anc}<0.42\), while \(I_\times\) stays only a coarse same-sign remainder in the total parity contrast.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O23 (Strict same-center subboxes do not improve the static anchor floor).}
To test whether the proof target can be localized further, define a strict same-center subbox inside the canonical anchor by

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O22 (The proof target has stabilized at the full same-center anchor scale).}
Proposition~O23 shows that no genuinely smaller same-center box inside the canonical anchor improves the static-source floor on the audited bridge. The best strict local box already carries

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O24 (Oriented moment reduction of the same-center static anchor).}
Keep the canonical same-center static anchor \(I_{\rm diag,anchor}(D)\) and define the oriented anchor density
\[
q_{\rm diag}(\rho,z;D)
:=
\operatorname{sgn}\!\bigl(I_{\rm diag,anchor}(D)\bigr)\,
\mathcal C(\rho,z;D)\Bigl(
\delta V_+(\rho,z;D)\,\mathbf 1_{B_+(D)\cap B_{\rm anchor}(D)}
+
\delta V_-(\rho,z;D)\,\mathbf 1_{B_-(D)\cap B_{\rm anchor}(D)}
\Bigr).
\]
Let
\[
P_{\rm diag}(D):=\int [q_{\rm diag}(\rho,z;D)]_+\,d\rho\,dz,
\qquad
N_{\rm diag}(D):=\int [q_{\rm diag}(\rho,z;D)]_-\,d\rho\,dz,
\]
so that
\[
|I_{\rm diag,anchor}(D)|=P_{\rm diag}(D)-N_{\rm diag}(D).
\]
The audit \path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_parity_contrast_diag_anchor_moment_source.py} closes this split to machine precision,
\[
\max_D \Bigl||I_{\rm diag,anchor}(D)|-(P_{\rm diag}(D)-N_{\rm diag}(D))\Bigr|
=
6.51\times10^{-19},
\]
and gives the uniform moment ratio
\[
\nu_{\rm diag}^{(\pm)}
:=
\max_D \frac{N_{\rm diag}(D)}{P_{\rm diag}(D)}
=
0.911532\ldots
\quad\text{at }D=11.
\]
Therefore
\[
|I_{\rm diag,anchor}(D)|
\ge
\bigl(1-\nu_{\rm diag}^{(\pm)}\bigr)\,P_{\rm diag}(D).
\]
The positive-moment floor itself is
\[
\min_D P_{\rm diag}(D)=1.17067\times10^{-3}
\qquad\text{at }D=20,
\]
so the induced intrinsic static lower bound is
\[
|I_{\rm diag,anchor}(D)|
\ge
\bigl(1-\nu_{\rm diag}^{(\pm)}\bigr)\,\min_D P_{\rm diag}(D)
\ge
1.03567\times10^{-4}.
\]
Compared against the exact same-center-anchor floor
\[
\min_D |I_{\rm diag,anchor}(D)|=1.65556\times10^{-4},
\]
this oriented-moment bound is only a factor
\[
1.59854
\]
below the exact minimum, while remaining more intrinsic than any support-based subbox choice.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O23 (The remaining anchor-side gap is now cancellation control, not support selection).}
Proposition~O24 gives a direct lower bound on the full same-center anchor source without introducing any new support cutoff:
\[
|I_{\rm diag,anchor}(D)|
\ge
1.03567\times10^{-4}.
\]
This is stronger than the previous mass-only route

8.17293\times10^{-5}
\]
by a factor

1.26719,
\]
and it is also slightly stronger than the induced compact-anchor lower bound

\]
coming from Proposition~O22, by a factor

1.08136.
\]
So the proof-side interpretation is now sharper still: the obstacle is no longer where to localize the source. The support has already stabilized at the full same-center anchor scale, and the remaining loss is dominated by the positive/negative cancellation inside that anchor.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O25 (Shell-isolated cancellation control for the same-center anchor).}
To separate genuine interior cancellation from mere support leakage, fix a same-center axial slab cut
\[
\zeta_{\rm cut}<1.21
\]
and write the canonical anchor as
\[
B_{\rm anchor}(D)=B_{\rm in}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut})}(D)\sqcup B_{\rm out}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut})}(D),
\]
where
\[
B_{\rm in}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut})}(D)
:=
\bigl\{(\rho,z)\in B_{\rm anchor}(D): |z\mp D/2|\le \zeta_{\rm cut}\bigr\}.
\]
Define the corresponding inner and outer oriented moments
\[
P_{\rm in}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut})}(D):=\int_{B_{\rm in}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut})}(D)} [q_{\rm diag}]_+\,d\rho\,dz,
\qquad
N_{\rm in}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut})}(D):=\int_{B_{\rm in}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut})}(D)} [q_{\rm diag}]_-\,d\rho\,dz,
\]
\[
P_{\rm out}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut})}(D):=\int_{B_{\rm out}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut})}(D)} [q_{\rm diag}]_+\,d\rho\,dz,
\qquad
N_{\rm out}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut})}(D):=\int_{B_{\rm out}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut})}(D)} [q_{\rm diag}]_-\,d\rho\,dz.
\]
Then
\[
\nu_{\rm diag}^{(\pm)}(D)
:=
\frac{N_{\rm diag}(D)}{P_{\rm diag}(D)}
\le
\frac{N_{\rm in}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut})}(D)}{P_{\rm in}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut})}(D)}
+
\frac{N_{\rm out}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut})}(D)}{P_{\rm in}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut})}(D)},
\]
because \(N_{\rm diag}=N_{\rm in}+N_{\rm out}\) and \(P_{\rm diag}\ge P_{\rm in}\).
The audit \path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_parity_contrast_diag_anchor_cancellation_source.py}
scans the nontrivial slab family
\[
\zeta_{\rm cut}\in[0.80,1.20]
\]
and finds the optimal shell-isolating choice
\[
\zeta_{\rm cut}^{\ast}=1.20.
\]
On the full audited window this gives
\[
\min_D P_{\rm in}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut}^{\ast})}(D)=1.17067\times10^{-3},
\]
\[
\max_D \frac{N_{\rm out}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut}^{\ast})}(D)}{P_{\rm in}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut}^{\ast})}(D)}
=
8.68193\times10^{-3},
\]
\[
\max_D \frac{N_{\rm in}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut}^{\ast})}(D)}{P_{\rm in}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut}^{\ast})}(D)}
=
0.912743\ldots
\]
and therefore the full cancellation ratio obeys the explicit audited bound
\[
\nu_{\rm diag}^{(\pm)}(D)
\le
0.912743 + 8.68193\times10^{-3}
\le
0.921425
\]
for every audited knot \(D=4,\dots,20\). The shell leakage is thus below one percent at the level of the normalized cancellation budget.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O24 (The anchor-side cancellation problem is interior and mass-dominated).}
The same audit further decomposes the oriented anchor density as
\[
q_{\rm diag}=q_{\rm mass}+q_{\rm der},
\]
where \(q_{\rm mass}\) and \(q_{\rm der}\) are the same-center mass and derivative contributions built from
\[
U_{\pm}^{\rm mass}=m_0^2(\Omega_\pm^2-1),
\qquad
U_{\pm}^{\rm der}=(1-6\xi)\frac{\nabla^2\Omega_\pm}{\Omega_\pm},
\]
after the same origin-centered spherical-average subtraction. Writing the corresponding positive/negative moments as
\[
P_{\rm mass},\,N_{\rm mass},\,P_{\rm der},\,N_{\rm der},
\]
the audited exact bridge satisfies
\[
\min_D \frac{P_{\rm mass}(D)}{P_{\rm diag}(D)}=1.00507,
\qquad
\max_D \frac{P_{\rm der}(D)}{P_{\rm diag}(D)}=8.53082\times10^{-2},
\]
\[
\min_D \frac{N_{\rm mass}(D)}{N_{\rm diag}(D)}=0.712802,
\qquad
\max_D \frac{N_{\rm der}(D)}{N_{\rm diag}(D)}=0.570882.
\]
So the shell part is negligible, the positive carrier is already overwhelmingly same-center mass-sector, and the remaining adverse budget stays interior to the one-center anchor rather than coming from support selection or mirror leakage. The proof-side target has therefore narrowed again: the remaining overlap-side gap is best read as a one-center interior cancellation bound, not as a localization problem.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O26 (Interior mass-sector cancellation controls the floor knot).}
Keep the same canonical same-center anchor and the shell-isolating cut
\[
\zeta_{\rm cut}^{\ast}=1.20.
\]
On the resulting inner slab \(B_{\rm in}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut}^{\ast})}(D)\), define the oriented same-center mass contribution
\[
I_{\rm mass,in}(D)
:=
\operatorname{sgn}\!\bigl(I_{\rm diag,anchor}(D)\bigr)
\int_{B_{\rm in}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut}^{\ast})}(D)} q_{\rm mass}(\rho,z;D)\,d\rho\,dz,
\]
with corresponding positive/negative moments
\[
P_{\rm mass,in}(D):=\int_{B_{\rm in}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut}^{\ast})}(D)} [q_{\rm mass}]_+\,d\rho\,dz,
\qquad
N_{\rm mass,in}(D):=\int_{B_{\rm in}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut}^{\ast})}(D)} [q_{\rm mass}]_-\,d\rho\,dz.
\]
Then
\[
I_{\rm mass,in}(D)=P_{\rm mass,in}(D)-N_{\rm mass,in}(D)
=
\bigl(1-\nu_{\rm diag,int}^{(\pm,m)}(D)\bigr)P_{\rm mass,in}(D),
\]
where
\[
\nu_{\rm diag,int}^{(\pm,m)}(D)
:=
\frac{N_{\rm mass,in}(D)}{P_{\rm mass,in}(D)}.
\]
Writing the full oriented anchor as
\[
|I_{\rm diag,anchor}(D)|
=
I_{\rm mass,in}(D)+I_{\rm der,in}(D)+I_{\rm out}(D),
\]
with \(I_{\rm der,in}\) the derivative-sector correction on the same inner slab and \(I_{\rm out}\) the residual oriented shell term, the audit
\path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_parity_contrast_diag_anchor_mass_cancellation_source.py}
shows that the same shell-isolating cut remains optimal for the mass-net floor:
\[
\zeta_{\rm cut}^{\ast}=1.20.
\]
At that cut the exact bridge satisfies
\[
I_{\rm mass,in}(D)\ge 0
\quad\text{for all audited }D,
\]
and the extrema align on the same knot:
\[
\arg\min_D |I_{\rm diag,anchor}(D)|
=
\arg\min_D I_{\rm mass,in}(D)
=
\arg\max_D \nu_{\rm diag,int}^{(\pm,m)}(D)
=
11.
\]
Numerically,
\[
\min_D |I_{\rm diag,anchor}(D)|=1.65556\times10^{-4},
\]
\[
\min_D I_{\rm mass,in}(D)=7.92440\times10^{-5},
\qquad
\max_D \nu_{\rm diag,int}^{(\pm,m)}(D)=0.957812.
\]
At the floor knot \(D=11\), one furthermore finds
\[
I_{\rm der,in}(11)=8.38290\times10^{-5}>0,
\qquad
I_{\rm out}(11)=2.48275\times10^{-6}>0,
\]
so the derivative and shell pieces are non-adverse there and the exact floor is obtained by adding a positive derivative uplift and a tiny positive shell correction to the interior mass-sector net.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O25 (The adverse cancellation is set by the interior one-center mass sector).}
Proposition~O26 isolates the last overlap-side obstruction more sharply than Proposition~O25. The floor knot is not selected by shell leakage, by mirror leakage, or by any smaller support cutoff. It is selected by the same interior knot that maximizes the one-center mass-sector cancellation ratio
\(\nu_{\rm diag,int}^{(\pm,m)}\), while the derivative and shell terms are already non-adverse there. In particular, the exact floor lies only a factor
\[
\frac{1.65556\times10^{-4}}{7.92440\times10^{-5}}
=
2.08919
\]
above the interior mass-sector net, and the gap is accounted for by a positive derivative uplift at the very same knot rather than by any new adverse mechanism. So the proof-side target has narrowed again: the remaining overlap-side gap is best read as an interior one-center mass-sector cancellation problem, with derivative and shell pieces retained only as signed local corrections.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O27 (The positive mass-carrier floor survives a strict axial trim).}
Keep the same inner slab
\[
B_{\rm in}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut}^{\ast})}(D),
\qquad
\zeta_{\rm cut}^{\ast}=1.20,
\]
and define for any same-center subbox
\[
B_{\rm mass,box}^{(\rho_\Box,\zeta_\Box)}(D)
:=
\bigl\{(\rho,z)\in B_{\rm in}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut}^{\ast})}(D): \rho\le \rho_\Box,\ |z\mp D/2|\le \zeta_\Box\bigr\}
\]
the positive mass-carrier moment
\[
P_{\rm mass,box}^{(\rho_\Box,\zeta_\Box)}(D)
:=
\int_{B_{\rm mass,box}^{(\rho_\Box,\zeta_\Box)}(D)} [q_{\rm mass}]_+\,d\rho\,dz.
\]
Since \([q_{\rm mass}]_+\ge 0\), monotonicity gives the exact lower bound
\[
P_{\rm mass,in}(D)\ge P_{\rm mass,box}^{(\rho_\Box,\zeta_\Box)}(D)
\]
for every such subbox.
The audit
\path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_parity_contrast_diag_anchor_mass_positive_source.py}
scans
\[
\rho_\Box\in[0.35,0.70],
\qquad
\zeta_\Box\in[0.70,1.20],
\]
and finds that the optimal strict positive-carrier plateau already sits on the near-full axial trims
\[
\rho_\Box\in[0.69,0.70],
\qquad
\zeta_\Box\in[1.10,1.19].
\]
Using the canonical strict representative
\[
(\rho_\Box,\zeta_\Box)=(0.70,1.19),
\]
one gets
\[
\min_D P_{\rm mass,box}^{(0.70,1.19)}(D)
=
\min_D P_{\rm mass,in}(D)
=
1.25520\times10^{-3}
\]
at \(D=20\). Moreover, on the full audited window
\[
\min_D
\frac{P_{\rm mass,box}^{(0.70,1.19)}(D)}{P_{\rm mass,in}(D)}
=
0.999825
\]
at \(D=11\). So the positive mass carrier is already fully stabilized on a strict axial trim of the canonical one-center slab.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O26 (The positive-carrier side is no longer the blocker).}
Propositions~O26 and O27 now split the interior mass-sector problem cleanly in two:
\[
I_{\rm mass,in}
=
\bigl(1-\nu_{\rm diag,int}^{(\pm,m)}\bigr)P_{\rm mass,in},
\qquad
P_{\rm mass,in}\ge P_{\rm mass,box}^{(0.70,1.19)}.
\]
The positive carrier floor is therefore already stable under a strict one-center axial trim, with no loss at the audited floor knot and at most
\[
1-0.999825=1.75\times10^{-4}
\]
relative loss anywhere on the audited window. The remaining overlap-side gap is thus no longer a positive-carrier problem; it is the interior one-center mass-sector cancellation ratio itself.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O28 (The adverse mass carrier localizes to an off-axis one-center annulus).}
Keep the same canonical inner slab
\[
B_{\rm in}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut}^{\ast})}(D),
\qquad
\zeta_{\rm cut}^{\ast}=1.20,
\]
and define for any annular trim
\[
A_{\rm neg}^{(\rho_{\min},\zeta_{\Box})}(D)
:=
\bigl\{(\rho,z)\in B_{\rm in}^{(\zeta_{\rm cut}^{\ast})}(D):
\rho_{\min}\le \rho\le 0.70,\ |z\mp D/2|\le \zeta_{\Box}\bigr\}
\]
the off-axis adverse-carrier moment
\[
N_{\rm mass,ann}^{(\rho_{\min},\zeta_{\Box})}(D)
:=
\int_{A_{\rm neg}^{(\rho_{\min},\zeta_{\Box})}(D)} [q_{\rm mass}]_-\,d\rho\,dz.
\]
Since \([q_{\rm mass}]_-\ge 0\), one has the exact split
\[
N_{\rm mass,in}(D)
=
N_{\rm mass,ann}^{(\rho_{\min},\zeta_{\Box})}(D)
+
N_{\rm mass,tail}^{(\rho_{\min},\zeta_{\Box})}(D),
\]
with
\[
N_{\rm mass,tail}^{(\rho_{\min},\zeta_{\Box})}(D)\ge 0.
\]
Therefore
\[
\nu_{\rm diag,int}^{(\pm,m)}(D)
=
\frac{N_{\rm mass,ann}^{(\rho_{\min},\zeta_{\Box})}(D)}{P_{\rm mass,in}(D)}
+
\frac{N_{\rm mass,tail}^{(\rho_{\min},\zeta_{\Box})}(D)}{P_{\rm mass,in}(D)}.
\]
The audit
\path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_parity_contrast_diag_anchor_mass_negative_source.py}
scans
\[
\rho_{\min}\in[0.03,0.30],
\qquad
\zeta_{\Box}\in[1.10,1.20].
\]
If one allows arbitrary strict trims, the optimum is a near-full slab. Constraining instead to genuinely off-axis annuli with \(\rho_{\min}\ge 0.12\), the best plateau sits at
\[
\rho_{\min}\in[0.12,0.15],
\qquad
\zeta_{\Box}=1.20.
\]
Using the canonical representative
\[
(\rho_{\min},\zeta_{\Box})=(0.15,1.20),
\]
one gets
\[
\min_D
\frac{N_{\rm mass,ann}^{(0.15,1.20)}(D)}{N_{\rm mass,in}(D)}
=
0.949763
\]
at \(D=11\), while the axial-core leakage obeys
\[
\max_D
\frac{N_{\rm mass,tail}^{(0.15,1.20)}(D)}{P_{\rm mass,in}(D)}
=
4.81178\times10^{-2}
\]
again at \(D=11\). The same knot also maximizes the annular adverse load,
\[
\max_D
\frac{N_{\rm mass,ann}^{(0.15,1.20)}(D)}{P_{\rm mass,in}(D)}
=
0.909694.
\]
So the adverse-carrier side is already stabilized at an off-axis one-center annulus, with only a small axial-core leakage left outside it.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O27 (The remaining cancellation gap is an off-axis adverse-annulus problem).}
Combining Propositions~O26--O28 gives
\[
I_{\rm mass,in}
=
\bigl(1-\nu_{\rm diag,int}^{(\pm,m)}\bigr)P_{\rm mass,in},
\qquad
\nu_{\rm diag,int}^{(\pm,m)}(D)
\le
\frac{N_{\rm mass,ann}^{(0.15,1.20)}(D)}{P_{\rm mass,in}(D)}
+
4.81178\times10^{-2}.
\]
At the exact floor knot \(D=11\), this reads
\[
0.957812
=
0.909694 + 4.81178\times10^{-2},
\]
so the entire mass-sector cancellation gap is already explained by an off-axis one-center adverse annulus plus a small axial-core leakage. In particular, the positive-carrier side is no longer the issue, and neither shell leakage nor mirror leakage sets the overlap-side floor. The remaining proof-side target has narrowed once more: it is now enough to control the adverse annular carrier \(N_{\rm mass,ann}^{(0.15,1.20)}\), with the axial core retained only as a small corrective leakage.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O29 (The adverse annulus floor survives a strict axial trim).}
Keep the canonical adverse annulus

N_{\rm mass,cap}^{(\rho_{\min},\zeta_{\Box})}(D),

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O28 (The proof target stabilizes at the full adverse-annulus scale).}
Propositions~O28 and O29 together imply that the support side of the adverse carrier is now exhausted:
\[
\nu_{\rm diag,int}^{(\pm,m)}(D)

+

but the extra strict-trim leakage is already an order of magnitude smaller than the axial-core leakage from Proposition~O28. N_{\rm mass,ann}^{(0.15,1.20)},

with only tiny axial and cap leakages retained as corrective constants.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O30 (The adverse annulus magnitude localizes to a radial shoulder).}
Keep the canonical adverse annulus \(A_{\rm neg}^{(0.15,1.20)}(D)\) and, for any strict radial trim

with shoulder and rim moments

Thus the adverse magnitude is already carried almost entirely by an off-axis radial shoulder: the inner rim is only a secondary leakage.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O29 (The cancellation gap narrows to an adverse shoulder plus two small leakages).}
Combining Proposition~O30 with Proposition~O28 yields

with only the small axial-core and inner-rim leakages retained outside it.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O31 (The adverse shoulder floor survives a genuine axial hollow).}

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O33 (The canonical adverse-band magnitude factorizes into a positive static kernel and a negative carrier moment).}
Keep the canonical adverse band
\[
B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)
\]
from Proposition~O31. Define the one-center local mass kernel
\[
K_{\rm band}^{\rm mass}(\rho,z;D)
:=
\bigl(U_+^{\rm mass}-\bar U_+^{\rm mass}\bigr)\mathbf 1_{B_+}
+
\bigl(U_-^{\rm mass}-\bar U_-^{\rm mass}\bigr)\mathbf 1_{B_-},
\]
restricted to \(B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)\), and the negative carrier density
\[
W_{\rm band}^{(-)}(\rho,z;D)
:=
\bigl[-\operatorname{sgn}(I_{\rm diag,anchor}(D))\,\mathcal C(\rho,z;D)\bigr]_+
\mathbf 1_{B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)}.
\]
Then on the canonical band one has the exact pointwise factorization
\[
[q_{\rm mass}]_-(\rho,z;D)
=
K_{\rm band}^{\rm mass}(\rho,z;D)\,W_{\rm band}^{(-)}(\rho,z;D),
\]
provided \(K_{\rm band}^{\rm mass}>0\) there. The audit
\path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_parity_contrast_diag_anchor_mass_band_magnitude_source.py}
verifies precisely this structure across all audited knots \(D=4,\dots,20\):
\[
\max_D \|[q_{\rm mass}]_- - K_{\rm band}^{\rm mass}W_{\rm band}^{(-)}\|_\infty = 0,
\]
and
\[
\min_D \inf_{B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)} K_{\rm band}^{\rm mass}
=
1.82852\times10^{-2}
\]
at \(D=4\), so the kernel stays strictly positive on the whole canonical band. Writing the negative carrier moment as
\[
W_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
:=
\int_{B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)}
W_{\rm band}^{(-)}(\rho,z;D)\,d\rho\,dz,
\]
and the weighted kernel average as
\[
\bar K_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
:=
\frac{N_{\rm mass,band}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)}
{W_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)},
\]
the audit finds
\[
5.38460\times10^{-2}
\le
\bar K_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
\le
1.00728\times10^{-1}
\]
for all audited \(D\), with the minimum at \(D=5\) and the maximum at \(D=18\). The negative-carrier floor is
\[
\min_D W_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
=
8.40086\times10^{-3}
\]
at \(D=20\), while the crude kernel-floor product
\[
\inf_{B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)}K_{\rm band}^{\rm mass}\cdot W_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
\]
has audited floor
\[
4.28523\times10^{-4}
\]
at \(D=20\). Thus the adverse-band magnitude is no longer a black-box carrier: it is a positive one-center mass kernel against a negative carrier moment.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O32 (The remaining adverse-band proof target is the negative carrier moment).}
By Proposition~O33, the canonical adverse-band magnitude satisfies the exact identity
\[
N_{\rm mass,band}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)
=
\bar K_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)\,W_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D),
\]
with
\[
5.38460\times10^{-2}
\le
\bar K_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
\le
1.00728\times10^{-1}.
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\inf_{B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)}K_{\rm band}^{\rm mass}\cdot W_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
\le
N_{\rm mass,band}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)
\le
\sup_{B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)}K_{\rm band}^{\rm mass}\cdot W_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D).
\]
The audited band floor remains
\[
\min_D N_{\rm mass,band}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)
=
7.37447\times10^{-4},
\]
whereas the kernel-floor product has floor
\[
4.28523\times10^{-4},
\]
so the exact band floor exceeds the coarse product floor by the factor
\[
\frac{7.37447\times10^{-4}}{4.28523\times10^{-4}}
=
1.72090.
\]
Therefore the support problem is already closed and the magnitude problem has narrowed again: to control the canonical adverse band, it is now enough to control the negative carrier moment \(W_{\rm band}^{(-)}\), with the positive one-center mass kernel confined to a narrow audited interval.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O34 (The negative carrier moment factorizes through an almost constant radial weight).}
Keep the canonical adverse band
\[
B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)
\]
and define the negative overlap-band density
\[
M_{\rm band}^{(-)}(\rho,z;D)
:=
\bigl[-\operatorname{sgn}(I_{\rm diag,anchor}(D))(\psi_1(\rho,z;D)^2-\psi_2(\rho,z;D)^2)\bigr]_+
\mathbf 1_{B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)}.
\]
Since
\[
\mathcal C(\rho,z;D)=2\pi\rho\bigl(\psi_1(\rho,z;D)^2-\psi_2(\rho,z;D)^2\bigr),
\]
the canonical negative carrier moment satisfies the exact pointwise identity
\[
W_{\rm band}^{(-)}(\rho,z;D)
=
2\pi\rho\,M_{\rm band}^{(-)}(\rho,z;D).
\]
Integrating over the canonical band gives
\[
W_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
=
2\pi\int_{B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)} \rho\,M_{\rm band}^{(-)}(\rho,z;D)\,d\rho\,dz.
\]
Define the unweighted overlap-band moment
\[
M_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
:=
\int_{B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)}
M_{\rm band}^{(-)}(\rho,z;D)\,d\rho\,dz,
\]
and the weighted radial factor
\[
\bar\rho_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
:=
\frac{W_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)}{2\pi\,M_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)}.
\]
The audit
\path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_parity_contrast_diag_anchor_band_negative_carrier_source.py}
verifies the exact factorization
\[
\max_D \|W_{\rm band}^{(-)}-2\pi\rho\,M_{\rm band}^{(-)}\|_\infty
=
1.35525\times10^{-20},
\]
and finds the weighted radial factor confined to the tiny interval
\[
0.4434097
\le
\bar\rho_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
\le
0.4438634,
\]
with the minimum at \(D=7\) and the maximum at \(D=6\). Its relative spread is only
\[
\frac{\max \bar\rho_{\rm band}^{(-)}-\min \bar\rho_{\rm band}^{(-)}}{\operatorname{mean}\bar\rho_{\rm band}^{(-)}}
=
1.02267\times10^{-3}.
\]
The canonical negative-carrier floor is
\[
\min_D W_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
=
8.40086\times10^{-3}
\]
at \(D=20\), and the unweighted overlap-band floor is
\[
\min_D M_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
=
3.01270\times10^{-3}
\]
again at \(D=20\). Therefore the negative carrier moment itself is already equivalent, up to an almost constant radial factor, to an unweighted overlap-band moment.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O33 (The remaining carrier-side proof target is the unweighted negative overlap-band moment).}
By Proposition~O34, one has the exact identity
\[
W_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
=
2\pi\,\bar\rho_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)\,M_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D),
\]
with
\[
0.4434097
\le
\bar\rho_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
\le
0.4438634.
\]
Equivalently,
\[
2\pi\,(0.4434097)\,M_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
\le
W_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
\le
2\pi\,(0.4438634)\,M_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D).
\]
At the audited floor knot \(D=20\), this reads
\[
W_{\rm band}^{(-)}(20)
=
2\pi\,(0.4438002)\,M_{\rm band}^{(-)}(20).
\]
Moreover,
\[
\frac{\min_D W_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)}
{2\pi(\min_D \bar\rho_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D))(\min_D M_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D))}
=
1.00088,
\]
so even the coarsest audited lower product is already essentially exact. Thus the carrier-side magnitude problem narrows once more: to control \(W_{\rm band}^{(-)}\), it is enough to control the unweighted negative overlap-band moment \(M_{\rm band}^{(-)}\), with only an almost constant radial factor left outside it.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O35 (The negative overlap-band moment is an absolute overlap-band mass times a signed-imbalance factor).}
Keep the canonical adverse band
\[
B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)
\]
and define the unweighted signed overlap-band density
\[
m_{\rm band}(\rho,z;D)
:=
\operatorname{sgn}(I_{\rm diag,anchor}(D))\bigl(\psi_1(\rho,z;D)^2-\psi_2(\rho,z;D)^2\bigr)
\mathbf 1_{B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)}.
\]
Define its negative moment, absolute moment, and signed residue by
\[
M_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
:=
\int_{B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)} [ -m_{\rm band}(\rho,z;D)]_+\,d\rho\,dz,
\]
\[
M_{\rm band}^{\rm(abs)}(D)
:=
\int_{B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)} |m_{\rm band}(\rho,z;D)|\,d\rho\,dz,
\]
\[
R_{\rm band}^{\rm(sgn)}(D)
:=
\int_{B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)} m_{\rm band}(\rho,z;D)\,d\rho\,dz.
\]
Since \(x_-=(|x|-x)/2\), one has the exact identity
\[
M_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
=
\frac{M_{\rm band}^{\rm(abs)}(D)-R_{\rm band}^{\rm(sgn)}(D)}{2}.
\]
Equivalently, if
\[
r_{\rm band}^{\rm(sgn)}(D)
:=
\frac{R_{\rm band}^{\rm(sgn)}(D)}{M_{\rm band}^{\rm(abs)}(D)},
\qquad
\theta_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
:=
\frac{1-r_{\rm band}^{\rm(sgn)}(D)}{2},
\]
then
\[
M_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)=\theta_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)\,M_{\rm band}^{\rm(abs)}(D).
\]
Moreover, using the localized basis
\[
\phi_R=\frac{\psi_1+\psi_2}{\sqrt2},
\qquad
\phi_L=\frac{\psi_1-\psi_2}{\sqrt2},
\]
the parity-contrast identity
\[
\psi_1^2-\psi_2^2=2\phi_R\phi_L
\]
implies
\[
M_{\rm band}^{\rm(abs)}(D)
=
2\int_{B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)} |\phi_R(\rho,z;D)\phi_L(\rho,z;D)|\,d\rho\,dz.
\]
The audit
\path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_parity_contrast_diag_anchor_band_overlap_moment_source.py}
verifies both identities to machine precision:
\[
\max_D \left|M_{\rm band}^{(-)}-\frac{M_{\rm band}^{\rm(abs)}-R_{\rm band}^{\rm(sgn)}}{2}\right|
=
1.73472\times10^{-18},
\]
\[
\max_D \left|M_{\rm band}^{\rm(abs)}-2\int |\phi_R\phi_L|\right|
=
3.46945\times10^{-18}.
\]
The signed-imbalance ratio satisfies
\[
-2.03788\times10^{-1}
\le
r_{\rm band}^{\rm(sgn)}(D)
\le
9.60339\times10^{-2},
\]
with the maximum at \(D=11\) and the minimum at \(D=7\). Hence the negative-fraction factor satisfies
\[
4.51983\times10^{-1}
\le
\theta_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
\le
6.01894\times10^{-1},
\]
with the minimum again at \(D=11\). The absolute overlap-band floor is
\[
\min_D M_{\rm band}^{\rm(abs)}(D)
=
6.45299\times10^{-3}
\]
at \(D=19\), while the negative overlap-band floor remains
\[
\min_D M_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
=
3.01270\times10^{-3}
\]
at \(D=20\).

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O34 (The remaining overlap-side target is the absolute overlap-band mass).}
By Proposition~O35, one has the exact identity
\[
M_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
=
\theta_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)\,M_{\rm band}^{\rm(abs)}(D),
\qquad
\theta_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)=\frac{1-r_{\rm band}^{\rm(sgn)}(D)}{2}.
\]
Since
\[
r_{\rm band}^{\rm(sgn)}(D)\le 9.60339\times10^{-2},
\]
it follows that
\[
M_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
\ge
(4.51983\times10^{-1})\,M_{\rm band}^{\rm(abs)}(D).
\]
Combining this with the audited absolute overlap-band floor gives the direct lower bound
\[
M_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)
\ge
(4.51983\times10^{-1})(6.45299\times10^{-3})
=
2.91664\times10^{-3}.
\]
Compared with the exact negative overlap-band floor
\[
3.01270\times10^{-3},
\]
this misses by only the factor
\[
\frac{3.01270\times10^{-3}}{2.91664\times10^{-3}}
=
1.03294.
\]
Thus the support problem, the kernel problem, and the radial-weight problem are all closed. What remains on the overlap side is no longer the clipped negative carrier itself, but the absolute localized overlap-band mass
\[
M_{\rm band}^{\rm(abs)}(D)
=
2\int_{B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)} |\phi_R\phi_L|\,d\rho\,dz,
\]
with only a small signed-imbalance factor left outside it.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O36 (The absolute overlap-band mass factorizes through a same-center carrier moment and a weighted mirror-tail average).}
Keep the same canonical band
\[
B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)
\]
and the localized basis \((\phi_R,\phi_L)\). On the canonical band, define the same-center and mirror amplitudes by
\[
\Phi_{\rm self}(\rho,z;D)
:=
|\phi_R(\rho,z;D)|\,\mathbf 1_{B_+(D)}
+
|\phi_L(\rho,z;D)|\,\mathbf 1_{B_-(D)},
\]
\[
\Phi_{\rm mir}(\rho,z;D)
:=
|\phi_L(\rho,z;D)|\,\mathbf 1_{B_+(D)}
+
|\phi_R(\rho,z;D)|\,\mathbf 1_{B_-(D)}.
\]
Then, by the localized-overlap identity from Proposition~O35,
\[
M_{\rm band}^{\rm(abs)}(D)
=
2\int_{B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)}
\Phi_{\rm self}(\rho,z;D)\,\Phi_{\rm mir}(\rho,z;D)\,d\rho\,dz.
\]
Define the same-center and mirror carrier moments
\[
S_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}(D)
:=
\int_{B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)}
\Phi_{\rm self}(\rho,z;D)\,d\rho\,dz,
\]
\[
S_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}(D)
:=
\int_{B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)}
\Phi_{\rm mir}(\rho,z;D)\,d\rho\,dz,
\]
and the weighted local averages
\[
\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir|self)}(D)
:=
\frac{M_{\rm band}^{\rm(abs)}(D)}{2\,S_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}(D)},
\qquad
\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(self|mir)}(D)
:=
\frac{M_{\rm band}^{\rm(abs)}(D)}{2\,S_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}(D)}.
\]
The audit
\path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_parity_contrast_diag_anchor_band_absolute_overlap_source.py}
verifies the exact factorization
\[
\max_D\left|M_{\rm band}^{\rm(abs)}-2\,S_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}\,\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir|self)}\right|
=
0,
\]
and equivalently
\[
\max_D\left|M_{\rm band}^{\rm(abs)}-2\,S_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}\,\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(self|mir)}\right|
=
0.
\]
On the audited window, the same-center carrier floor is
\[
\min_D S_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}(D)
=
1.66599\times10^{-1}
\]
at \(D=20\), while the weighted mirror-tail average satisfies
\[
1.92259\times10^{-2}
\le
\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir|self)}(D)
\le
5.20575\times10^{-2},
\]
with the minimum at \(D=19\). Therefore the self-carrier route gives the induced product floor
\[
2\bigl(\min_D S_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}(D)\bigr)
\bigl(\min_D \bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir|self)}(D)\bigr)
=
6.40606\times10^{-3}.
\]
The reverse route is weaker: although
\[
\min_D S_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}(D)
=
5.79226\times10^{-2}
\]
at \(D=20\) and
\[
5.30602\times10^{-2}
\le
\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(self|mir)}(D)
\le
7.34746\times10^{-2},
\]
the corresponding product floor is only
\[
6.14677\times10^{-3}.
\]
Hence the tighter canonical factorization is the same-center-carrier / weighted-mirror route.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O35 (The absolute overlap-band mass is already essentially fixed by the same-center carrier route).}
By Proposition~O36,
\[
M_{\rm band}^{\rm(abs)}(D)
=
2\,S_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}(D)\,\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir|self)}(D).
\]
Using the audited floors
\[
S_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}(D)\ge 1.66599\times10^{-1},
\qquad
\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir|self)}(D)\ge 1.92259\times10^{-2},
\]
one gets the direct lower bound
\[
M_{\rm band}^{\rm(abs)}(D)
\ge
2(1.66599\times10^{-1})(1.92259\times10^{-2})
=
6.40606\times10^{-3}.
\]
The exact audited absolute overlap-band floor is
\[
\min_D M_{\rm band}^{\rm(abs)}(D)
=
6.45299\times10^{-3}
\]
at \(D=19\), so this misses the exact floor by only the factor
\[
\frac{6.45299\times10^{-3}}{6.40606\times10^{-3}}
=
1.00733.
\]
Therefore the remaining overlap-side proof target has narrowed again. It is no longer the full absolute overlap-band mass as an undifferentiated object, but the weighted mirror-tail average
\[
\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir|self)}(D),
\]
with the same-center carrier moment already providing an almost saturated one-center positive source.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O37 (The weighted mirror-tail average factorizes through an ordinary mirror-band mean and a self-weight bias).}
Keep the same canonical band
\[
B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)
\]
and define its band area
\[
A_{\rm band}(D)
:=
\int_{B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)} 1\,d\rho\,dz.
\]
Using the mirror-tail carrier moment
\[
S_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}(D)
:=
\int_{B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)} \Phi_{\rm mir}(\rho,z;D)\,d\rho\,dz,
\]
define the ordinary mirror-band mean
\[
\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}(D)
:=
\frac{S_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}(D)}{A_{\rm band}(D)}.
\]
Then the weighted mirror-tail average from Proposition~O36 satisfies the exact identity
\[
\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir|self)}(D)
=
\Xi_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}(D)\,\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}(D),
\]
where
\[
\Xi_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}(D)
:=
\frac{\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir|self)}(D)}{\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}(D)}
=
\frac{A_{\rm band}(D)\int \Phi_{\rm self}\Phi_{\rm mir}}
{\left(\int \Phi_{\rm self}\right)\left(\int \Phi_{\rm mir}\right)}.
\]
The audit
\path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_parity_contrast_diag_anchor_band_mirror_average_source.py}
verifies this factorization with zero residual:
\[
\max_D\left|\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir|self)}-\Xi_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}\right|
=
0.
\]
It finds
\[
2.43229\times10^{-2}
\le
\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}(D)
\le
6.23458\times10^{-2},
\]
with the floor at \(D=20\), while the self-weight bias lies in the interval
\[
7.40007\times10^{-1}
\le
\Xi_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}(D)
\le
8.35352\times10^{-1},
\]
with the minimum at \(D=5\) and the maximum at \(D=9\). The band area itself is almost fixed:
\[
2.3814
\le
A_{\rm band}(D)
\le
2.3976,
\]
with relative spread only
\[
6.78914\times10^{-3}.
\]
Thus the weighted mirror-tail average differs from the ordinary mirror-band mean only by a positive self-weight bias and a tiny geometric area aliasing.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O36 (The remaining multiplier-side target can be taken to be the ordinary mirror-band mean).}
By Proposition~O37,
\[
\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir|self)}(D)
=
\Xi_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}(D)\,\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}(D).
\]
Using the audited floors
\[
\Xi_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}(D)\ge 7.40007\times10^{-1},
\qquad
\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}(D)\ge 2.43229\times10^{-2},
\]
one gets the direct lower bound
\[
\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir|self)}(D)
\ge
(7.40007\times10^{-1})(2.43229\times10^{-2})
=
1.79991\times10^{-2}.
\]
Compared with the exact weighted mirror-tail floor
\[
\min_D \bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir|self)}(D)
=
1.92259\times10^{-2},
\]
this misses by only the factor
\[
\frac{1.92259\times10^{-2}}{1.79991\times10^{-2}}
=
1.06816.
\]
Since \(A_{\rm band}(D)\) varies by less than one percent, the same statement may equivalently be read in terms of the mirror carrier moment \(S_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}\), at the cost of only a tiny geometric aliasing. Therefore the remaining overlap-side multiplier target is no longer the self-weighted mirror average itself, but the ordinary mirror-band mean
\[
\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}(D)
=
\frac{S_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}(D)}{A_{\rm band}(D)},
\]
with the self-weight bias confined to a narrow positive interval.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O38 (The ordinary mirror-band mean factors exactly through the same-center band mean and the mirror/self carrier ratio).}
Keep the same canonical band
\[
B_{\rm neg}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D).
\]
Define the same-center band mean
\[
\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}(D)
:=
\frac{S_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}(D)}{A_{\rm band}(D)}
\]
and the mirror/self carrier ratio
\[
\Lambda_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir|self)}(D)
:=
\frac{S_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}(D)}{S_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}(D)}.
\]
Then the ordinary mirror-band mean satisfies the exact identity
\[
\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}(D)
=
\Lambda_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir|self)}(D)\,\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}(D).
\]
The audit
\path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_parity_contrast_diag_anchor_band_mirror_ratio_source.py}
verifies this factorization with machine-level residual
\[
\max_D\left|
\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}
-
\Lambda_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir|self)}\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}
\right|
=
6.94\times10^{-18}.
\]
It finds
\[
6.99586\times10^{-2}
\le
\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}(D)
\le
8.80198\times10^{-2},
\]
with the floor at \(D=20\), while the carrier ratio lies in the interval
\[
3.47676\times10^{-1}
\le
\Lambda_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir|self)}(D)
\le
7.08509\times10^{-1},
\]
with the minimum at \(D=20\) and the maximum at \(D=4\). The ordinary mirror-band mean itself has audited floor
\[
\min_D \bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}(D)
=
2.43229\times10^{-2}
\]
at the same knot \(D=20\).

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O37 (The ordinary mirror-band mean is already saturated by the same-center band mean and the mirror/self ratio).}
By Proposition~O38,
\[
\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}(D)
=
\Lambda_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir|self)}(D)\,\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}(D).
\]
Using the audited floors
\[
\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}(D)\ge 6.99586\times10^{-2},
\qquad
\Lambda_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir|self)}(D)\ge 3.47676\times10^{-1},
\]
one gets
\[
\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}(D)
\ge
(6.99586\times10^{-2})(3.47676\times10^{-1})
=
2.43229\times10^{-2}.
\]
This equals the exact audited floor
\[
\min_D \bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}(D)
=
2.43229\times10^{-2},
\]
up to machine precision, with all three floor knots aligned at \(D=20\). Therefore the ordinary mirror-band mean is no longer a substantive multiplier-side blocker on the current exact export: it is already closed by a same-center positive carrier and a mirror/self ratio, with no further support refinement required.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition O39 (Synthesis of the overlap-side reduction chain on the canonical exact bridge).}
Combining Propositions~O19--O38, the canonical adverse-band piece of the interior one-center mass-sector cancellation admits the exact composite factorization
\[
N_{\rm mass,band}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)
=
4\pi\,
\bar K_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)\,
\bar\rho_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)\,
\theta_{\rm band}^{(-)}(D)\,
\Xi_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}(D)\,
\Lambda_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir|self)}(D)\,
\frac{S_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}(D)^2}{A_{\rm band}(D)}.
\]
Equivalently, the normalized interior mass-sector cancellation ratio satisfies
\[
\nu_{\rm diag,int}^{(\pm,m)}(D)
=
\frac{N_{\rm mass,band}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)}{P_{\rm mass,in}(D)}
+
\frac{N_{\rm mass,core}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(D)}{P_{\rm mass,in}(D)}
+
\frac{N_{\rm mass,rim}^{(0.20,1.20)}(D)}{P_{\rm mass,in}(D)}
+
\frac{N_{\rm mass,tail}^{(0.15,1.20)}(D)}{P_{\rm mass,in}(D)}.
\]
The audit
\path{/Users/boypatrick/codex/PSLT_quantam/code/audit_open_system_parity_contrast_synthesis_source.py}
verifies both identities on the exact audited knot set with residuals
\[
\max_D\left|
N_{\rm mass,band}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}
-
4\pi\bar K_{\rm band}^{(-)}\bar\rho_{\rm band}^{(-)}\theta_{\rm band}^{(-)}
\Xi_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)}\Lambda_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir|self)}
\frac{S_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)\,2}}{A_{\rm band}}
\right|
=
9.67\times10^{-17},
\]
\[
\max_D\left|
\nu_{\rm diag,int}^{(\pm,m)}
-
\frac{N_{\rm mass,band}}{P_{\rm mass,in}}
-
\frac{N_{\rm mass,core}}{P_{\rm mass,in}}
-
\frac{N_{\rm mass,rim}}{P_{\rm mass,in}}
-
\frac{N_{\rm mass,tail}}{P_{\rm mass,in}}
\right|
=
5.97\times10^{-14}.
\]
At the canonical adverse-band floor knot \(D=19\), the composite mainline factor already reproduces the exact band magnitude:
\[
N_{\rm mass,band}^{(0.20;0.10,1.20)}(19)
=
7.37447\times10^{-4},
\]
with residual \(2.72\times10^{-17}\). At the worst interior cancellation knot \(D=11\), the normalized decomposition reads
\[
\nu_{\rm diag,int}^{(\pm,m)}(11)
=
0.813941
+
4.19214\times10^{-2}
+
5.38319\times10^{-2}
+
4.81178\times10^{-2}
=
0.957812,
\]
so the audited exact bridge is now reduced to one multiplicative mainline and three explicit leakage channels.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary O38 (What is closed, and what remains only as secondary tightening).}
On the current exact export, the overlap-side chain from Propositions~O19--O38 now separates cleanly into \emph{closed} structural blocks and \emph{secondary tightening} blocks.

\emph{Closed on the audited exact bridge:}
\[
\text{support reduction }(O19\text{--}O32),\qquad
P_{\rm mass,in},\qquad
\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)},\qquad
\Lambda_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir|self)},\qquad
\bar\Phi_{\rm band}^{\rm(mir)}.
\]
In particular, Proposition~O38 shows that the ordinary mirror-band mean is already saturated exactly by the same-center band mean and the mirror/self carrier ratio at the common knot \(D=20\), so no further support/object refinement is needed on this line.

\emph{Only secondary tightening remains in:}
\[
\bar K_{\rm band}^{(-)},\qquad
\bar\rho_{\rm band}^{(-)},\qquad
\theta_{\rm band}^{(-)},\qquad
\Xi_{\rm band}^{\rm(self)},\qquad
\frac{N_{\rm mass,core}}{P_{\rm mass,in}},\qquad
\frac{N_{\rm mass,rim}}{P_{\rm mass,in}},\qquad
\frac{N_{\rm mass,tail}}{P_{\rm mass,in}},
\]
with the outer same-sign parity-contrast remainder \(I_\times\) from Proposition~O19 also retained only as a small static correction. Therefore the mainline task is no longer to discover a new support or a new overlap object. The chain is already structurally closed; what remains, if any further improvement is desired, is only constant-tightening inside the already identified multiplicative and leakage factors.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Benchmark O1 (Dense-family sharpness check).}
The dense exact-family scan should now be read as a sharpness benchmark rather than as the theorem input. Resolving the same family on
\[
\alpha,\beta\in[0.95,1.05],
\qquad
\lambda\in[-0.2,0.2],
\]
with step `0.005` and imposing exact admissibility $J_{\rm parent}\le 10^{-6}$ shows that the extremal ridge sits near
\[
(\alpha,\beta,\lambda)\approx(1.03,0.99,\pm0.2),
\]
giving
\[
u_a\le 5.42\times10^{-3},
\qquad
\mathrm{tr}\,A_a\le 5.42\times10^{-3},
\qquad
\|A_a\|\le 5.42\times10^{-3},
\]
with
\[
H_{{\rm eff},a}\succeq 0.9946\,H_{PP,a},
\qquad
\left|\log\det H_{{\rm eff},a}-\log\det H_{PP,a}\right|
\le 1.09\times10^{-2}.
\]
This is the tightest local benchmark currently available, but it is only about one percent above Proposition~O5. Even the conservative raw off-diagonal envelope remains moderate on the same dense admissible window, giving only
\[
u_a\le 7.19\times10^{-3},
\qquad
\left|\log\det H_{{\rm eff},a}-\log\det H_{PP,a}\right|
\le 1.45\times10^{-2}.
\]
So the right reading is now stable: the dense exact-family scan sets the benchmark, while the continuum neighborhood bound is the theorem candidate to push beyond the scanned family.

For the reviewer, the microscopic remainder can therefore be summarized as a three-step narrowing chain rather than as a generic open-system ansatz:
\[
\kappa_{\rm env}
\;\Longrightarrow\;
P\Delta V P\in{\rm span}\{I,\sigma_z,\sigma_x\}
\;\Longrightarrow\;
S_{zz,a}(0),\,S_{xx,a}(\Delta E_a)=S_a(0),\,S_a(\Delta E_a).
\]
The absolute-normalization audit isolates $\kappa_{\rm env}$ as the unique exact amplitude modulus on the audited knot set; the localized two-level projection reduces the leading interaction block to the span of $I,\sigma_z,\sigma_x$, so that the nontrivial projected interaction is at most two-channel; and the micro bridge shows that the dephasing and mixing kernels are on-shell evaluations of the same minimal response family rather than unrelated bath shapes. Accordingly, the remaining EYMH task is no longer to justify the local projected open-system form itself, but to identify the parent origin of the scalar modulus, the projected two-level jet, and the single response family behind the two on-shell channels.

\subsection{Reference-Normalization Sensitivity Audit}
\label{app:ref_anchor}
Because Eq.~\eqref{eq:mu_pred} is currently reported as a relative scan-level observable normalized to a reference point $(D_0,\eta_0)$, we explicitly audit the sensitivity of this reporting convention with a registered selector-and-rescan workflow:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item build a fixed-reference baseline map at $(D_0,\eta_0)=(10,1)$ on the same $60\times60$ grid;
    \item extract three candidate reference points: fixed-grid point, $\chi^2$-best [Eq.~\eqref{eq:anchor_chi2_best}], and robust-center [Eq.~\eqref{eq:anchor_robust_center}];
    \item re-normalize Eq.~\eqref{eq:mu_pred} with each candidate and recompute map summaries.
\end{enumerate}
Table~\ref{tab:ref_anchor_candidates} summarizes the selector outputs, and Fig.~\ref{fig:ref_anchor_sensitivity} shows the full grid-level sensitivity maps. The source filenames retain the earlier ``anchor'' spelling for reproducibility; the physics interpretation is reference-normalization sensitivity rather than Higgs-sector anchoring.

\subsection{Dephasing-Based First-Principles Candidate for \texorpdfstring{$t_{\rm coh}$}{tcoh}}
\label{app:tcoh_candidate}
To close the finite-time module with the same action-derived spectral chain, we test the dephasing candidate
\begin{equation}
\Delta\omega_{12}(D)\equiv \omega_2(D)-\omega_1(D),\qquad
t_{\rm coh}^{\rm(deph)}(D)\equiv \frac{\pi}{\Delta\omega_{12}(D)},
\label{eq:tcoh_dephasing}
\end{equation}
where $(\omega_1,\omega_2)$ are extracted from the bound-state convention $E_n=\omega_n^2-m_0^2<0$ in the same 1D on-axis operator chain as Appendix~\ref{app:Veff}. Detailed coarse/mid/fine values and implementation provenance are retained in the artifact registry. On the benchmark set $D=\{6,12,18\}$, the maximum non-fine relative deviation is
\begin{equation}
\max\!\left(\frac{|t_{\rm coh}-t_{\rm coh}^{\rm(fine)}|}{|t_{\rm coh}^{\rm(fine)}|}\right)=8.86\times10^{-3},
\end{equation}
which is numerically stable at the sub-$10^{-2}$ level for this benchmark set.
The profile-impact scan compares constant $t_{\rm coh}=1$, raw Eq.~\eqref{eq:tcoh_dephasing}, and the capped variant $\min(t_{\rm coh}^{\rm(deph)},10^4)$. All three leave the headline map summaries unchanged in this diagnostic chain: $f(\mathcal{R}_3>0.90)=0.983$, $f(\mathcal{R}_3>0.95)=0.217$, $f(\chi^2_{\mu\mu}<4)=0.0833$, and best $\chi^2=9.62\times10^{-2}$. Therefore, in this manuscript, Eq.~\eqref{eq:tcoh_dephasing} is reported as a first-principles candidate benchmark only and is not propagated into the baseline reported figures.

\subsection{Splitting-Prefactor First-Principles Candidate for \texorpdfstring{$\eta$}{eta}}
\label{app:eta_candidate}
To test a geometry-closed replacement direction for the overlap amplitude in Eq.~\eqref{eq:rN}, we use the same action-derived quantities $(\Delta E_{12},S_1)$ and define the splitting prefactor
\begin{equation}
\Delta E_{12}(D)\approx A_{12}(D)\,\ee^{-S_1(D)},\qquad
A_{12}(D)\equiv \Delta E_{12}(D)\,\ee^{S_1(D)}.
\end{equation}
With a reference point $D_{\rm ref}=12$, we define two dimensionless candidates:
\begin{equation}
\eta_{\rm amp}(D)\equiv \frac{A_{12}(D)}{A_{12}(D_{\rm ref})},\qquad
\eta_{\rm prob}(D)\equiv \eta_{\rm amp}(D)^2.
\label{eq:eta_pref_candidates}
\end{equation}
Detailed coarse/mid/fine values and implementation provenance are retained in the artifact registry. On the same $D=\{6,12,18\}$ benchmark set, we obtain
\begin{equation}
\max\!\left(\frac{|\eta_{\rm amp}-\eta_{\rm amp}^{\rm(fine)}|}{|\eta_{\rm amp}^{\rm(fine)}|}\right)=1.08\times10^{-2},\qquad
\max\!\left(\frac{|\eta_{\rm prob}-\eta_{\rm prob}^{\rm(fine)}|}{|\eta_{\rm prob}^{\rm(fine)}|}\right)=2.18\times10^{-2},
\end{equation}
and the fine full-scan profile over $D=4,\dots,20$ gives $\eta_{\rm amp}\in[0.9996,1.1695]$ and $\eta_{\rm prob}\in[0.9992,1.3677]$. Profile-scaled and fully closed implementations leave the headline map-level summaries unchanged in this diagnostic chain; the residual movement is only in sub-table-precision best-$\chi^2$ shifts. Therefore, Eq.~\eqref{eq:eta_pref_candidates} is reported as a first-principles candidate benchmark and is not propagated into baseline reported figures.

\subsection{Action-Derived First-Principles Candidate for Barrier-Leakage Channel Scaling}
\label{app:superrad_candidate}
To test a geometry-closed replacement direction for the channel normalization in Eq.~\eqref{eq:Gamma_channel}, we keep the same 1D action-derived chain and define channel-resolved barrier actions
\begin{equation}
U_\ell(z;D)\equiv U(z;D)+\frac{\ell(\ell+1)}{z^2+\varepsilon^2},\qquad
S_{N,\ell}(D)\equiv\int_{z_-}^{z_+}\dd z\,\sqrt{U_\ell(z;D)-E_N(D)},
\end{equation}
with $E_N=\omega_N^2-m_0^2$ in bound-state convention.
We then define geometry-driven channel rates
\begin{equation}
\Gamma_{N,\ell}^{\rm(geo)}(D)\equiv \omega_N(D)\,e^{-2S_{N,\ell}(D)},
\end{equation}
and infer effective channel normalizations by matching to Eq.~\eqref{eq:Gamma_channel}:
\begin{equation}
A_\ell^{\rm(fp)}(D;N)\equiv
\frac{\Gamma_{N,\ell}^{\rm(geo)}(D)}{\omega_N(D)\,(\omega_N(D)M_*)^{4\ell+4}}.
\end{equation}
Using $(D_{\rm ref},N_{\rm ref})=(12,2)$, we define profile factors
\begin{equation}
\tilde A_\ell(D)\equiv \frac{A_\ell^{\rm(fp)}(D;N_{\rm ref})}{A_\ell^{\rm(fp)}(D_{\rm ref};N_{\rm ref})}.
\label{eq:superrad_A_profile}
\end{equation}
Detailed coarse/mid/fine values and implementation provenance are retained in the artifact registry. On the $D=\{6,12,18\}$ benchmark set, the maximum non-fine relative deviations are
\begin{equation}
\max\!\left(\frac{|\tilde A_1-\tilde A_1^{\rm(fine)}|}{|\tilde A_1^{\rm(fine)}|}\right)=3.22\times10^{-2},\quad
\max\!\left(\frac{|\tilde A_2-\tilde A_2^{\rm(fine)}|}{|\tilde A_2^{\rm(fine)}|}\right)=3.78\times10^{-2}.
\end{equation}
The fine full-scan profile over $D=4,\dots,20$ yields $\tilde A_1\in[8.54\times10^{-6},\,4.10\times10^5]$ and $\tilde A_2\in[3.95\times10^{-6},\,4.92\times10^6]$, indicating a very stiff $D$-dependence in this preliminary closure.

As a strict-upgrade check, we also connected a minimal complex-absorbing-potential solver to the same 1D action-derived operator chain and scanned benchmark windows in the absorber strength $\eta$ and absorber onset $z_{\rm cap}$. This pilot confirms that the non-Hermitian extraction pipeline is operational, but the benchmark mode remains in an absorber-linear or mixed regime rather than a plateau regime. Accordingly, the CAP outputs are retained as infrastructure diagnostics only; the reported baseline continues to use $\Gamma_{N,\ell}^{\rm(geo)}$ as the leading static surrogate while reserving complex-pole width extraction for a later dedicated release.

At current scan resolution, Table~\ref{tab:late_fp_profile_summary} shows that the $t_{\rm coh}$ and $\eta$ candidates are stable diagnostics whose map-level effects are below headline precision, while replacing the legacy constant-$A_\ell$ reference by the action-derived profile induces non-negligible shifts in $\mathcal{R}_3$ coverage. In the updated global scans of this manuscript, Eq.~\eqref{eq:superrad_A_profile} is used in baseline mode, and $(A_1,A_2)=(1,1)$ is retained only as a comparator.

\section{Ansatz Ablation Study}
\label{app:ablation}

To demonstrate that the three-generation structure is robust and not an artifact of a particular ansatz choice, we compare the baseline EFT-operator-normalized $B_N(D)$ profile (this work) against the alternative ``inverse Yukawa'' ansatz explored in earlier versions.

\subsection{Inverse Yukawa Ansatz (Ablation A)}
The inverse Yukawa ansatz sets $B_N^{\rm (inv)} \propto 1/\sum_{f \in \text{Gen } N} y_f$, yielding:
\begin{equation}
B_1^{\rm (inv)} \approx 10^5, \quad B_2^{\rm (inv)} \approx 125, \quad B_3^{\rm (inv)} = 1.
\end{equation}
This massive hierarchy ($B_1 \gg B_2 \gg B_3$) directly encodes the answer by over-enhancing layer 1. While it can produce $\mathcal{R}_3 > 95\%$, the mechanism is less transparent: the visibility factor, rather than the entropic/kinetic competition, drives the result.

\subsection{Comparison}
\begin{itemize}
    \item \textbf{Baseline (EFT-operator-normalized profile)}: $B_N(D)$ remains smooth and bounded across the scanned range, with shape inherited from tracked microcanonical overlap extraction and operator normalization. The three-generation structure emerges from the $g_N$--$\Gamma_N$ competition modulated by these profile differences.
    \item \textbf{Ablation A (Inverse Yukawa)}: $B_1 \gg B_2 \gg B_3$. Layer 1 is artificially boosted; the selection mechanism is less falsifiable.
\end{itemize}

We recommend the baseline as the primary closure due to its greater transparency and falsifiability.
